**Update 17:40 SAST — ORACLE FIELD: containment on the supervision itself is perfect, fruit included (Paul: "are we actually embedding the right thing where fruits are").** `containment_eval.py --oracle` (aru_sil_core): instead of rendering, take the frame's per-pixel TRAINING TARGETS from the pipeline's own `mask_dataloader.get_high_mask(i)` (the dataloader's lookup: each painted id → its manifest word → the model's CLIP text encoder → HyperEmbedder with node type → tangent latent; ||target|| 4.83 mean over the 30.4 % labelled pixels of kf_000025) and score them with the unmodified containment code (exp-map, 4-step ancestor walk to 11.1, decode, cosine; best-IoU sweep). Chunk 1_0 fruit-densify side-car config, frame kf_000025: **TREE 2 / 4 / 5 / 6 IoU 1.000, FRUIT of 5 "wildly" 1.000 (thr 0.50), FRUIT of 6 "gregg" 1.000 (thr 0.57), ROW 6 / 7 1.000.** So the supervision marks the right pixels, the embedder places fruit where containment separates it from its own tree, and the scorer (walk included) recovers it exactly when the field is right. The entire fruit failure on the side-car (0.027) is in the RENDERED field — the gaussian carriers — i.e. the densify gate that never fired. Two retractions: (1) my earlier line that targets are CLIP *image* features of the masks — wrong, `HighDataloader.create()` encodes the ids' WORDS as text (the CLIP-512 table is the same text embedding kept for the reconstruction loss); (2) my first hand-built oracle (`oracle_field.py`: tree 5 IoU 0.000, fruit 0.012) re-encoded the words through its own open_clip call instead of the model's encoder and is withdrawn — only the pipeline-lookup oracle counts.

**CORRECTION (23:1x SAST, from the peer's re-test): the metres gate alone fixes nothing — the operative blocker is the boost WINDOW.** `fruit_wm_test.sh` (20:31–21:11 box; gates 4 cm / 50 cm in world units through the old flags): 0 boost lines again, 6.53 M gaussians, share-assigned fruit mass 0.074 (was 0.079), FRUIT of 5 IoU 0.000. Cause: `fruit_densify_tail` (HiGH a5639f0, 08-31, a pre-end boost cutoff for full runs; default 2000) forbids boosting after `stop_split_at − tail`; the side-car densify pass resumes at step 15000 with stop_split_at 17001, so with tail 2000 the boost is permitted only at step ≤ 15001 — the resume step, before the protect tally exists. Every side-car densify since 09-27 therefore split nothing; the 08-27/28 block runs that fired predate the tail (boosts at 15500–17000). The 09-27 block side-car fruit results (018/019 ≈ 0.5) came from the share-seed on leaves already carrying fruit below the gate, not from splits. The units bug stands but is secondary (with the window open, 0.04 model units = 1.13 m would still block the chunk): both fixes are needed. `automation/densify_block.sh` now defaults FD_TAIL to 0 (this commit); HiGH's `fruit_densify_in_metres` stays. Peer's v2 test (`fruit_wm_test_v2.sh`: FD_TAIL=0 + metres gate, tag `fruit_densify_wm_t0`, ~45 min) runs after the demo-chunk videos (ETA ~23:00 box). My 16:30 and 18:50 entries above should be read with this correction: "fixed" was incomplete.

## 2026-10-01 — fruit on the chunk side-car: the densify gate never fires on H3DGS leaves (units bug), measured against the block fixes
Paul (01:3x): "We fixed fruit multiple times before, investigate that." The fixes on record: 08-26 argmax hands fruit pixels to the
tree → fruit-targeted densify (`densify_block.sh`) + `--fruit-share-assign 0.1`; 08-30 embedder era pairing; 08-31 ray-inheritance
leak + min-range filter (b040 fruit 0.962); 09-27 side-car fruit "geometric, not seeding" → `sidecar_fruit_densify.sh` (block
side-cars 018/019 → 0.49/0.52, 021 → 0.002). Measured today on the censuses and checkpoints (CPU, nothing re-run):
| | chunk_1_0_expo (IoU 0.027) | block_018 side-car (IoU 0.494) |
|---|---|---|
| gaussians after the densify pass | 6.52 M (seed 11.72 M: the pass CULLED 44 %) | 2.64 M |
| fruit 10001 / 10037: gaussians with fruit mass | 86,892 | 1,182 |
| share > 0.1 (the seed rule) | 3,532 | 191 |
| **fraction of the fruit's census mass held by share-assigned gaussians** | **0.079** | **0.545** |
| carriers ≥ 4 cm hold … of the fruit's mass (mass-weighted carrier scale) | 86 % (16.5 cm) | 36 % (4.4 cm) |
The chunk shows exactly the 08-26 failure signature (fruit's blend owned by coarse tree gaussians; the 08-26 no-training ceiling
was 8–23 %). The pass that is supposed to split those carriers never did: **`[fruit-densify] step= … boosted` appears 0 times in
every side-car densify log (018, 019, 021, chunk 1_0)** but fires on every HiGH block run (04 b027 73→330, b040 146→320, …).
Cause (high_model.py:204-206): `fruit_densify_scale` (documented "m", default 0.04) is compared against `exp(scales)` in MODEL
units, so the world threshold is 0.04 / dataparser_scale — 0.42 m on the HiGH blocks (scale 0.0945; their coarse splat gaussians
cross it), 0.40 m on block_018's side-car, **1.13 m on chunk_1_0_expo (scale 0.0355)**; H3DGS leaves never reach it, so nothing
is boosted and the only change is nerfstudio's cull (−44 % on the chunk). Blocks 018/019 succeeded anyway because the
default-recipe hierarchy already carried their fruit on < 4 cm leaves; the expo hierarchy carries tree 5's fruit on 10–20 cm
leaves. `sidecar_fruit_densify_chunk.sh` now takes FD_SCALE_M / FD_MAXSCALE_M (metres → model units through the seed run's
dataparser scale) and FD_EXP (tagged run, no collision with the existing one). **Proposed test (GPU ~1 h, not run):**
`FD_EXP=fruit_densify_wm FD_SCALE_M=0.04 FD_MAXSCALE_M=0.5 SIDECAR_PROJ=…/h3dgs_expo bash automation/h3dgs/sidecar_fruit_densify_chunk.sh 05_13D_Jackal chunk_1_0_expo 1_0`
— if the boost fires and the share-assigned fraction rises toward block_018's 0.55, the fruit verdict on kf_000025 is the answer.
Also noted: tree 5's oranges are only ever seen at ≥ 3.1 m with ≤ 3.4 k px (block-style b000 'wildly' scored 0.246), so the ceiling
for that fruit is low in every pipeline.

changes (automation/h3dgs, backward-compatible defaults): `sidecar_demo_path.py --chunk-drive --sidecar-root --proj` (drive = the
side-car dataset's cameras inside the cell, in time order, as passes ≥ 40 frames — a 30 m cell holds ~110-frame row passes, so
the drive is 4–6 passes with jump cuts: 05 456 frames / 57 s at 8 fps, 28 trees incl. tree 5, 6 row words; 01 447 frames / 56 s,
45 trees, 12 row words; `--orbit-seconds 0` skips the dolly); `sidecar_row_backdrop.py --proj --exposure mean` (mean of the

colours render dark/blue); `sidecar_demo_overlay.py --sidecar-root --proj`; new `sidecar_demo_script_chunk.py` (segments laid out
proportionally to the drive; "show me the trees at the end of row N" for 01 (row 22 = "teak", 5 trees in the drive); the fruit

### CORRECTION — the fruit boost never fired on side-cars because of the boost WINDOW, not (only) the gate units
The world-unit re-test (`fruit_wm_test.sh`, 20:31–21:11, gate 0.04 m → 0.00142 model units, max 0.5 m) changed nothing: 0
`[fruit-densify] … boosted` lines, 6.53 M gaussians (was 6.52 M), share-assigned mass for tree 5's fruit 0.074 (was 0.079),
verdict FRUIT of 5 "wildly" IoU **0.000** (TREE 5 0.617, TREE 2 0.797). So the units finding above is NOT the operative
blocker. Measured: `fruit_densify_tail` entered HiGH in a5639f0 (2026-08-31, "pre-end boost cutoff") — AFTER the block runs
whose boosts fired (04 b027 / 05 b018 on 08-27/28: stop_split_at 17001, no tail option in their printed config, boosts at
15500–17000). Every side-car densify since 09-27 (018, 019, 021, chunk 1_0 ×2) carries tail 2000 with stop_split_at 17001 on
a pass resumed at step 15000, so the boost condition `step < stop_split_at − tail = 15001` holds on the resume step only,
when the protect tally is still zero → the pass can never boost. The gate units are real but secondary (with the window open,
0.04 model units = 1.13 m would still block the chunk). The 09-27 block side-car successes (018/019 ≈ 0.5) therefore came
from the share-seed on leaves that already carried fruit at < 4 cm, not from any split. Corrected test queued behind the demo
videos: `~/logs/fruit_wm_test_v2.sh` = FD_TAIL=0 + FD_SCALE_M 0.04 / 0.5 (tagged run fruit_densify_wm_t0, ~45 min), then
the diet numbers and the verdict. The demo runner's rule (tree-5 IoU ≥ 0.25) left the fruit segment OUT of the 05 video;
if v2 is honest, the 05 composite is re-run with `--reuse-maps` + the wm_t0 fruit maps (~15 min).
- **05 chunk_1_0_expo cut DELIVERED (21:14–21:20, 5 min GPU): `~/logs/demo_chunks/05_1_0/demo.mp4`, 59 s at 8 fps, 472 frames
  (456 + holds), 9 MB; backdrop 456 frames in 53 s (3.45 GiB resident, mean exposure diag 1.09/1.09/1.05); identity maps 456
  frames in 179 s (38 trees, 6 row words; camera-frame check |dR| 6e-8); script: plain → row → "which tree is this?" = Tree 2 of
  row 5 (tree 36) → every tree → by row → orchard; no fruit (re-test IoU 0.000 < 0.25).** Identity coverage per pass (tree px /
  row px of the frame): kf 0–42 40 % / 25 %, 409–515 29 / 19, 883–928 25 / 25, 2403–2444 41 / 36, 2804–2913 28 / 17, 3278–3385
  20 / 11; 8–14 trees firing per frame. **Finding on the cell:** 1_0's in-cell cameras are the survey's eastern turnaround —
  two headland passes along y ≈ +11 (looking at row ends), two short edge-lane passes at x ≈ −18.5 and the x ≈ +0.9 track east of

  outlines), but the viewpoint is the weaker one. **01 chunk_3_1 backdrop OOM'd after 43 of 447 frames alone on the card**
  (`std::bad_alloc: cudaErrorMemoryAllocation` from the hierarchy's C++ expand — the torch caching allocator's hold, the same
  failure the render service had): `sidecar_row_backdrop.py` now empties the CUDA cache every 25 frames; retry queued
  (`demo_chunks_01_retry.sh`: expandable segments, tau 0 then tau 3 fallback; also re-composites 05 WITH fruit if the corrected
  fruit test's tree-5 IoU ≥ 0.25) behind the corrected fruit test (started 21:23). A 05 chunk_0_0_expo cut (`demo_chunks_00.sh`,

- **Corrected fruit test (tail 0 + metres gate 4 cm / 50 cm; `fruit_wm_test_v2.sh`, 21:23–21:56): the boost FIRES — 952 protected
  coarse gaussians boosted at step 15500, 8,705 at 16000, 184,232 at 16500 — but the pass is unbounded on this geometry and died
  of OOM at ~step 16850 (37.7 GB; 9.39 M gaussians).** The refine log shows why: the first refine (15700) pruned 4.45 M of the
  11.7 M leaves (opacity < 0.1 → 7.27 M), then "duplicated" grew ×2 every 100 steps once the big boost landed (188 k → 360 k →
  705 k → 1.40 M at 16800) while "split" stayed at 2–6 k. Boosted gaussians are being CLONED, not split: gsplat splits only above
  its size threshold (`densify_size_thresh` 0.01 model units = 0.28 m world at this chunk's dataparser scale 0.0355; 0.11 m on
  the HiGH blocks), so a 4–28 cm carrier with a boosted gradient is duplicated at the same size, the clone inherits protection
  and is boosted again → exponential. So: window fixed, units fixed, and now the third term — the split/duplicate size rule is
  also in model units and sits far above the fruit gate on H3DGS chunks. No census/verdict (train failed); the 05 reel stays
  without fruit; the 01 retry took the card at 21:57 (backdrop 447 frames in 60 s with the cache fix).

contrastive loss was still falling at the end. v2_e400 (400 epochs, 1 min, loss 4.63 vs 4.49): per-head ORACLE with the
global-id registry, three frames, v1c → v2: image_40 TREE 16 0.451 → **0.932**, 18 0.426 → **1.000**, 19 0.090 → **1.000**,

29 0.13 → 0.13; image_33 unchanged (12 0.168, 14 0.576, 15 0.256); rows 1.000 / 0.712 / 0.288 both. More training lifts some
heads to the ceiling and leaves others — v3 at 1,600 epochs running next. Correction: the earlier oracle "TREE 16/18/19 =
0.000 (thr nan)" under v1c carried a stale `clip_cache_*` from the renumbered-registry run (the cache holds target features);
the clean back-to-back v1c numbers are 0.45 / 0.43 / 0.09 — delete the cache between registry changes. Script:

verdict IoU ≥ the floor; count/row questions everywhere).

  v3 5 (14, 15, 18, 25, 26). So the embedder's per-head separation varies run to run (init/epochs), it is not monotonic in
  training — the ceiling for "which cabbage is this?" is set by which heads a given run separates. Decision: v2_e400 for the
  demo (most heads ≥ 0.5); final stage launched 09:1x (reseed with v2 → verdicts on image_33/40/52 → reel with the honesty rule).

So the experiment is the refine itself: `automation/h3dgs/stage2_train_chunk.sh` = ns-train high from the census seed (the seed ckpt carries no optimizer/scheduler state → fresh Adam; high_features lr 1e-3 → 1e-4 over 30 k), geometry frozen (means/scales/quats/opacities/SH locked, only high_features train, no refines), high-loss-weight 1.0, antialiased, sky-loss 1.0, the seeds' own 1-in-9 eval split; then the per-gaussian feature displacement vs the seed and containment verdicts on the chain's frames. **Pilot 18:28 box: cabbage lane, v2 seed, 2,000 iters** (saves at 16000/17000), verdicts on image_40 (heads 16/18/19: seed 0.42/0.03/0.03 vs oracle 0.93/1.00/1.00), image_52 (25: 0.15 vs 0.95; 28: 0.37 vs 0.69), image_64 (30: 0.69 vs 0.96 — must not fall). **Night queue chained** (`stage2_night_queue.sh`): runs only if the pilot moved ≥ 60,000 of the 127,827 seeded rows by > 0.01 (else "NIGHT ABORTED", nothing launched); budget ≈ 2 h per cell from the pilot's it/s, clamped 3 k–10 k iters, saves every 2,500; order 05 chunk_0_0_expo (trees_only), 05 chunk_1_0_expo from the v3 fruit seed (trees_fruit_v3, fruit-protect, fruit weight 2), 01 chunk_3_1, cabbage lane at the full budget; verdict frames from the chain's own picker. Seed baselines to beat: 3_1 kf_003426 TREE 164 0.972 / 163 0.710 / 165 0.941 / 172 0.718, ROW 22 0.940 / 23 0.960; 1_0 kf_000025 TREE 5 0.735 / 2 0.863 / 4 0.420 / 6 0.258, FRUIT of 5 0.301, ROW 7 0.882 / 6 0.649. Serving roots untouched until Paul reads the verdicts.
**Pilot 1 result (19:01 box): the optimizer is live but slow — night queue held.** 2,000 iters in 32 min (1.03 it/s on 7.38 M gaussians, geometry frozen). Feature displacement vs the seed: seeded rows median 0.0305 against a seed norm of 8.0 (0.4 %); 209,061 unseeded gaussians (2.9 %) gained identity at median norm 0.99; 473,043 rows moved > 0.01 (the go/no-go floor of 60,000 passed). Verdict image_40 at step 2000: TREE 16 0.447 (seed 0.419), 18 0.027 (0.027), 19 0.029 (0.027), ROW 1 0.716 (0.746) — unchanged. Reading: each gaussian is visible in a few of the 64 training views, so at lr 1e-3 it gets ~100 effective Adam steps in 2 k iterations; the field is gradient-rate-limited, not stuck. Spending the night on 7,500 iterations of the same drift would not be a measurement → the chained night queue was killed before its marker, and **pilot 2 runs the one lever that changes the rate: high_features lr 1e-2 (→ 1e-3), same 2 k iters, same frames** (`stage2_train_chunk_v2.sh`, `--optimizers.high-features.optimizer.lr`). The night relaunches with whichever LR moves the verdicts.
**Pilot 2 (19:09–19:47 box, high_features lr 1e-2 → 1e-3, otherwise identical): the field moves, the verdicts get worse.** Seeded rows median displacement 0.632 (8 % of the seed norm; 20× pilot 1), 319,445 unseeded gaussians (4.4 %) at median norm 2.9 (pilot 1: 209,061 at 0.99), rows moved > 0.01 480,013. High loss active on 83 % of steps (54 of 64 train frames labelled), mean non-zero loss 5.07 → 4.50 over the run (pilot 1: 3.54 → 2.90). Verdicts at step 2000 (seed → lr 1e-3 → lr 1e-2): image_40 TREE 16 0.419 → 0.447 → 0.406, 18 0.027 → 0.027 → 0.003, 19 0.027 → 0.029 → 0.025, ROW 1 0.746 → 0.716 → 0.663; image_52 TREE 25 0.129 → 0.139 → 0.121, 28 0.368 → 0.366 → 0.323, ROW 0 0.649 → 0.641 → 0.628; **image_64 TREE 30 0.692 → 0.692 → 0.483**, ROW 0 0.672 → 0.650 → 0.645. Monotone in the amount of movement: the loss (rendered-feature geodesic at labelled pixels, bg weight 0) is not what containment measures — the census seed's clean one-word-per-gaussian assignment is already the containment optimum and training smears it (unseeded gaussians that blend into labelled pixels are pulled toward the targets, at norm ~3 of the word scale ~8 they decode to nothing or the row). The chained night queue (7,500 iters × 4 cells) was NOT relaunched on this recipe. Running instead: the lane continued from pilot 2's step-17000 ckpt by 5,500 iters to 7,500 total (`lane_v2_lr1e2_7k5`, 19:48, ~90 min, mid score at +2,750) — the long-run test of "incomplete movement" (recovery) vs "loss–metric mismatch" (further decline) — then a bg-weight pilot (`high_loss_bg_weight` re-admits unlabelled pixels, pulling blended-in gaussians to the void instead of leaving them free). Citrus cells only if a configuration beats the seed on the lane.
**Lane long-run (`lane_v2_lr1e2_7k5`, 19:48–21:19 box): pilot 2 continued to 7,500 iterations at lr 1e-2 — containment keeps falling as the loss keeps falling.** 5,500 iters in 81 min (1.12 it/s); high loss at the sampled steps 4.88 → 3.84 → 3.82 → 2.31; the same 480,013 gaussians keep moving (median a further 0.89), no new gaussian gains identity (unseeded rows stay at zero: only gaussians rendered inside labelled pixels ever receive gradient). Verdicts (seed → 2k → 4.25k → 7.5k): image_64 TREE 30 0.692 → 0.483 → 0.450 → 0.435, ROW 0 0.672 → 0.645 → 0.621 → 0.585; image_40 TREE 16 0.419 → 0.406 → · → 0.389, 18 0.027 → 0.003 → · → 0.000, 19 0.027 → 0.025 → · → 0.013, ROW 1 0.746 → 0.663 → · → 0.758; image_52 TREE 25 0.129 → 0.121 → · → 0.094, 28 0.368 → 0.323 → · → 0.303, ROW 0 0.649 → 0.628 → · → 0.540. The "incomplete movement" reading is dead: more training moves the field further from the containment optimum on every head and both rows (row 1 on image_40 the lone exception). Conclusion for the night: a live stage-2 refine of the census seed on frozen H3DGS geometry, with the current loss, is a regression, not a fix — the seed-only side-car is the better field. Last candidate running: bg weight 0.1 (unlabelled pixels pull blended-in gaussians to the void), 2 k iters at lr 1e-2 (`lane_v2_lr1e2_bg01_2k`); the citrus cells run only if it beats the seed (rule in `stage2_night_queue_v3.sh`).
**Night aborted by the rule (21:52 box) + disk incident.** The bg-weight pilot (`lane_v2_lr1e2_bg01_2k`, lr 1e-2, bg 0.1, 2 k iters) trained to the end but died at the final save on **"No space left on device"**: the root filesystem (2.0 TB, one volume for /, /home and the data) was at 99 % with ~1 GB free — my four runs tonight had written 4.58 GB per checkpoint, nine checkpoints ≈ 41 GB. `stage2_night_queue_v3.sh` found no verdicts for the bg run and no winner for the 7.5 k run (head 30 0.435 < 0.68) → "NIGHT ABORTED … citrus cells not run; card free" — the rule worked as designed. Freed 23 GB by deleting the five INTERMEDIATE checkpoints of my own runs (pilot 1 step-16000, pilot 2 step-16000, 7k5 step-19250/22000, bg step-16000 — each redundant with its run's final) plus the regenerable clip caches; 24 GB free after. The bg run's final checkpoint was truncated (443 MB) → run dir removed and the pilot relaunched 21:5x with a single save (SAVE=2000). Remaining tonight: four final checkpoints of negative results (pilot 1, pilot 2, 7k5 = 13.7 GB) — Paul's call to delete. The disk at 99 % is a hazard for the demo prep (720p renders, serving caches) independent of this experiment.
**bg-weight pilot result (22:31 box, run `stage2_train_lane_v2_lr1e2_bg01_2k`, rerun after the disk kill): the last candidate also loses to the seed.** lr 1e-2, `high_loss_bg_weight 0.1`, 2 k iters, 32 min at 1.04 it/s, high loss 0.98 → 0.23 (the bg term pulls the loss to a quarter of the no-bg run's 2.3 — it is optimising the void target, not the heads). Feature move: seeded rows median displacement 0.80 (10 % of the seed norm 8.0), 941 k unseeded rows (13 %) now carry norm > 0.5, 3.29 M rows moved > 0.01. Verdicts at step 2000: image_64 TREE 30 0.567 (seed 0.692, no-bg 2 k 0.483 — bg helps relative to no-bg but is still 0.125 under the seed), ROW 0 0.582; image_40 TREE 18 0.002 / 19 0.017 (seed 0.027), ROW 1 0.709; image_52 TREE 25 0.083 (seed 0.129). Rule (30 >= 0.68 AND a row-side head at seed level) fails → `stage2_night_queue_v4b.sh` logged "NIGHT ABORTED (bg 0.1)" at 22:31:49; the citrus cells were not run; the card was free overnight. Conclusion for the night: four settings measured (lr 1e-3; lr 1e-2 at 2 k / 7.5 k; lr 1e-2 + bg 0.1), none beats the census seed on any head; the live stage-2 refine on frozen H3DGS geometry is closed as a negative result (memory: stage2-live-refine-regression). Disk: the rerun still wrote TWO checkpoints (nerfstudio saves at step % steps_per_save == 0 → 16000, plus the final 17000), 9.2 GB; 16 GB free on the root volume this morning.

## 2026-10-03 — H3DGS × HiGH: native census seed on the hierarchy (no side-car)

**Ask (Paul, 14:2x box): "can we continue with h3dgs high embedding integration".** Read with his 10-02 instruction ("remove the sidecar option and have two stage h3dgs census training like we did for blocks"): last night's experiment refined the SIDE-CAR model, which keeps the side-car; the integration proper puts the identity INTO the H3DGS hierarchy. **Plan (stated before building):**
- *Goal:* stage 1 = the trained H3DGS RGB hierarchy (frozen); stage 2 = the blocks' census seed computed on the hierarchy's own nodes (census → argmax assignment with the side-car flags tree-floor 1.0 / bg-competes / bg-ratio 2) — seed only, no gradient refine (10-02: every refine setting lowered containment; the block fleet was seed-only too). Features live per hierarchy node (`features.bin`, the format of the 09-25 `train_features.py`) and render through the same level-of-detail cut as RGB (expand_to_size + parent interpolation, serving tau 3 at 1280 px).
- *Decisive test:* containment verdicts, same scorer / embedder / supervision / frames, native vs the side-car seed (`stage2_censusinit_glref_bg_f1.0_r2`) on 05 chunk 1_0 (`h3dgs_expo`, 659 keyframes): the side-car's verdict frames + more train frames across the cell + held-out frames (the side-car's 1-in-10 eval split, out of both censuses). The native census uses exactly the side-car's train frames. Pass = native ≥ side-car on trees and rows; then serving moves to the hierarchy (the broker's third process goes away). If it fails, the per-head table says where.
- *Dependencies:* (1) census over hierarchy nodes in the h3dgs env (gsplat, CUDA_HOME=code/_cuda12): one multi-channel render per view, the gradient w.r.t. the rendered per-gaussian colours = blend weight into each label's pixels, scattered to node and parent by the interpolation weights; (2) `build_census_init.py --out-features` (nerf_new env) to write node features instead of a ckpt; (3) a native feature renderer; (4) `containment_eval.py --features-npz` so both models are scored by the identical code from dumped feature maps (also skips the ~16 min eval_setup per frame); (5) a side-car feature dump at the same frames.
**Built (14:0x–14:2x box; `automation/h3dgs/native/`, aru_sil_core scripts patched additively):** `hier_common.py` (load a trained hierarchy like train_post, cameras by image name, per-view expansion + render_post parent interpolation, serving cut threshold 2(τ+0.5)·tan(FoVx/2)/(0.5·W)); `hier_census.py` (one C-channel gsplat render per view with zero leaf colours; grad of Σ label-channel over the label's pixels = each rendered gaussian's blend weight; scattered to node ×w and parent ×(1−w); unlabelled row 65535 = CENSUS_WITH_BG; 0.5 render scale like the nerfstudio census); `hier_feature_render.py`; `sidecar_feature_dump.py` (the side-car's own render_frame at the same frames); `sidecar_split_names.py`, `pick_frames.py`, `score_maps.sh`, `native_table.py`; `build_census_init.py --out-features` (node features instead of a ckpt) and `containment_eval.py --features-npz` (stock open_clip fp16 exactly as high/encoders/clip_encoder.py builds it; default path unchanged).
**05 chunk 1_0 (h3dgs_expo, 17.7 M hierarchy nodes, 05_13D_v1g, trees_only; the side-car's 593 train / 66 held-out frames).** Census at τ 3 / 1280 px over the 593 train views: 1.3 min of compute (~6.0 M gaussians per cut), W 39 labels × 17.7 M; assignment with the side-car flags: 1,672,840 nodes (9.4 %) assigned, 224,596 withheld by bg-competes. Native render ~1 s per frame; identity (‖f‖ > 0.5) on 39–53 % of px, the same as the side-car (39–55 %). **Scorer check: the npz path reproduced all 6 canonical side-car verdicts on kf_002411 exactly (IoU and thr).** Native vs side-car, 13 frames (9 in both censuses, 4 held out of both), 61 heads:
| kind | split | heads | native | side-car | native wins / ties / losses (±0.02) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TREE | train | 25 | 0.724 | 0.706 | 12 / 9 / 4 |
| TREE | held out | 9 | 0.826 | 0.798 | 5 / 3 / 1 |
| ROW | train | 19 | 0.830 | 0.821 | 6 / 11 / 2 |
| ROW | held out | 8 | 0.857 | 0.850 | 2 / 5 / 1 |
**The native seed passes the decisive test on this cell** (≥ the side-car on every kind × split; the clearest gains on mixed-tree heads: kf_002411 TREE 38 0.164 vs 0.065, TREE 60 0.850 vs 0.792; kf_000025 TREE 4/6 +0.09/+0.11). Its one weak frame is kf_000412 (TREE 0 0.159 vs 0.328, ROW 7 0.203 vs 0.320 — edge-of-frame heads). Table: `experimental/h3dgs_native/chunk_1_0_expo/table.md`.
**Level of detail (same τ-3 seed, rendered at other cuts):** τ 0 (finest, 11.5 M gaussians) = τ 3 (trees 0.715 / 0.812, rows 0.827 / 0.852 train / held out); τ 9 rows hold (0.821 / 0.857) but trees drop (0.645 / 0.734); τ 15 trees 0.506 / 0.659, rows 0.783 / 0.845 (two frames' τ-15 scores missing). Serving runs τ 3, the census cut; a viewer narrower than 1280 px coarsens the cut, so a multi-τ census is the refinement if that matters.
**Incident (mine, fixed):** v1 of the chain exported CUDA_HOME=code/_cuda12 for the h3dgs env and the nerf_new side-car dump inherited it — nerf_new's gsplat JIT cache (`~/.cache/torch_extensions/py310_cu118/gsplat_cuda`) was rebuilt against CUDA 12.4 at 14:39 and failed to load (libcudart.so.12). Nothing live used it. The clean rerun rebuilt it at 14:49 with the env's own nvcc 11.8 (build.ninja verified); v2 of the chain scopes CUDA_HOME to the h3dgs commands. The first side-car eval_setup took 995 s building the CLIP target cache; later ones take ~30 s.
**05 chunk 0_0 (h3dgs_expo, 17.6 M nodes; 15:03 box): native passes again.** Census 189 s (with load); 1,737,450 nodes (9.9 %) assigned. 15 frames, 56 heads:

The two losses are both on held-out kf_001863 (TREE 90 0.714 vs 0.769, TREE 92 0.328 vs 0.359). 01 chunk 3_1, the cabbage lane and the 1_0 fruit test (trees_fruit_v3 + share-assign 0.1 vs the v3 densified fruit side-car) are running.
**Native identity SERVED (15:00–15:02 box; aru_sil_core 22c41760, opt-in).** `interfaces/splat_viewer/native_identity.py` + `hier_render_service.py` with HIER_FEATURES / HIER_EMBEDDER / HIER_TEXT_BANK: the query is answered from the hierarchy's own per-node features through the colour's own cut — no side-car process. The mechanism is the side-car's, ported verbatim: half-res feature pass, block_renderer containment heat (exclusive for rows), render_service Otsu + absence guard or the client cut, solid 0.42 + 0.25 ramp. Needs einops in the h3dgs env (installed, 0.8.2 wheel, no deps; HiGH's lorentz.py uses one rearrange) and CUDA_HOME=_cuda12 for gsplat; the CLIP text side is a per-cell text bank (`automation/h3dgs/native/build_text_bank.py`, nerf_new env, fp16 CLIP exactly as clip_encoder.py; 05 1_0: 64 words). Embedder decode parity h3dgs env vs nerf_new: max |Δ| 1.9e-6. Offline check through the serving code on kf_002411 (`native_identity_check.py`, side-car map pushed through the identical code): trees native 0.907 / 0.796 / 0.160 / 0.145 vs side-car 0.876 / 0.732 / 0.045 / 0.162; rows need the client's cut in both (Otsu without a cut: 0.47 / 0.04 vs 0.47 / 0.08 — the absence guard assumes a minority of positives, as render_service's docstring says); with the verdict cuts 0.88 / 0.90: native 0.886 / 0.894 vs side-car 0.879 / 0.871. Live on a spare port (8036, `native_ws_check.py`, recorded kf_002411 pose in the wire convention): native acks, unknown ids refused with a reason, 160–250 ms per frame with a query (colour 37–43 ms; first query 1.5 s warm-up). The broker (launch_api / sites.json) is NOT switched — the stages still start the side-car; switching = add the three env vars + CUDA_HOME to the render entry and drop `identity`.
**Bug found on the live stack (fixed in the same commit):** `hier_render_service.py` used `Path` for HIER_EXPOSURE=mean without importing it (since e5bc6055, 10-01) → NameError caught → "[hier] exposure requested but not applied" → sites.json asks for mean exposure on two H3DGS stages, so the live stage has rendered WITHOUT the trained exposure (darker, bluer). With the import: "exposure: mean of 294 trained matrices … diag 1.093 / 1.085 / 1.046" on 05 1_0. Takes effect at the next stage start; the colours on the live stage will change (brighter, warmer) — the behaviour 10-01 intended.
**What "native" is, for the record (Paul, 15:0x box: "I hope this isn't a back door, and that H3DGS is actually getting 32-d features so it is exactly like the original HiGH approach").** Measured on 05 1_0: `features.bin` = 17,712,373 nodes × 32 float32 (2.27 GB) in the hierarchy's node order (leaves AND interior nodes); 1,672,840 nodes carry a vector, which is one of 38 distinct HiGH targets (log_map0 of the embedder's encoding of the label word's CLIP embedding, norms 3.45–4.4); the rendered identity is a [360, 640, 32] alpha-blended feature map (gsplat, colours = the 32-d features) through the same cut and parent interpolation as RGB, decoded by the same embedder / Lorentz walk / CLIP cos as every HiGH block. No supervision is read at query time; held-out frames (outside both censuses) score at the training level. Same as the blocks' stage 2: the census-init seed (recipe of record since 08-07; the production fleet ran seed-only, CENSUS_SEED_ONLY=1). NOT the same as full HiGH training: the 32-d features are not further optimised with the geodesic loss after the seed — the blocks did not do that either (fw2 refine = dead optimizer), and on 10-02 every live refine setting on the side-car lowered containment. A gradient-trained variant on the hierarchy exists (`train_features.py`, 09-25, from zero; generalised at 2 k, overfit at 15 k) and has never been containment-scored; seed → that training → this same test is the open question.

**Fruit, 05 chunk 1_0** (trees_fruit_v3, the same assignment flags + `--fruit-share-assign 0.1` on the UNdensified hierarchy: 855 fruit-argmax + 11,787 share-assigned nodes) vs the v3 densified fruit side-car (`fruit_densify_wm_t0s_r2`), 6 frames: FRUIT 0.376 / 0.100 vs 0.334 / 0.072 (train / held out; 4 wins, 3 ties, 0 losses), trees 0.768 / 0.608 vs 0.738 / 0.599, rows 0.782 / 0.742 vs 0.744 / 0.716. The demo's fruit frame kf_000025: FRUIT of 5 native 0.404 vs side-car 0.301 (the 10-01 verdict, reproduced).
**Reading:** the identity seeded in the H3DGS hierarchy itself matches or beats the side-car on every cell — ahead on both 05 cells and on fruit, level on 01 3_1 and the cabbage lane — so the side-car adds nothing the hierarchy cannot carry. Where native loses by > 0.03 it is a few heads at row ends (01: trees 163 / 172 on kf_003426–27, tree 181 on kf_003619 −0.18; 05: kf_000412 edge heads, kf_001863 trees 90 / 92). The cabbage ceiling is upstream (embedder separation + seeding gap, 10-02) and shared by both. Two GPU contention failures on the way (01 census OOM beside my lingering test servers; the cabbage side-car dump OOM beside the 01 census) were rerun in isolation (`native_cell_resume.sh`, `lane_sidecar_redo.sh`); no result is from a partial run. Tables: `experimental/h3dgs_native/<cell>/table.md` per survey.
**Fruit without the side-car = no densification (Paul, 15:3x box: "does densification now work without the side car").** It does not run natively — densification exists only in the side-car's nerfstudio training; the native fruit seed above used the frozen H3DGS geometry. Measured why it is not needed on 05 1_0: the 11,787 nodes the native census gives a fruit identity (8,420 leaves, 3,367 interior) have a largest-axis 2σ extent of 0.7 / 2.0 / 5.7 cm (p10 / p50 / p90); the whole hierarchy 0.5 / 2.0 / 12.2 cm — H3DGS training already made the canopy finer than a fruit. Why the native census seeds more fruit than the side-car census on the same leaves (11,787 vs a few thousand share-assigned in the v3 diet) is not isolated; candidates are the hierarchy's own bundle-adjusted poses (the side-car renders on block LIO poses, 3–7 cm residual) and the level-of-detail cut. Held-out fruit stays weak in both (0.100 vs 0.072).
**What fruit IoU 0.4 means here (Paul, 15:4x box: "0.4 IoU is low for a few pixels no, that's either half or double the size right"; `automation/h3dgs/native/fruit_anatomy.py`).** A nested mask of half or double the true area scores exactly 0.5; 0.4 = 40 % covered or 2.5× the area. At the verdict's half resolution tree 5's oranges on kf_000025 are 21 blobs, median 43 px (~7 px across), 831 px in all — a 1-px boundary error alone moves such a blob's area by roughly half. Measured at the verdict thresholds:
| frame / model | lit px vs fruit px | IoU | fruit px within 1 px of a lit px | lit px within 1 px of fruit | oranges lit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| kf_000025 native | 1,072 / 831 | 0.404 | 0.85 (0.92 at 2 px) | 0.59 (0.63) | 18 / 21 |
| kf_000025 side-car | 1,317 / 831 | 0.301 | 0.73 (0.79) | 0.49 (0.57) | 16 / 21 |
| kf_003285 native | 831 / 757 | 0.387 | 0.80 (0.90) | 0.62 (0.67) | 16 / 17 |
| kf_003285 side-car | 639 / 757 | 0.289 | 0.63 (0.78) | 0.59 (0.66) | 13 / 17 |
Where the lit pixels > 1 px from fruit fall (kf_000025 native 439 px): tree 5's own canopy 256, unlabelled 182, other fruit 1, other trees 0 (side-car: 617 on the canopy). The identity is right; the loss is fruit halo onto the same tree's leaves — the documented trade-off of `--fruit-share-assign` (lower share = more recall, more halo; 0.1 here). Raw IoU undersells few-pixel objects; orange hits + a 1-px band describe them better. Next lever for precision: sweep the share threshold (assignment + render + score only, minutes).
**Landing check (Paul, 15:5x box: "when will this land") — the two live identity stages, emulated exactly as launch_api starts them (`native_stage_emulate.py`: cwd + absolute script + sites.json render env + the native keys, PATH without CUDA to prove the service adds it), driven with the live client's queries (no cut).** Citrus A (01 3_1) and Citrus B (05 1_0, the fruit seed + a trees_fruit_v3 text bank, 82 words) both come up in 10–16 s with identity=native, 150–200 ms per lit frame, fruit id 10001 (the client's 10000 + node id) resolving to tree 5's fruit word. **Under the live rule (no cut → per-frame Otsu), native vs today's side-car (`live_rule_compare.py`, identical heat + alpha code, 05 1_0 kf_000025 / kf_003285):** trees level (tree 5 covered 96.5 / 89.2 % native vs 92.6 / 93.1 %, < 1 % on other objects); rows weak in both (row 7 18 % vs 20 % covered — the Otsu absence guard assumes a minority of positives; pre-existing); **fruit: a no-cut query for tree 5's oranges lights tree 2's canopy in BOTH (127,759 px native, 128,997 px side-car on kf_000025) — today's live stage answers "show me the oranges" with the wrong tree on this view.** Exclusive scoring for fruit (the rows' mechanism: the fruit word must beat every other word in the bank; no fitted threshold) fixes it: native 3,436 px, 55 % on the oranges, the rest on tree 5, none on tree 2, 57 % of the oranges covered (kf_003285: 58 % on fruit, 40 % covered); for trees, exclusive scoring blanks the answer (absence guard), so trees stay plain. Added to `native_identity.py` (fruit only). The side-car cannot do this on Citrus B: its live field has no fruit identity. Prepared, NOT applied: sites.json for farms 0 and 1 — render.env += HIER_FEATURES / HIER_EMBEDDER / HIER_TEXT_BANK / CUDA_HOME, render.need_gb 8 → 10, `identity` → `identity_sidecar_off` (revert = rename back); the broker rereads sites.json per request, so it takes effect at the next stage start. Waiting on Paul's go (the dashboard session rehearses on these stages).
**Row 7 sheets for Paul (16:0x box; `automation/h3dgs/native/query_sheet.py`, sheets in `05_13D_Jackal/experimental/h3dgs_native/sheets/`).** Citrus B, the stage's colour render with mean exposure, lit by: supervision / today's side-car (no cut) / native (no cut, the live client's rule) / native with ONE fixed cut 0.8 on every frame. Row 7 covered: kf_000025 20 % / 18 % / 99 %; kf_003289 3 % / 2 % / 99 %; held-out kf_003297 1 % / 1 % / 99 % (side-car / native no cut / native cut 0.8), 87–93 % of the cut's lit pixels on row 7, row 6's tree left dark. Under the no-cut rule both fields light only a fringe along the canopy silhouette: inside the canopy the features sit at tree level and score uniformly for the row word, the blended edge pixels score highest, and the per-frame Otsu keeps that top tail. So "rows look weak on the live stage" is the cut rule, not the field; one global row cut (a constant, not fitted per frame or per plant) lights the row. Paul's call — his 09-30 rule forbids cuts tuned against the masks.
**Rows: how they were done, and why they now light a fringe (Paul, 16:1x box: "Never cut … the embedding must be one" / "How did we do rows previously. Don't implement anything before starting").** History: Aug 30–31 (block era, live stream :8004) trees lit on the per-frame Otsu split (tree 71 0.853), rows only with an explicit per-row cut sent by the client (row 2 at 0.60 lit the opposite row, 0.78 recall 0.993 / bleed 0.7 %, 0.85 in the reel; row 3 needed 0.78 — "per-row cuts are the rule"); Sep 2 exclusive row scoring added (block_renderer: a pixel scores for a row only if that row's word wins the walk against every other row word — b013 tree 72 had fired 0.964 for the adjacent row); Sep 30 no fitted cuts (Paul) → rows = exclusive scoring + Otsu, no cut. Measured on Citrus B kf_003289, native field, row 7: the embedding is consistent — every row-7 tree pixel scores 0.882 for row 7 (p10 0.882, p90 0.889), the other row's trees 0.40–0.50, coverage (alpha) 1.0 on row-7 pixels. The fringe is the exclusive step: it leaves only row-7 pixels valid, all ~0.882, and Otsu splits that near-constant set at 0.905 (811 of 37,015 px lit). Plain containment (no exclusive) + the same Otsu, no cut: threshold 0.649, 36,923 of 37,015 row-7 px lit (99.8 %), no other-row pixel lit, ~5.6 k unlabelled px. Open: whether dropping exclusivity brings back the Sep 2 boundary-tree capture on the native field — untested. My alpha-normalisation experiment (coverage is already 1.0) was reverted; nothing implemented.

**Grey frames, per-frame PSNR of the phone cut (Paul, 17:3x box: "so many grey cuts, for each frame give me the psnr graph for the video", "also we need the sky"; `automation/demo/video_psnr_timeline.py`, graph + CSV in `data/demo_video_v2/psnr/`).** Every video frame is a render at a recorded keyframe pose (reels: `demo_path.json` src = the keyframe; the reel poses equal the chunk BA poses, 0.0 cm / 0.00°; walks: the scene server's `lio` trajectory in image_idx order, frame n = record n). Scored against the keyframe photo, FULL FRAME with sky: "as shown" (the plain render, mean trained exposure — what the stage and reels use) and "colour-matched" (per-frame 3×4 affine fit to the photo = the H3DGS exposure model; isolates render quality). On one 0_0 frame the chunk camera with mean exposure gives 18.6 dB and with the image's own trained exposure 25.3 — the camera's auto-exposure swings, not geometry, cost the as-shown numbers 4–7 dB. Colour-matched medians: Citrus B lane 21.4 (74 % < 25), fruit clip 19.2, Citrus A 27.8 (12 % < 25), Klapmuts walk 17.2 (140 of 280 frames have a photo in h3dgs_e2), Gwakungu 18.2, Kendu Bay 15.5 — the three non-citrus segments are below 25 on every frame. **The grey frames are the views the model never trained on:** trained vs held-out (each chunk's own test.txt), colour-matched: Citrus B lane 25.7 vs 19.9 dB (held out 100 % < 25), fruit clip 23.2 vs 18.2, Citrus A 27.9 vs 23.7 (98 % < 25). The expo recipe held out EVERY OTHER keyframe on 05, so 203 of the lane's 368 frames are untrained views — the sawtooth in the graph, flicker at 8 fps; 01's 1-in-10 hold-out shows as periodic dips. Sky only (colour-matched, survey sky masks): Citrus B 23.8 / 22.9, Citrus A 31.5. The reels BLACK OUT the sky (09-27 instruction, `sidecar_demo_overlay.py` sky masks); the plain renders carry the rendered sky. Walks were captured at the raw LIO poses through the live stream — part of their deficit may be pose offset; not isolated.
**Fruit containment on the fruit clip (Paul: "and the fruit containment, measure this"; `automation/demo/fruit_clip_containment.py`).** 28 of the clip's 55 frames show tree 5's SAM3 fruit (id 10001, chunk-specific supervision; 342 oranges in total). Clip as made (densified v3 side-car, lit where the heat clears each fruit's VERDICT threshold): mean IoU 0.190 (trained 0.208 / held out 0.175), precision 0.332, 0.407 of fruit px within 1 px of light, 241/342 oranges lit, off-fruit light 25,539 px on tree 5's canopy, 253 px on other trees, 6,251 unlabelled. Native seed B (chunk supervision, no side-car) under the live no-cut rule (exclusive fruit + Otsu): mean IoU 0.190 (0.240 / 0.147), precision 0.373, 0.397 within 1 px, 233/342 oranges, 3 frames light nothing, off-fruit 14,368 px on tree 5, 0 on other trees, 4,721 unlabelled. **Disclosure: every identity light in the demo reels — trees, rows and fruit — is gated by per-block verdict thresholds fitted against the SAM3 mask on the verdict frame (`sidecar_demo_overlay.py` verdict_thr → tthr / rthr / fthr), the fitted-cut mechanism Paul ruled out today; the live stage uses no cut.** Native without any cut matches the clip's fruit (equal IoU, higher precision, less halo, never another tree).
**Fruit clip PSNR dip, examined (Paul: "how does the fruit clip drop in psnr").** Two parts: (1) every other frame is an untrained view (held out by the expo recipe): colour-matched median 18.2 vs 23.2 trained; (2) the first run (kf_000013–042, the survey's opening stretch) decays even on TRAINED views, 23.2 → ~18 dB over 30 frames, while the later runs (kf_000409–418, kf_003279–3290) hold 24–27. Ruled out: camera leaving the cell (all 55 inside chunk 1_0), canopy share of the frame (r ≈ +0.1), view coverage (training cameras within 3 m facing ±45°: median 10 vs 11 in the good runs; 14 → 7 along the run), photo sharpness (Laplacian variance 1052 vs 903 / 974; r = −0.29). Cause of (2) not isolated.
**What clears Paul's bar now — 25 dB render and IoU > 0.8 (Paul, 17:5x box: "video isn't fit for purpose, we need to show people 25 dBs and IoUs of over 0.8. Right now what can be made with this bar").** Render (colour-matched PSNR ≥ 25, the reel frames): Citrus A 392/401 trained frames (98 %), longest unbroken run 195 frames = 24.4 s at 8 fps; Citrus B lane 96/165 trained (58 %), longest run 4.6 s; fruit clip 10/24, longest 0.8 s; Klapmuts, Gwakungu, Kendu Bay 0 frames. Identity (best-threshold verdict IoU ≥ 0.8, chunk-supervision seed B, scored frames): Citrus A trees 16/25 heads, rows 9/10; Citrus B 0_0 trees 5/14, rows 4/5; 1_0 trees 5/16, rows 3/5; fruit 0/6 heads anywhere. Caveats: the render figure is colour-matched — showing it needs each frame rendered with its own trained exposure (trained views have one; the stage uses the mean); the IoUs are best-threshold verdicts on 15 scored frames per cell, so the no-cut lighting must be measured on the chosen frames before claiming > 0.8.
**Citrus A video under the bar, with sky (Paul, 18:0x box: "so make the new video and add sky"; `automation/demo/citrus_a_bar_video.py`, output `data/demo_video_v2/citrus_a_bar/`: citrus_a_bar.mp4 42.4 s 22.2 MiB, frames, metrics.csv, contact sheet; sent).** 01 chunk 3_1, the original reel's drive and six-segment storyline. Frame rule: only the chunk's TRAINED views, rendered at the chunk camera with that view's own trained exposure, sky included (no sky mask), kept if full-frame PSNR vs the photo ≥ 25 dB. Identity: native seed B (chunk supervision) with NO cut — per word the containment heat + per-frame Otsu / absence guard; rows and trees by plain containment (no exclusive step; my choice, Paul's row-rule decision was open); "this row" / "this tree" = the nearest scene-graph tree in front of the camera (the 'tree' segment keeps the script's focus, tree 176, when visible). Every lit object scored against its SAM3 mask (chunk supervision) on that frame; lit only if IoU ≥ 0.8; a single-query frame whose object misses is dropped. Result: 339 of 447 path frames shown; dropped 46 untrained, 21 < 25 dB, 41 single-query misses. Per segment (frames / PSNR min–median / lit objects, min IoU): plain 45 / 25.0–26.6; row 37 / 25.6–27.4 / 37, 0.83; tree 29 / 25.7–27.1 / 29, 0.83; every tree 122 / 25.2–26.8 / 185, 0.80; by row 60 / 25.2–26.4 / 93, 0.81; orchard 46 / 25.6–27.0. 22 multi-object frames light nothing (no object cleared 0.8) and say so. Each frame carries its own measured PSNR and IoU in the footer.
**Row IoU dips between frames, row 17, kf_003112–3135 (Paul: "show single consecutive frames, why is IoU dropping below 0.8"; `automation/demo/row_iou_frames.py`, sheet + CSV in `citrus_a_bar/row17_kf3112-3135/`).** The field is constant: every row-17 pixel scores 0.958 for row 17 on every frame, other rows' trees 0.62–0.70. The dips are the per-frame split (Otsu + median floor + absence guard): with the row a small part of the frame the split falls to 0.77–0.82 and lights unlabelled pixels (kf_3112–3113, 3120–3125: precision 0.08–0.79, 10–13 k px on other rows at 3123–3124); once the row is most of the frame the median IS the row's score, p99 − p50 = 0, and the absence guard declares the row absent — nothing lit at kf_3129–3135 (the case render_service's docstring says needs a caller cut). Candidate no-cut rule: light a pixel for the row whose word scores highest among the row words, gated only by the 0.5 identity norm — argmax, no split, no threshold.
**Paul (same exchange): "Keep answers concise, under one paragraph."** (memory: feedback-concise-answers)
**kf_003112 / 3113, row 17 — the IoU drop is a SAM3 gap (`automation/demo/row_frame_panels.py`, `citrus_a_bar/row17_panels_003112_003113_003115.png`).** The containment field lights the same whole row-17 tree at 3112, 3113 and 3115 (heat ~0.96, split 0.817–0.819). SAM3 labels a 5,066-px sliver of it at 3112, nothing at 3113, and the whole tree (35,637 px) at 3115 → IoU 0.070 / 0.000 / 0.827. The "false light" on 3112–3113 sits on the right tree; per-frame IoU against SAM3 undercounts wherever SAM3 misses a tree, and the bar video dropped such frames for that reason.
**Correction: the kf_003112 / 3113 blank is NOT a SAM3 miss; the global-id lifting voided SAM3's mask (Paul: "why is this row supervision blank, yet we have other trees … what rows do they belong to"; `automation/demo/supervision_void/`).** SAM3 detected the row-17 tree on both frames: oid 1617 at 3112 (26,126 px, score 0.56) and oid 1632 at 3113 (29,608 px, score 0.55) each cover 84–85 % of the field-lit tree. Neither has an entry in `sam3_v2/global_ids.json` `member_to_global`, and `markers_v2_from_sam3.py` writes id-less masks as void (they still carve pixels from the wider masks over the same area, oid 1626/1629, which own 0.4 % of the lit tree). Why no id: `cluster_tree_instances.py` lifts the laser returns inside the mask and keeps only those within 1.5 m of the mask's NEAREST return. The mask's left edge touches near tree 148 (row 18, centre 1.9–2.2 m deep), so the nearest return is on 148 at ~3 m and every return of tree 146 (6–9 m) is discarded; 5 points (3112) and 1 point (3113) survive, all inside 148's 3D box, below the 25-point coverage veto → unassigned. At 3115 the mask clears 148 and lifts 724 points at 6.1–7.6 m (470 inside 146's box) → id 146 and 35,637 px in the supervision. The 3112 sliver (5,066 px) is a separate small detection (oid 1618) that did get 146. The labelled trees on these frames: 148 (row 18, near left, ~2 m), 156 (row 20, near right, ~2 m), 157 (row 18, ahead right, ~8 m). The camera heads 46–47° across the rows here (the drive is turning), so near trees of rows 18 and 20 flank it. Caveats: the point counts come from the Aug-19 collect cache (same 1.5 m / 10 m gates, no partition hygiene); the Aug-22 ids ran with hygiene, under which these masks keep 98 % of their pixels. Depths use a LIO → H3DGS similarity fitted on 45 scene-graph trees (median residual 0.41 m). Consequence: per-frame IoU against the supervision undercounts wherever a far tree's mask touches a near tree (the depth-back anchoring that the coverage-veto comment in `cluster_tree_instances.py` describes for adoption; here it voids the far tree). The previous entry's "SAM3 gap" and commit 5ca7a12's message are wrong on the cause. (memory: supervision-void-depth-back)
**kf_003112 / 3113: the LiDAR does return on the blank tree; the lifting discards it (Paul: "surely we should get lidar returns on the closest tree?"; `automation/demo/supervision_void/lidar_in_mask.py`, figure `citrus_a_bar/row17_lidar_in_mask_003112_003113_003115.png`).** Raw scan projected with the lifting's own LaserProjector (rig intrinsics, Jackal default l2c, 80 ms match) into the centre-tree mask's owned pixels: 577 returns at 3112 (572 at 6–10 m, 426 inside tree 146's 3D box) and 667 at 3113 (660 at 6–10 m, 412 in 146's box). The nearest return is 3.28 m / 3.08 m, a few returns on tree 148's foliage at the mask's left edge (5 / 1 under 4.5 m); the 1.5 m window from it keeps only those, so 572 / 660 returns on the tree are dropped and the coverage veto unassigns the mask. At 3115 the nearest return is 6.22 m on the tree and 724 are kept → id 146. These counts reproduce the pipeline's collect cache exactly (5 / 1 / 724).
**The 01 scene graph's rows 16–28 run at 42–45° across the lanes (`automation/demo/supervision_void/rows_vs_lanes.py`, figure `citrus_a_bar/rows_vs_lanes_01_kf003112.png`). Corrects my previous entry's "the drive is turning".** Lane axis = length-weighted mode of the rover's keyframe headings (build_marker_hierarchy's own trajectory rule): LIO (x, z) = (+0.02, −1.00). Row axes by PCA of each row's trees: 27 of 40 rows within 10° of the lanes, but rows 16–28 (81 of 290 trees) sit at 42–45° — the limit the census row fit allows (`dominant_direction_xz` 1,0 with `dir_threshold` 0.7 admits ±45.6°), on a near-square ~6 × 6.5 m planting grid where a diagonal is as dense as a row. At kf_003112 the rover drives the lane (4° off the lane axis): the left planted row holds 148 (row 18, 2.3 m ahead) then 146 (row 17, 8.7 m); the right one holds 156 (row 20, 2.1 m), 157 (row 18, 8.2 m) and 147 (row 17, 13.8 m). So "row 17" and "row 18" each have trees on both sides of the lane. The survey embedder learned these rows (earlier today the row-17 word scored 0.958 on every row-17 tree's pixels, 147 included; trained 7 min after this hierarchy was written), so every Citrus A row query lights a diagonal — and "this row" (nearest tree's row) changes id as the rover passes consecutive trees of one planted row, the switching Paul saw. Row-IoU against the supervision cannot catch this: it scores against the same row ids. Fix (not started, Paul's call): refit 01's rows from the lane axis with a tighter direction check, retrain the survey embedder, re-seed (every 01 field seeded against 01_13B_v1g is orphaned — embedder-era pairing). Check the other surveys with rows_vs_lanes.py first. (memory: scene-graph-diagonal-rows)
**Check of the claim above that the embedder learned the diagonal row (`automation/demo/supervision_void/row_words_on_trees.py`).** The earlier 0.958 covered only frames where 147 was unlabelled. On kf_003133, 3138, 3141 and 3143, where the chunk supervision labels 147, the row-17 word ("cherry") scores 0.927–0.959 on tree 147 and 0.952–0.958 on tree 146. It is the best row word on both, and the two trees stand on opposite sides of the lane. The other trees follow their scene-graph rows: 145 → row 16 at 0.91, 157 → row 18 at 0.88. Planting grid measured from nearest neighbours over the 64 trees within 25 m of kf_003112: 6.3 m along the lanes, 6.5 m across.
**Every mask on kf_003112/3113/3115 through the lifting rules (Paul: "we missed the closest tree"; `automation/demo/supervision_void/chunk_void_scan.py`, run on those three frames only, output `chunk_3_1_sam3/void_scan.json`).** The nearest trees lift fine and get their ids: 148 keeps 425–620 returns and 156 keeps 85–249. Tree 146's mask sees 411–420 non-ground returns within 10 m but keeps 5 / 1, because the window starts at returns from 148 at the mask's edge (class "window"); at 3115 it keeps 462 and becomes 146. The chunk-wide count of window-blanked trees was not run.
**kf_003112: all LiDAR returns and all SAM3 masks on one frame (Paul: "show all lidar points projected to frame 3112, and then sam3 masks on this same image"; `automation/demo/supervision_void/frame_lidar_sam3.py`, figure `citrus_a_bar/kf003112_lidar_and_sam3.png`).** There are 6,431 returns in the frame, and the scan lines land on the matching canopies: near tree 148 at 2–3 m, the centre tree at ~7.5 m, the trees down the lane at 13–17 m. The five near returns inside the centre-tree mask (3.28–3.30 m, pixels 385–394 on row 247) are the end of one scan line on tree 148. They run ~10 px past 148's SAM3 outline, and none lies inside 148's own mask. That could be a tight SAM3 edge or a small LiDAR-camera offset: 01 has no rig.json, so the projector uses the Jackal default l2c. The same nearest-return window also mislabels in reverse. The small mask painted as tree 146 (oid 1618, 5,075 px) holds 150 returns: 29 at 6–10 m on the centre tree's right edge and 121 beyond 13 m on the farther tree it actually covers. Its window starts at 7.97 m, keeps the edge returns, and it adopts id 146. So on this frame the real 146 is blank and the tree behind it is painted 146.

The fit left 3 trees in that block without a row (gids 128, 129, 143). The planting spacing looks the same in all three blocks, so why the fit went diagonal only in the middle block is not established.

My earlier "CORAL had 10 rows > 30° off" lumped 9 straight diagonals with 1 catch-all. ## 2026-10-03 — global-id lifting: median depth anchor vs nearest return (01, experimental)

- **Goal:** Paul: "this is clearly a calibration error, we have to use a better rule, either median points not closest points". 01 has no rig.json, so the projector uses the Jackal default l2c. Near-tree returns land a few px inside neighbouring masks and set the nearest-return window.
- **Command/config:** aru_sil_core `ee9279a9` adds `cluster_tree_instances.py --depth-anchor median`: keep returns within `--depth-back` 1.5 m of the mask's MEDIAN depth, both sides. The default stays `min`. `--out-dir` was added for experiments. `automation/lifting_median_01.sh {median,min}` uses prod's 01 params (voxel 0.1, dilate 2, min_voxels 50, min_pts/voxel 1, split 0, depth-back 1.5, 10 m, ground 0.3, hygiene on) with masks from prod sam3_v2, and writes to `experimental/lifting_anchor_20261003/{median,min}/`. Both took 15 min in parallel. Judged with `automation/compare_lifting_anchor.py` and `automation/judge_lifting_anchor.py [--gains]`: the majority 3D tree box of a mask's own LiDAR returns within 10 m, using the lifting's projector and partition.
- **Result:** The nearest-return rerun reproduces prod exactly: 52,439 masks with points, 25,456 with an id, 290 trees. Median: 34,656 masks keep points (43,031 have none left within 10 m). The coverage veto removes 5,375 rather than 18,920. 27,626 masks get an id (1,286 vs 1,235 M SAM3 px). Still 290 trees: 289 match prod within 1.5 m (centroid shift median 0.06 m, max 0.38 m), but 196 of them get different id numbers, since ids follow first appearance.

  | median vs nearest (same code) | masks | SAM3 px | judged by the majority tree of the mask's returns within 10 m |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | gain an id | 3,220 | 63.9 M | 3,215 get the majority tree, 5 another tree |
  | lose an id | 1,050 | 12.4 M | 1,014 are masks mostly beyond 10 m whose old id came from nearer returns (corrections); 35 real losses; 1 other |
  | change tree | 206 | 3.4 M | 186 move to the majority tree, 20 away from it |
  | same | 24,200 | | |

  At kf_003112–3115: the centre tree's masks #1617 and #1632 go None → 146. #1618, the farther tree that had adopted 146, goes 146 → None. #1660 stays 146. #1661, tree 145 beyond 10 m, and the merged #1626 / #1669 stay None.
- **Verdict:** The median rule is better by this measure: ~4,400 masks right that were wrong or missing, ~60 newly wrong. Adopting it in prod needs, in order: (1) remap the median run's ids onto prod's numbering by centroid, so the supervision, hierarchy, embedder vocab, ledger and demo names keep their trees; (2) markers + semantic monolithics; (3) CORAL-strict hierarchy (rows should be unchanged with stable ids, so no embedder retrain); (4) block and chunk supervision maps; (5) re-census W (GPU) and re-seed all 01 fields; (6) verdicts. That is Paul's call. The calibration itself (a real 01 l2c) is untouched.

  - The bar is unchanged: trained views only, own exposure, sky in, PSNR ≥ 25 dB, every lit object IoU ≥ 0.8 vs the chunk supervision.
- **Result:** 364 of 447 path frames shown (v1: 339); dropped 46 untrained, 21 under 25 dB, 16 with an object under IoU 0.8. Per segment, shown/trained: row 61/73 (v1 37/73), tree 30/39, all 122/128 (240 lit trees), rows 60/64 (104 lit rows), plain 45, orchard 46. Min IoU of lit objects 0.80–0.82, PSNR min 25.0. 45.5 s, 23.6 MiB (phone-size). Contact sheet `contact_sheet_rows.jpg`: row 19 lights as one straight planted row along the lane on every sampled frame.
**v3: no IoU minimum (Paul: "remove the minimum iou, show them all"; `automation/demo/citrus_a_bar_video_v3.py`, `data/demo_video_v2/citrus_a_bar_v3/`).** Every object the query names is lit on every kept frame. There is no SAM3-presence requirement: "every tree" lights each tree in front by geometry, and "group by row" lights each row of those trees. Captions carry the measured IoU where SAM3 labels the object. Frames still need a trained view and ≥ 25 dB.
- Result: 380 of 447 frames shown; the only drops are 46 untrained and 21 under 25 dB.
- Lit objects: row 72 / 72 frames (min IoU 0.64), tree 30 lit over 35 frames, all 1,181 lit trees (min 0.35), rows 233 lit rows (min 0.75). 47.5 s, 26.0 MiB.
- Visible consequences on the contact sheet:
  - "Every tree" frames overlap neighbouring trees' live-rule masks, so colours stack at the edges (the lowest-IoU frames, kf_003446 0.35 and 3324 0.40).
  - 5 "which tree is this?" frames show the label with nothing lit, because the live rule's absence check declined.
  - Candidate (not done): argmax over the visible trees' words for "every tree" and "which tree", as for rows.

## 2026-10-04 — Citrus B cut (05 chunk 1_0) with fruit

- **Goal:** Paul: "show me a citrus cut" after "move on to citrus B and fruit".
- **Command/config:** `automation/demo/citrus_b_cut.py` (h3dgs env) runs the Citrus A v3 recipe on 05 chunk 1_0: h3dgs_expo project, cell chunk_1_0_sam3 seed B, 05_13D_v1g embedder, 05 rows (8, all along the lanes). No IoU minimum. Rows by argmax with one row per segment (row 4). It adds a FRUIT segment: the fruit clip frames (drive 13–52, 346–360) with the bilingual question, and tree 5's oranges (fruit 10001) lit natively through exclusive fruit scoring and the per-frame rule, with no threshold. Fruit is drawn magenta with a white outline, because orange-on-orange was invisible. `FULL=1` shows every frame: held-out views use the MEAN exposure and there is no PSNR minimum. It needs `torch.cuda.empty_cache()` per frame: the 511-frame run fragmented the allocator and OOM'd in expand_to_size at frame ~54, although single frames peak at 7.5 GiB.
- **Result:**
  - The bar version (trained views, ≥ 25 dB) keeps only 59 of 511 frames (7.4 s). Of the drops, 284 are held-out views, since the expo recipe held out every other keyframe, and 168 trained views render under 25 dB.
  - FULL: 511 frames, 63.9 s, 48 MiB, phone copy 22.9 MiB. PSNR median 19 dB (plain 19.3, orchard 14.6; min 9.5).
  - Fruit: lit on 40 of 55 frames, best IoU 0.44 (kf_000023), 0.43 (kf_003288) and 0.35 (kf_003282), min 0.06.
  - Row min IoU 0.21, trees min 0.18, "every tree" 931 lit, min 0.00.
  - Outputs in `data/demo_video_v2/citrus_b_cut{,_full}/`.
- **Verdict:** Citrus B is held back by render quality (half the views held out, most trained views under 25 dB) and by weak fruit identity. 05's supervision has not had the median-lifting fix; it has no rig.json either.
**Best containment everywhere (Paul: "no threshold is IoU, I still need best containment"; `citrus_a_bar_video_v4.py`, `citrus_b_cut.py`).** A pixel lights for an object only where that object's word has the highest containment heat among its competitors. Rows compete against the row words, trees against all tree words in the cell's word table, and fruit against every bank word (the exclusive test). There is no per-frame Otsu/absence split anywhere.
- Citrus A v4 (bar filters, no IoU minimum): 380 / 447 frames. "Which tree" lights on all 35 frames (v3: 30), min IoU 0.82. "Every tree" lights 1,024, min 0.12. Rows and "this row" are unchanged. 25.0 MiB.
- Citrus B full drive: fruit lights on 55 / 55 frames (was 40). On the 27 frames where SAM3 labels tree 5's fruit, mean IoU goes 0.197 (per-frame rule, 10-03) → 0.259; better on 17, worse on 10, best 0.58 (kf_000036). Held-out frames only: 0.147 → 0.161. Phone copy 22.9 MiB.
**Fruit with vs without densification, same frames (Paul: "so 0.16 is after we have densification?" / "makes no sense").** On the 27 Citrus B clip frames where SAM3 labels tree 5's fruit (`fruit_clip_containment.json` + `citrus_b_cut_full/metrics.csv`):
- Densified v3 fruit side-car with its fitted cut: 0.198 mean IoU (held-out 0.175, trained 0.225).
- Native H3DGS chunk without densification, per-frame rule: 0.197 (0.147 / 0.258).
- Native best containment: 0.259 (0.161 / 0.383).

The native chunk has never been fruit-densified: its 17.7 M nodes come from the colour training. The 0.301 of 10-01 was the side-car's single verdict frame with a fitted threshold, not a clip average. Densification as built in the side-car did not lift held-out fruit. The gap is trained vs held-out views (0.38 vs 0.16), so fruit identity is not carrying to new viewpoints.

## 2026-10-04 — Native fruit densification of the 05 chunk 1_0 (in progress)

- **Goal:** Paul: "densify the chunk then". The native Citrus B chunk was never fruit-densified (side-car only), so fruit-densify the H3DGS chunk itself. Decisive test: tree 5's fruit (10001) by best containment on the fruit clip frames, held-out especially, without losing PSNR.
- **Baseline** (`automation/h3dgs/fruit_densify/fruit_bc_score.py h3dgs_expo chunk_1_0_sam3 baseline`): 28 frames with SAM3 fruit, mean IoU 0.251 (held-out 15: 0.161, trained 13: 0.355). PSNR on those frames 20.5 (17.7 / 23.8); all clip frames 20.3 (trained 23.8, held-out 17.7).
- **Recipe** (`train_single_fruit.py`, `fruit_densify_05.sh`, sibling project `experimental/h3dgs_expo_fruitd` with camera_calibration and scaffold symlinked):
  - Fine-tune the trained chunk's leaves (12,258,477 incl. 100,000 scaffold, from done_*.pt) for 6,000 iterations: stock L1 + SSIM, position lr 2e-6 → 2e-7, densify every 300 from 0, no opacity reset, trained exposures loaded and frozen (lr 0), budget 15 M.
  - Fruit term: Gaussians whose projected centre is in a 2-px-dilated SAM3 fruit pixel (89 images, 92 k fruit px) get their densification gradient × 5 and are tagged. At each step, tagged carriers with max scale in (4 cm, 0.5 m) are split (cap 300 k per step), even over budget.
  - Then GaussianHierarchyCreator, stock train_post (15,000 its, with the chunk's 294 trained exposures restored), the native cell `chunk_1_0_sam3_fruitd`, and the same scorer.
  - Bug found on the first launch: H3DGS `render` returns visible INDICES (not a mask) and radii already subset to them.
- **Progress (13:49):** it 1200: 12.26 M → 13.30 M Gaussians. Per step ~270–420 k splits including 38–58 k fruit splits; loss 0.047.
**Fruit densification stopped; 05 chunk 1_0 retrain on the 01 formula first (Paul: "wait why is our mean PSNR 20 I thought we had passed 25" → "if not can we use citrus 01 formula to get better" → "retrain first before densification").**
- **Why 20 dB:** the clip average mixes trained views (23.8) with held-out views (17.7, mean exposure). The expo recipe that made the Citrus B chunk held out 365 of chunk 1_0's 659 views ("half views": 05 0_0 ladder 24.75 → 26.37 dB on in-cell TRAINING views, at the cost of half the coverage). 01's chunks use the shippable recipe: all views with the export's 1-in-10 held out (3_1: 82 of 810), 60k / 45k / 0.0075, 12 M cap, per-image exposure 0.001. In-cell training views: 3_1 28.23, 2_0 30.53, 7_2 31.09.
- **Densify fine-tune:** stopped at ~it 2000. Scripts kept for after the retrain.
- **Retrain:** `automation/h3dgs/retrain_05_1_0_01recipe.sh` (13:54). It runs in the sibling project `experimental/h3dgs_01recipe`: the expo chunk 1_0 hardlinked (same images and chunk-BA poses, rectified symlinked, scaffold hardlinked) with test.txt replaced by the h3dgs project's 1-in-10 list, so 588 views train and 71 are held out. Then train_single with the 01 flags, depths, scaffold, bounds and skybox locked, then the creator, post-opt (exposure 0.001), `h3dgs_eval_chunk.py` held-out, native cell `chunk_1_0_sam3_01r`, and the fruit / PSNR scorer (baseline expo: fruit 0.251, held-out 0.161; clip PSNR trained 23.8 / held-out 17.7).
- **Retrain result (chain done 17:21):**
  - Timings: train 8,079 s, hierarchy 2.6 GB, post-opt 3,477 s.
  - The chain's eval stage FAILED: `h3dgs_01recipe` had no `camera_calibration/aligned`. It is now symlinked from h3dgs_expo, whose aligned test.txt is the same 1-in-10 list.
  - Cell `chunk_1_0_sam3_01r` built: 53-word bank.
  - Scorer `fruit_bc_recipe01.json`: fruit best containment on 28 SAM3-fruit frames 0.251 → 0.289; clip PSNR 20.34 → 22.20.
  - **Paired by frame**, from the two scorer jsons, the gain comes from the SPLIT, not the recipe:

  | clip frames | n | PSNR expo → 01r | fruit IoU (SAM3-fruit frames) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | trained in both | 24 | 23.78 → 22.87 | 0.355 → 0.300 (13) |
  | held out in both | 6 | 17.00 → 17.85 | 0.174 → 0.179 (3) |
  | expo held-out, 01r trained | 25 | 17.85 → 22.59 | 0.157 → 0.305 (12) |

  - So the 01 recipe fits the views both models trained on 0.9 dB worse (twice the views on the same 12 M budget, hypothesis) and makes the clip better by training on 25 more of its frames. Clip frames at ≥ 25 dB: 8 of 55 (expo, max 25.94) → 2 of 55 (01r, max 25.34).
  - **Paired held-out eval** (`automation/h3dgs/eval_05_1_0_paired.sh`, `h3dgs_eval_chunk.py --only_chunk 1_0 --taus 0`) on all 71 views that both models held out:
    - expo 15.75 mean / 14.99 median → 01r 16.51 / 16.08: +0.76 mean, +0.94 median, 01r better on 51 of 71.
    - Sky-masked: 16.15 → 16.89.
    - Outputs are in `output/eval_heldout71/` of each project.
  - Verdict: the 01 recipe generalises +0.8 dB better and fits the shared training views 0.9 dB worse. Neither is near 25 dB away from its training views.


## 2026-10-04 — Cabbage cut: the photos and the SAM3 id maps are RAW frames, the splat is undistorted

- **Goal:** Paul: "while this runs show me a cabbage cut".
- **Command/config:** `automation/demo/cabbage_cut.py`, the Citrus B recipe on the gwakungu IMG_7993 lane chunk:
  - Inputs: `h3dgs_native/chunk_lane` features_census.bin (embedder v2_e400; the cut pins the embedder from the features json, because the alphabetical last is cabbage_v1c) and a new text bank (`build_text_bank.py`: 30 cabbage ids + 2 rows from the SAM3-derived id maps' manifest and the cabbage scene graph). The lane chunk has no scaffold.
  - Output: `data/demo_video_v2/cabbage_cut/` (72 frames, 9 s); not sent.
- **Result: the measurement references are wrong, so the cut is held back.**
  - The cut's PSNR (median ~15.5 dB in every segment) compared renders with `prod/scratch_sam3`. Those are the RAW phone frames.
  - The splat trains on `camera_calibration/rectified/images` (undistorted, PINHOLE fx 1563 / fy 1560 / cx 540 / cy 960). The two are the same frames (best edge match = same number for 72 / 72) but differ in geometry: edge NCC 0.41, ~28 grey levels mean abs diff.
  - Against the training images: trained image_12 22.8 dB; held-out image_30 17.9 and image_0 15.6 (no exposure).
  - The SAM3 id maps (`h3dgs_sidecar_chunks/chunk_lane/supervision/trees_only`) follow the RAW photos: image-edge strength on label boundaries is 1.12–1.23× mean on raw vs 0.63–0.81× on rectified (image_12 / 30 / 50).
  - So the cabbage census (and any IoU vs those maps) pairs undistorted renders with distorted labels.
- **Verdict / next:** find the raw camera's distortion (the image-farm GLOMAP model before undistortion) and undistort the id maps (nearest neighbour) into the training camera. Then re-census and re-seed the lane cell, and score PSNR against the rectified images.
**Where the cabbage undistortion came from (Paul: "we've never undistorted masks for phone videos, we don't have intrinsics so how can that be").** There are no factory intrinsics, but the phone survey's own SfM self-calibrated the camera: `IMG_7993_s0/sparse/0/cameras.bin` is OPENCV 1080×1920, fx 1563.01 / fy 1560.35 / cx 540 / cy 960, k1 0.14825, k2 −0.26098, p1 0.00238, p2 −0.00032. H3DGS's preprocessing (`preprocess/generate_colmap.py` → `colmap image_undistorter`) used it to write the training images into `camera_calibration/rectified` as a PINHOLE camera with the same focal and centre (`h3dgs_8m` and the demo_root copy). The black side bands in the renders are the undistortion's empty border. The SAM3 id maps were never undistorted, so they are off the splat by a median 8.8 px, p95 21.6, max 22.3 (largest mid-frame top and bottom; corners 5.7, where k1 and k2 partly cancel). generate_colmap.py already carries a mask path ("undistort masks the same way images were distorted") that the SAM3 maps never went through. Same check pending for the other phone farms.
**Cabbage: masks undistorted, embedder rebuilt, re-seeded (Paul: "undistort the masks and reseed the cabbage").**
- **Undistortion** (`automation/image_farm/undistort_id_maps.py`): OPENCV → PINHOLE, nearest neighbour, outside → 65535. Check on image_12: warped raw photo vs training image edge NCC 0.314 → 0.930, mean abs diff 25.7 → 4.2. 3.3 % of output pixels fall outside the raw frame (the black bands). 72 maps → `h3dgs_native/chunk_lane_ud/supervision/trees_only`.
- **Re-census** (hier_census, the old cell's exact recipe: 64 views, τ 3, cut 1080, bg row): 993,614 nodes with label weight (raw maps 1,020,478); seed 122,107 assigned (raw 130,811).
- **The real blocker was the cabbage embedder.** Round trip of the 30 cabbage words through the seed → decode path (raw CLIP in, as build_census_init does):

  | embedder | own word recovered | cos own vs other |

  | v2_e400 (image-farm recipe: contrastive 0.1, temperature 2.22, 400 ep) | 10 % | 0.471 vs 0.433 |
  | v1c (same recipe, 100 ep) | 3 % | 0.378 vs 0.377 |
  | 01_13B_v1g (citrus graph c20cos20) | 100 % | 0.996 vs 0.291 |
  | **new v3g** (citrus graph recipe on the cabbage scene graph, 1500 ep; `experimental/embedder_v3g/marker_hierarchy_graph.json` adds super_row 0) | **97 %** (29/30; the 30th id is not in the scene graph) | **0.983 vs 0.288** |

  | v2_e400, raw-map census | 0.020 | 0.017 | 0.038 | 1 % |
  | v2_e400, undistorted census | 0.027 | 0.024 | 0.044 | 2 % |
  | v3g, raw-map census | 0.393 | 0.407 | 0.313 | 37 % |
  | **v3g, undistorted census** | **0.454** | **0.474** | **0.341** | **44 %** |

- **Cabbage cut v2** (`cabbage_cut.py`: chunk_lane_ud + features_census_v3g, PSNR vs the rectified training image):
  - 72 frames, 9 s, 19.3 MiB. PSNR medians 22–23 dB.

**SAM3 masks vs the field on "how many cabbages are there?" (Paul: "show the sam3 masks vs the how many cabbages are there"; `automation/image_farm/cabbage_sam3_vs_field.py`, figure `cabbage_cut/sam3_vs_field_how_many.png`; same per-cabbage colours in both panels).**

- **image_40:** 15: 0.23, 16: 0.39, 18: 0.42, 19: 0.30. Mid-distance heads are speckled: best containment flips between cabbage words inside one head.

So the "how many" count is the field's; SAM3 alone would count 2–7 per frame. The field is right on near heads, noisy on mid-distance heads, and misses partial heads at the frame edge.

- Per segment (median dB, frames ≥ 25, identity min-IoU median):
