- **Figure:** `automation/demo/citrus_b_fruit_sam3_vs_field.py` → `citrus_b_cut_trained/fruit_sam3_vs_field.{jpg,json}`. It covers the 13 training-view fruit frames, zoomed.
  - Field: the cut's magenta pixels, recovered exactly from the saved frames (lit = 0.1 render + 0.9 magenta). This matches the scorer's lit count on frames clear of the caption boxes (kf_000415 1,456 = 1,456); the caption boxes hide some lit pixels on others.
  - SAM3: the RAW "fruit" detections (`prod/bateleur/sam3_fruit`, run inside each census tree's crop).
- **The supervision keeps 100 % of SAM3's raw tree-5 fruit on all 13 frames.** My mid-run lead (that the supervision dropped fruit SAM3 found on kf_003284) was WRONG: the raw tree-5 fruit there is 1 mask, 88 px. The 18 entries I saw were clip_041's 18 frames.
- **Field vs raw SAM3 tree-5 fruit:** IoU 0.331 mean (scorer 0.355). Of the field's pixels, 43 % (mean per frame) sit on SAM3's tree-5 fruit and 0.0 % on other trees' SAM3 fruit. Recall of SAM3's tree-5 fruit is 0.63.
- **Colour split** (`citrus_b_fruit_colour_split.py`, CIELAB a*b* nearest centroid; a proxy, not ground truth) of the 20,157 field pixels outside SAM3's tree-5 fruit:
  - 43 % are fruit-coloured, i.e. nearer SAM3's fruit pixels than the frame's tree pixels: likely oranges SAM3 missed. kf_003284 is 66 %: visible oranges low on the right tree, which SAM3 left unmasked.
  - 57 % are leaf-coloured: bleed around the fruit, the halo of magenta around green in the agreement panels.
- So the IoU understates the field wherever SAM3 under-detects. The leaf bleed is the field's own error, and the target for fruit densification.
**Fruit false positives, per blob (Paul: "this is not clear, also are there any false positives").** `automation/demo/citrus_b_fruit_fp.py` → `citrus_b_cut_trained/fruit_false_positives.{jpg,json}`, CPU only. The figure shows photo | verdict overlay at 2× zoom.
- **Visible region only:** the caption boxes (81,345 px per frame, exact fill colours) are removed from both the field and SAM3.
- **Each 8-connected field blob gets one verdict:** it touches SAM3 tree-5 fruit; touches only another tree's SAM3 fruit; or touches no SAM3 fruit. The last group is split by CIELAB a*b* nearest centroid (SAM3 fruit vs tree pixels of the same frame), a colour proxy.
- **13 training-view frames, 742 lit blobs:**
  - 145 on SAM3 tree-5 fruit
  - 3 on another tree's fruit
  - 225 orange-coloured with no SAM3 fruit
  - **369 leaf-coloured with no SAM3 fruit = false positives**
  - SAM3 tree-5 fruit masks: 134 of 138 found.
- **Pixels: 33,510 lit:**
  - 13,473 (40 %) on SAM3 fruit
  - 7,101 (21 %) bleed around them
  - 6,932 (21 %) orange-coloured with no SAM3 mask
  - 6,004 (18 %) leaf false positives: specks, mean 16 px per blob
- **The yellow blobs are mostly oranges SAM3 missed.** kf_003284: SAM3 masked 1 fruit, the field lights 55 orange-coloured blobs on the low oranges. kf_003282: SAM3 masked the canopy fruit and missed the bright low ones. Some visible oranges are missed by both, e.g. kf_000025's bottom-centre fruit.
**Whole-clip cabbage H3DGS done (20:52).** `IMG_7993_sall/h3dgs` at the 8 M recipe:
- Train 6,656 s, 8,045,212 Gaussians; hierarchy 1.8 GB; post-opt 30k in 1 h 31 min.
- Held-out: 13 views, median 18.46, mean 18.21. Training sample: 40 views, median 22.92, mean 23.19.
- Same recipe on the 72-frame s0: held-out 18.16, training 23.43. So the opening 54 frames come at no held-out cost (+0.3) and −0.5 on the training sample.
- Identity for the whole clip (SAM3 on frames 0–53, undistortion, v3g embedder, census) has not started.

**Fruit false-positive trace queued (Paul: "cool queue").** `automation/h3dgs/native/fruit_fp_trace.py` started at 20:52 on the free GPU.
- CPU part already done, from W.npz and the seed-B rule; the recomputed set equals the features file, which holds 8,981 tree-5 fruit nodes.
  - Only 1,749 (19 %) have fruit as their census majority. 7,232 (81 %) were promoted by `--fruit-share-assign 0.1` while a tree was the majority (tree 5 for 6,132).
  - Fruit share of the 8,981: median 0.216; 4,123 sit at 0.1–0.2.
- **Not densification:** the expo chunk 1_0 was never fruit-densified.
**Fruit false positives on kf_000025 traced to Gaussians (Paul: "focus on kf 25, tell me why we have those, are the gaussians fruits in previous frames that got densified").** Script: `automation/h3dgs/native/fruit_fp_trace.py`. It uses hier_census.py's math on the cell's cut (tau 3 at 1280 px) with the verdict classes as labels, at full resolution; outputs in `citrus_b_cut_trained/fp_trace/`.
- **Who paints the red (leaf false-positive) pixels:** 1,052 px. Weight share by seed:
  - tree-5 fruit by the share rule: 46.4 %
  - a tree/row target: 42.0 %
  - no identity: 7.4 %
  - tree-5 fruit by fruit majority: **3.5 %**
- For comparison, green (on SAM3 fruit, 3,914 px): share rule 44.6 %, fruit majority 19.6 %, tree 28.2 %.
- **The 171 fruit-seeded Gaussians behind 90 % of the red fruit weight:**
  - Seeded: 157 by the share rule, 14 by majority. Census majority: tree 5 for 152.
  - Weighted fruit share 10/50/90 %: 0.11 / 0.15 / 0.38, so the typical one is ~85 % tree in its census.
  - Size median 1.09 cm (green fruit Gaussians 0.75 cm).
  - Distance to the nearest green-fruit Gaussian: 7 / 19 / 86 cm.
- **Origins (the census again, per training view, real supervision):** over the 294 views these 171 gathered fruit 291 vs tree 1,244 vs void 850.
  - The fruit came from only 17 views.
  - 58 % of it came from the three approach frames next to kf_000025: kf_000029 80.4, kf_000027 56.9, kf_000023 31.6.
  - The return pass added kf_003282 20.1, kf_003288 19.8, kf_003279 15.0, kf_003286 13.2.
- **Mechanism, measured:**
  1. The census credits every Gaussian's alpha weight on a SAM3 fruit pixel to the fruit, including leaves in front of the orange or at the mask edge from those angles.
  2. ~15 % fruit credit passes `--fruit-share-assign 0.1`.
  3. From kf_000025 the same leaves sit beside the orange, so the field lights leaves.
  - **Not densification:** the chunk was never fruit-densified. "Fruit in previous frames": yes, in the neighbouring frames' SAM3 masks.
- **Trade-off:** the share rule also carries 44.6 % of the fruit weight on TRUE fruit pixels (majority-only 19.6 %), so dropping it costs most of the recall. Hypothesis, not tested: a per-view vote (fruit in most of the views where the Gaussian is visible) would separate "in front of an orange from one angle" from "is the orange".
- **One cluster followed across views** (`fruit_fp_trace.py` step 4, CPU redraw `fruit_fp_cluster_fig.py` → `fp_trace/fruit_fp_cluster.jpg`). These are the 32 red-fruit Gaussians within 0.3 m of the heaviest red spot, carrying 16 % of the 171 nodes' red weight.
  - 29 of the 32 were seeded by the share rule. Census fruit share median 0.20 (0.105–0.765).
  - All of their fruit credit comes from two views: kf_000029 58.6 (81 %) and kf_000027 14.1 (19 %). There they fall on two SAM3 oranges.
  - kf_000023 gives none. In kf_000025 they sit on leaves beside the orange: the red pixels.
- The first draw captioned every origin view "inside SAM3 fruit". That was wrong for kf_000023 and has been redrawn with measured captions.

## 2026-10-05 — Audit: the fruit-seeded Gaussians that light leaves (Citrus B)

**Question (Paul):** "do an audit and tell me where these gaussians on leaves come from and why they don't go to trees. If they aren't supervised how do they end up there".

**Method:** `automation/h3dgs/native/fruit_fp_audit.py`, run via `logs/fruit_fp_audit.run.sh`; output `citrus_b_cut_trained/fp_audit/fruit_fp_audit.json`.
- Scope: expo chunk 1_0, cell chunk_1_0_sam3, the 13 training-view fruit frames.
- Attribution uses the census math on the cell's cut (tau 3 at 1280 px) at full resolution. The FP set is the fruit-seeded Gaussians carrying 90 % of the red (leaf false-positive) weight; the TP set is the same for green (on SAM3 fruit).
- Per set it measures: hierarchy role, size, opacity, SH-DC colour (CIELAB nearest centroid: fruit-majority vs tree-5-seeded Gaussians), census diet, a per-view census over the 294 training views with the real supervision, and the seed path without the 0.1 share rule.
- First run died: skimage is not in the h3dgs env, so it now uses OpenCV Lab.

| | FP set (red) | TP set (green) |
|---|---|---|
| Gaussians | 654 | 1,544 |
| seed path without the share rule (weight) | **would be a tree 79 %**, fruit majority 13 %, void-withheld 6 %, below floor 2 % | would be a tree 68 %, fruit majority 26 %, void 6 %, floor 1 % |
| hierarchy leaves (trained Gaussians) | 87 % | 88 % |
| max scale median / opacity median | 0.98 cm / 0.92 | 1.03 cm / 0.82 |
| orange-coloured (weight) | 42 % | 53 % |
| census diet: tree 5 / other trees / fruit 10001 | 61 / 13 / 25 % (void 25 %) | 54 / 13 / 31 % (void 23 %) |
| total labelled weight median | 6.5 | 5.1 |
| training views that see them (median) | 23 | 25 |
| views giving fruit-5 weight / views where fruit beats tree | 2 / 1 | 2 / 1 |
| share of seen views where fruit wins | 7 % | 7 % |

- **Overlap: 444 of the 654 FP Gaussians are also TP Gaussians.** The same Gaussians paint true fruit pixels in some frames and leaf pixels in others. FP and TP are not two populations.
- **Under the red pixels the frame's own supervision says:** 6,004 px = tree 5 4,722 (79 %), void 1,182 (20 %), other tree 100, fruit 0.
- **Answers:**
  - **Where they come from.** Trained leaf-level Gaussians of tree 5's canopy at and around the oranges, about 1 cm and opaque. 42 % of their weight is orange-coloured, so this is the fruit's own surface Gaussians plus leaves touching it. No densification is involved.
  - **Why not trees.** 79 % of the FP weight sits on Gaussians whose census majority is tree 5 and which pass every tree check (above the 1.0 floor, void not dominant). Seed B's `--fruit-share-assign 0.1` overrides that majority: ~25 % fruit pooled from ~2 views beats ~61 % tree from ~23 views.
  - **"Not supervised."** They are supervised, mostly as tree 5, including under the false-positive pixels themselves (79 % tree 5). But a Gaussian gets ONE label pooled over all its views, and the share rule decides it.
- **Correction:** the "per-view vote" fix proposed yesterday cannot separate them. FP and TP Gaussians both win fruit in 7 % of the views that see them, and fruit is never most of any carrier's diet. Even the TP weight is 68 % "would be a tree".
- **Open hypotheses, untested:** (a) mixed carriers at the fruit silhouette paint fruit from some angles and leaf from others; (b) see-through: partly occluded fruit Gaussians behind leaves carry alpha weight at leaf pixels. Decisive test: depth of the fruit-seeded weight at red pixels vs the rendered surface depth. If (a), splitting the carriers (fruit densification) is the geometric remedy, which revises yesterday's "densifying will not remove these" to untested.

## 2026-10-05 — Bar walks for the final cut: Klapmuts lane 2 and Kendu Bay (for the dashboard session)

**Request:** the dashboard session, on Paul's final-cut audit, asked for "a Klapmuts lane bar walk and a Kendu Bay (IMG_7975_s0) bar walk: trained views only in drive order, each view's own exposure, sky in, keep if ≥ 25 dB, PSNR caption like citrus_a_bar; RGB only, 8 fps".

**Script:** `automation/demo/bar_walk.py`, generic for any H3DGS chunk. It renders with CompactHierarchy at tau 3, the citrus_a_bar setting, and measures full-frame PSNR against `camera_calibration/rectified/images`. There is no map inset; the citrus_a_bar scripts draw none.

**Results:**
- `klapmuts_bar`: `dec_2025_ten_rows/experimental/lane2/h3dgs_e2`.
  - 224 trained views, median 28.99 (24.55–30.98).
  - 222 kept at ≥ 25 dB, kept median 29.00.
  - 27.8 s; mp4 18.6 MiB, phone copy 9.0 MiB.
- `kendu_bar`: `kendu_bay/2026-05-14/IMG_7975_s0/h3dgs`.
  - 54 trained views, median 26.21 (24.59–36.67).
  - 51 kept, kept median 26.25.
  - 6.4 s; 11.0 MiB / 3.4 MiB.

**Renderer bug, found and fixed.** The Cam class copied from hier_render_service built FoVx from FoVy and the aspect ratio, which assumes fx = fy.
- Kendu's fx 1616.4 vs fy 1605.9 is a 0.65 % difference, about 3.5 px off at the frame edges. The first Kendu pass read median 20.73 with 0 frames kept. With FoVx = 2·atan(W / 2fx), as in the evaluator's make_cam, it reads 26.21; the evaluator's training sample said 26.06.
- tau is not a factor: 0 / 1 / 3 gave identical PSNR on 5 views.
- Other cuts that used the same Cam: 01 and 05 shift 0.3 px (negligible). The cabbage cut shifts 0.9 px and the chilli renders 0.8 px, so their PSNR may be slightly understated; not re-measured.

## 2026-10-05 — Chillies: class-level pepper identity, then the chilli trainings

**Paul:** "why can't we train with those, pepper masks", then "go class-level pepper after the renders then and then kick off the chili trainings, i think klapmuts should also get some q/a but that can come afterwards".

**Why the probe masks were thin:** the full-frame SAM3 probe (`build_tree_instances.py`, prompt "pepper") found pods on only 50 of 241 frames (1,402 masks). Three causes:
- SAM3 resizes the 1080×1920 frame to its ~1008 px input.
- The whole-image presence score multiplies every mask.
- The probe's `--min-mask-area 400` drops small pods.

**Tiled pass** (`automation/image_farm/sam3_tiled_class.py`):
- 3×4 overlapping tiles (15 %), each its own SAM3 image; conf 0.3, min area 20 px; class maps OR-ed per frame.
- Result: pods on **227 of 241 frames, 18,659 masks**. Frames 0–141: 133 of 142 with masks, median 38 masks and 12,551 px per frame.
- Frame 206: 255 masks vs the probe's 39 ("chili"), on red and green pods. Runtime 323 s.

**Chain** (`automation/image_farm/pepper_class_chain.sh`, 15:39–15:47):
- Maps: uint16, pepper = fruit 10000, every other pixel = 0 (plant/other), undistorted OPENCV → PINHOLE (3.1 % outside → 65535).
- Graph: 1 object, 1 row, 1 fruit.
- Embedder `IMG_7990_s1_pepper_v1` (citrus recipe, 2.5 min). Round trip: each target decodes to its own word (1.0 on the diagonal, 0.42–0.68 off it).
- Census: 127 training views 0–141, the tail being excluded for its collapsed poses; 11.9 M nodes, 10 s.
- Seeds: majority 22,367 pepper Gaussians; share > 0.3 adds 61,754; share > 0.1 adds 174,866.
- Text bank: asus = plant/other, bumper = pepper, oak = row.

**First score: zero.** All three seeds had IoU 0.000 and precision 0.000 on frames 0–141. On the broken frames 142–240 they lit nearly every pixel (104 M lit px).
- Debug on image_60: rendered feature norms are 5.3–5.6, plant-dominated.
- Comparing only the pixel's OWN decoded word, pepper wins on 23.4 % of SAM3 pepper pixels and 0.4 % of other pixels.
- But best containment also takes the max over a 4-step walk toward the root. For a pod that walk passes the plant (parent) node, so the plant word wins every pepper pixel.
- The scorer now reports both rules ('walk' and 'own').

**Chilli trainings queue** (`automation/image_farm/chilli_trainings_queue.sh`, started 15:47 after the chain): s1a = clip 153–294 (fresh SfM, track check + camera-step check), then s0 = clip 0–150 (path healthy, max step 1.7×), then s1b = the tail (trained only if its step check passes). Each is H3DGS 8 M.
- **Demo card windows** (dashboard session for Paul, from the run-of-show: no training during the demo, because training starved Gemma into 503s in the 1 Oct bank run):
  - Card free from 18:15 UTC (20:15 SAST) until the dashboard says "rehearsal done" (~45 min).
  - Card free again from 05:00 UTC Tue (07:00 SAST) until the demo is over (~11:00 UTC); a segment that can't finish is not started.
- v1 (`chilli_trainings_queue.sh`) was stopped at 15:53, while still waiting for the GPU after s1a's SfM. s1a: 142 cameras, step check OK, max 1.5×.
- **v2** (`automation/image_farm/chilli_trainings_queue_v2.sh`) runs H3DGS phase by phase, with the same commands as `image_farm_h3dgs.sh`.
  - Each phase starts only if its measured duration fits before the next blackout: train 7,200 s, hierarchy 600 s, post-opt 6,600 s, eval 900 s, segment SfM 1,800 s.
  - A watchdog kills a phase still running when a blackout starts.
  - "rehearsal done" → `touch /home/paperspace/logs/rehearsal_done.flag`.
- **Own-decode rescore** (`pepper_bc_score.py`, rules 'walk' and 'own'; frames 0–141, 'pepper' masks):

  | seed | trained IoU (median) | precision | recall | held-out IoU |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | majority (22 k pepper Gaussians) | 0.208 (0.195) | 0.617 | 0.248 | 0.111 |
  | **share > 0.3** (+62 k) | **0.308 (0.294)** | 0.517 | 0.507 | 0.191 |
  | share > 0.1 (+175 k) | 0.267 (0.278) | 0.297 | 0.887 | 0.191 |

  - The 'walk' rule stays at 0 for every seed. The broken-pose frames 142–240 light nearly everything (precision 0.02) under both rules.
  - The share > 0.1 pattern (high recall, low precision) is the citrus leaf-halo pattern again.
- **The 'pepper' masks are mostly green.** The figure `cell/pepper_vs_sam3.jpg` (`pepper_fig.py`) shows cyan masks on green elongated shapes, while the red pods are often unmasked.
  - HSV over frames 0–141: SAM3 'pepper' pixels are 14.7 % red-coloured and 54.3 % green-coloured.
  - Only 34.1 % of the red-coloured photo pixels sit inside any mask.
  - Some of the field's "off-SAM3" pixels are red pods SAM3 missed (image_86).
- **Prompt test** (tiles, frames 55–66, raw frames; red = HSV hue < 10 or > 170, sat > 120, val > 70; green = hue 35–85, sat > 60):

  | prompt | masked px | red | green | red px covered |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | pepper | 233,763 | 9.6 % | 67.6 % | 42.4 % |
  | chili pepper | 601,211 | 6.3 % | 68.8 % | 71.4 % |
  | **red chili pepper** | 145,155 | 27.9 % | **0.8 %** | **76.7 %** |
  | red pepper | 125,567 | 31.7 % | 0.6 % | 75.3 % |

- **Re-run with 'red chili pepper'** on all 241 frames into `sam3_tiled_red/`, then a new cell `cell_red` (same graph and embedder; census, seeds, bank, score). The first cell stays as the record. The chain and scorer now take `TILED=` / `CELL=`.
- **cell_red** ('red chili pepper' masks; tiled pass 734 s, run beside the s1a training). Seeds: majority 27,108 pepper Gaussians; share > 0.3 adds 58,183; share > 0.1 adds 140,962. Own-decode, frames 0–141:

  | seed | trained IoU (median) | precision | recall | held-out IoU (median) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | majority | 0.357 (0.360) | 0.716 | 0.414 | 0.281 (0.313) |
  | **share > 0.3** | **0.437 (0.446)** | 0.633 | 0.580 | **0.328 (0.365)** |
  | share > 0.1 | 0.357 (0.364) | 0.378 | 0.854 | 0.260 (0.306) |

  - On a par with the cabbage (0.45), above Citrus B fruit (0.25–0.29).
  - Figures: `cell_red/pepper_vs_sam3.jpg`, and `pepper_vs_sam3_zoom.jpg` (×3 on the busiest spot; CPU from the figure). Lit fills sit on SAM3's red pods with some spill onto leaves.
  - This is still the OLD s1 splat (census from views 0–141). Redo the census on s1a after tonight's retrain.
**Chilli cut** (Paul: "make a chilli cut, where are the red chillies"). `automation/demo/chilli_cut.py` → `data/demo_video_v2/chilli_cut/`. Rendered 19:14 UTC, on the card the dashboard lent before its rehearsal.

## 2026-10-05 — Live stage: Citrus B lane walk and nearest-camera exposure (for the dashboard session)

**Request:** Paul on the live stage: "citrus farm B renders served looked so bad". The live walk was the whole in-cell 1_0 trajectory (474 poses, including headland turns and the open track) at MEAN exposure.

**(1) Lane-only walk root** `05_13D_Jackal/experimental/h3dgs_demo/expo_chunk_1_0_lane/`: 77 poses in drive order.
- LIO is y-down, so the lane axis is LIO z. Lane A sits between the rows at x ≈ −2.5 and +3.
- A pose is kept if |x − 0.5| ≤ 1 m, its heading is within 25° of the lane axis, and z lies within the flanking trees' extent (2.66–17.93) ± 1 m.
- Result: kf 12–48 (+z, 37) and kf 3279–3318 (−z, 40), an out-and-back loop.
- **45 of the 77 are held-out views** of the expo split. They render at a median 18.2 dB (mean exposure) against 25.4 for the 32 trained poses (own exposure; citrus_b_cut_full metrics).
- Hence also `expo_chunk_1_0_lane_trained/` (32 trained poses only), the recommended one.

**(2) `HIER_EXPOSURE=nearest`** in `hier_render_service.py` (aru_sil_core/src 79e67eb8, local commit, not pushed). Opt-in; mean unchanged.
- Per frame it applies the exposure of the nearest trained camera facing within ~45° (cameras.json c2w × exposure.json); nearest by position if none faces that way.
- Offline check on the lane poses: walk poses (R_W·LIO) vs cameras.json give forward dot 1.000 and position offset median 0.225 m (max 0.39). So 24 of 32 trained poses select their own camera, the rest a neighbour (median offset 1 kf, max 7).
- Not render-tested; the dashboard's services held the card.

## 2026-10-06 — Klapmuts April lane 2: the lane recipe (Paul: "run it now")
- **Goal:** December's lane recipe (E2) on April lane 2, so April and December are compared on the same recipe. The decisive test is the April E2 training-view median against December's 29.72 (held-out against 19.82), then the same spot from both (kf_000285 ↔ f_01186).
- **Rig (Paul: "Klapmuts April is the same rig as Dec right"):** the records say yes.
  - Both rig.json files hold the identical laser→camera extrinsic.
  - December's entry says "identical to the Jan klapmuts rig entry (same sensor head)".
  - Both carry the citrus-copy camera (529.40/529.65 @ 647.20/354.64). The April run therefore uses the ZED conf December's lane recipe used (527.985/527.88 @ 638.975/333.18), not April's rig.json.
  - Paul: "April is zed LIO, December is vo". These are the surveys' own pose sources; the lane recipe replaces both with KISS-ICP LO.
- **Base v1 (rejected): `automation/tenrows/apr_lane_base.py`.**
  - Method: the April H3DGS export keyframe poses, slerped to the 613 stream stamps; LiDAR init from every 3rd frame's nearest scan placed through those poses.
  - Output: 1.35 M points; kept as `transforms_lo_export.json` and `init_lidar_export.ply`.
  - Self-consistency test: single scans placed as in the init, then ICP of scan j onto scan i for frames 2, 5 and 10 m apart, at three places in the lane. The ICP correction is the misplacement between the two frames (script in the session scratchpad, `scan_consistency.py`).
  - Correction at the camera, median over 9 pairs (single-scan ICP is noisy; read the medians, not single pairs):

    | base | correction at camera | rotation | NN-median gain from ICP |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | April v1 (export poses) | 10.2 cm (4.8–19.3) | 0.56° | 0.3–5 cm |
    | December lane 2 (KISS-ICP LO, the recipe) | 4.3 cm (2.2–11.1) | 0.47° | 0–2.8 cm |

    April v1's error grows with separation (2 m: 5–10 cm; 10 m: 10–19 cm).
  - So the export-placed init is about 2.5× less consistent than the recipe's.
- **Base v2 (used): `automation/tenrows/apr_lane_lo.py`, December's construction.**
  - `dump`: 469 scans of the lane window ± 3 s from laser.monolithic → `lane2_apr/laser_dump/`, in the tenrows_laser_dump.py format.
  - KISS-ICP with December's settings (`tenrows_kiss_icp.py`: voxel 0.4, range 0.6–60 m, deskew off): 469 scans in 1.0 min, 43.8 m path.
  - `base`: the LO pose slerped at each frame stamp × laser→camera⁻¹ (tenrows_lane_prep.py lo) → `transforms_lo.json`. End-to-end distance 34.93 m against the export's 34.74 m (scale agrees to 0.5 %); the LO path is longer (38.1 vs 35.6 m) from frame-to-frame jitter, which the warp's LOESS (σ 15 frames) smooths.
  - LiDAR init: every frame's nearest scan, placed with its own LO pose (tenrows_lo_lidar_init.py --stamps) → `init_lidar.ply`: 1,462,589 points at 5 cm, all 613 frames, 35 % coloured.
  - Same test: correction at the camera, median **5.9 cm** (2.5–12.1), rotation 0.27°; ICP leaves the NN median unchanged (−0.3 to +0.5 cm), i.e. nothing left to fix at single-scan resolution. December's level; the like-for-like base.
- **SfM:** `automation/tenrows/apr_lane_run.sh` (frozen copy in `/home/paperspace/logs/`), stage `sfm`, 00:26–01:11 box (2,672 s beside the Citrus B stage service). GPU SIFT + exhaustive + GLOMAP on 613 frames, fixed ZED-conf PINHOLE camera.
  - Result: 613/613 registered, 330,648 points, reprojection 0.65 px (December: 562, 350,052, 0.74 px).
  - **But the chain is broken into three pieces.** Jumps of 7.5 m at f_01176→77 and 5.6 m at f_01261→62 (the LO steps are 6.7 and 7.8 cm); piece scales 2.228 / 1.866 / 2.146.
  - The frames at the cuts are clean: sharp, unchanged exposure, 2,353 and 2,485 inliers to their neighbours (typical 3,212).
  - **Why it split (measured, Paul: "why did glomap split the lanes? we seeded from laser odom").**
    - Neither December's recipe nor this port seeds GLOMAP from the LO. GLOMAP's global positioning starts from random camera positions (`InitializeRandomPositions`, glomap/estimators/global_positioning.cc:57); the LO enters only at the warp.
    - The match graph is not weak at the cuts. 71,782 verified pairs cross cut 1 (4.2 M inliers), against a median of 48,066 elsewhere; f_01176↔77 have 2,353 inliers.
    - In the final model f_01176 and f_01177 share only **70** 3D points, all far: median depth 65 SfM units (≈ 140 m), baseline/depth 0.04.
    - Piece 2 sits 2.45 units (≈ 5 m) **behind** f_01176 along its optical axis (cos −0.97).
    - Reading: global positioning landed piece 2 offset along the forward axis. The near tracks bridging the cut were then filtered as outliers ("Filtered 32,823 tracks by angle error"), leaving only far points, which cannot see a forward offset, so BA could not pull the piece back.
    - Why April and not December: not tested.
    - Seeded alternative, if a single connected solve is wanted: LO poses → colmap point_triangulator → bundle_adjuster (fixed camera).
  - The December warp on it: residual p90 1.00 m, yaw corrections up to 160°. Rejected.
- **Warp v2: `tenrows_lane_warp.py --split-jumps 0.5`** (frozen copy `logs/tenrows_lane_warp_v2.py`). Cut wherever a consecutive SfM step (at the global scale) disagrees with the LO step by more than 0.5 m, then warp each piece on its own (Sim(3) + LOESS + windowed yaw inside the piece).
  - Regression check: December lane 2, flag off and flag on, identical to the original output (max |Δ| 0.0, no jumps found).
  - Each piece fits the LO on its own: rigid p50 8.2 / 2.1 / 4.3 cm; camera orientation vs LO 2.1° median (December's extrinsic offset).
- **Lane window: `apr_lane_lo.py window`** (December's tenrows_lane_windows.py rule on the lane LO).
  - The keyframe window kf 193–348 starts in the ~100° turn into the lane (heading −31° → +66°, frames 862–962).
  - In the turn the warp's position-based yaw was off by up to 7.6°; LO, export and SfM-rigid headings agree there to within 0–2.3°.
  - December's rule (straight core + 1.5 s pads) dropped only 9 frames. Its start pad is a ~40° pivot in 1.5 s, exactly the frames the warp got wrong.
  - So `PAD_START_S=0`: **582 frames f_00893..f_01474** (31 left out at the start, 0 at the end).
- **Base, init and placement rebuilt on the window:**
  - LO base: 582 frames, 36.6 m.
  - LiDAR init: 1,380,124 points, 36 % coloured.
  - Warp v2: cuts at the same two frames, residual p50 1.6 cm / p90 3.9 cm (December 3.7 / 8.2), yaw correction median 0.09°, max 3.78° (December 0.14 / 4.18).
- **Check, warped vs LO camera heading** (deviation from the median offset):

  | lane | p50 | p90 | p99 | max | frames > 2° |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | April | 0.13° | 0.83° | 2.14° | 3.16° | 7 (f_00898–904, the end of the turn) |
  | December lane 2 (the recipe of record) | 0.24° | 0.98° | 2.93° | 3.43° | 21 |

  My first pass condition ("every frame within ~2°") was a guess. December's own lane fails it, and April is at or below December on every statistic.

  - Spacing-matched (recommended): `apr_lane_prep.py --resample-cm 9.5`, arc-length resampling to December's per-frame spacing, then E2 → 492 frames → **196 training (~19 cm) + 50 held-out**. December: 562 → 224 + 57.
  - Raw stream halved: 352 training (~11 cm) + 89 held-out, i.e. 1.6× December's view density. December's own density ablation moved the result a lot: all frames 25.73 / 21.54 vs every 2nd 29.72 / 19.82.
  - The resampler was dry-tested on the first April lane (582 → 369 frames at 9.5 cm along 35.0 m). `apr_lane_run.sh` v2 (logs copy `apr_lane_run_v2.sh`; the running chain uses the v1 copy, left untouched) takes `LANE_PROJ` / `PREP_EXTRA`.
- **Full-lane chain result (03:31 box):**
  - SfM 5,265 s: 881/881 registered, 492,355 points, reprojection 0.58 px.
  - Split again: cuts before f_01176 and f_01239, pieces of 283 / 63 / 535 frames, piece scales 1.74 / 1.624 / 5.92.
  - Warp v2: p90 4.0 cm. Heading check: p99 2.52, max 3.24°.
  - The automatic pass condition (heading + warp residual vs December) passed, and training started into `h3dgs_e2s` (Paul's "A": 493 resampled frames → 197 training + 50 held-out).
- **Stopped at ~300 iterations (03:32 box):** the check also printed orientation vs LO **max 50.53°**.
  - Cause: the whole middle piece (f_01176–01238, 63 frames, 3.7 m) is rolled 50.4° about the track. A position-only Sim(3) of a short near-straight piece cannot see roll about its own axis.
  - My heading check is blind to it too: the optical axis stays put. 16 of its frames were training views. The killed log is kept as `..._e2s_train_stopped50deg.log`.
  - Lesson: the pass condition must include orientation vs LO (December max 4.06°), not heading only.
- **Warp v3: `--roll-from-lo 10`** (frozen `logs/tenrows_lane_warp_v3.py`, `logs/apr_lane_run_v3.sh`).
  - Per piece, take the twist about the piece's track axis of the mean rotation placed → LO cameras; apply it when it exceeds 10° (the LO cameras carry the 2–4° extrinsic, so 10° sits well above it), keeping the centroid.
  - Regression: December lane 2 and the first April lane are byte-identical with it on (no piece triggers).
  - April full lane: roll fixed on 1 piece (50.3°).
- **Check after the fix**, April (December): heading dev p50 0.15 / p90 1.26 / p99 2.53 / max 3.25° (0.24 / 0.98 / 2.93 / 3.43); orientation vs LO median 1.75 / p90 2.19 / max 3.34° (2.10 / 2.55 / 4.06); position p50 1.7 / p90 4.0 cm (3.7 / 8.2).
- **Training relaunched 03:33 box** (05:33 SAST): `LANE_PROJ=h3dgs_e2s PREP_EXTRA="--resample-cm 9.5" apr_lane_run_v3.sh lane2_apr_full train hierarchy post eval`; 1.51 M initial gaussians, 14 it/s at the start, sharing the GPU with the Citrus B stage service.
- **RESULT (09:16 box = 11:16 SAST), April lane 2 E2, spacing-matched (`lane2_apr_full/h3dgs_e2s`):**
  - Run: train 14,796 s (8.07 M gaussians, budget reached at iteration 11,400); hierarchy 1.95 GB; post-opt 5,634 s.

    | | training views (60) | held-out |
    |---|---|---|
    | April, tau 0 | median **32.21** (mean 31.89) | median **16.59** (mean 16.75), 50 views |
    | April, tau 3 | 31.59 | 16.60 |
    | December E2, tau 0 | 29.72 | 19.82 |

  - So April fits its training views +2.5 dB better and generalises −3.2 dB worse. The train/held-out gap is 15.6 dB vs December's 9.9.
  - Held-out along the lane, medians by fifth: April 16.5 / 17.2 / 16.4 / 16.2 / 18.8; December 20.0 / 21.5 / 20.8 / 17.7 / 18.1. The deficit is lane-wide, not the roll-fixed piece: f_01177–1225 score 15.0–17.1, like their neighbours.
  - April min 12.6 at f_00893, the first window frame.
  - Cause not measured. Candidates: open-sky lighting and auto-exposure between views (E2 trains with the exposure model off), moving clouds, denser foliage.
  - Decisive next test: held-out PSNR after a per-view affine colour fit to the photo. A big jump → exposure/lighting; none → geometry generalisation.
- **Relevancy on the April lane model** (Paul: "we have a survey embedder, we would just be creating supervision and doing the second stage … I don't see how this is different from what we do regularly"). He is right: it is the regular H3DGS native-identity chain, `automation/tenrows/apr_lane_native.sh` + `apr_lane_native_prep.py`.
  - The survey keyframes are frames of the lane stream: all 213 keyframes kf 190–402 match a stream frame by exact stamp (`kf_map.json`).

  - Second stage on H3DGS = the census seed (memory: a live stage-2 refine lowers containment). Flags: tree floor 1.0, bg competes, ratio 2.
  - Census: 43 s (12.2 M nodes, 50 labels incl. bg). Then seed, text bank, render and containment verdicts on 29 keyframes (21 held out + 8 census).
- **RESULT, relevancy on the April lane model** (11:32 box; whole chain 3 min: census 43 s, seed 25 s, render + score 141 s).
  - Seed: 131,521 of 12.2 M gaussians (1.1 %) carry a tree. The supervision labels only registry trees: median 5.0 % of pixels, 4 trees per frame; 24 of 204 keyframes have no labels.
  - Containment (containment_eval, best_mask sweep, GT = compiled supervision), 29 keyframes:

    | head | held out of the census (21 frames) | census frames (8) |
    |---|---|---|
    | TREE | 77 heads, mean **0.616**, median 0.644 | 28 heads, mean 0.695, median 0.748 |
    | ROW | 36 heads, mean 0.537 | 14 heads, mean 0.491 |

  - **Head to head on the April blocks' own verdict frames** (lio_row100 `verdicts_censusinit_fw2.json`, the per-block HiGH fleet; same trees, same scorer; `cell_trees/vs_blocks/`). Tree IoU, blocks → lane model:

    - kf_000308 (held out): sabine 0.061 → 0.606; tobago 0.041 → 0.748; counterparts 0.53 → 0.853; ridley 0.357 → 0.794; pacers 0.066 → 0.583.
    - kf_000349 (in the census): emoji 0.393 → 0.807; cochran 0.492 → 0.940; haskell 0.052 → 0.930; undermine 0.152 → 0.737; clones 0.023 → 0.607.

## 2026-10-06 — Scope: SAM3 fine-tuning for oranges on citrus 04/05 (Paul: "Demo done, good push. Scope out sam3 finetuning for oranges in 04/05")
- **Why.** Stock SAM3 under the canonical fruit recipe (in-tree bbox crop, pad 24, 2.0× upscale, prompt 'fruit', parent gate 0.5) misses visible oranges:
  - 05 kf_003284: 1 fruit masked, ~55 orange blobs visible.
  - kf_003282: low, bright oranges missed.
  - The 08-28 audit exhausted the image-side levers: crop A/B/C, prompt ensemble (every alternative a strict subset of 'fruit'), CLAHE, more upscale. Threshold lowering was ruled out by Paul (FP risk). COCO YOLOv8x 'orange' finds 0; CitDet has no weights and is CC BY-NC-SA.
  - Fine-tuning is the remaining image-side lever.
- **Not measured yet:** SAM3's per-fruit recall and precision on oranges. No exhaustive orange ground truth exists for 04/05; the ledgers are SAM3's own output.
- **Tooling (checked):**
  - `code/sam3` has `sam3/train/train.py` (Hydra). Configs include `roboflow_v100_full_ft_100_images.yaml` (full fine-tune on 100 images, COCO-style data, resolution 1008, batch 1, 20 epochs, lr transformer 8e-5 / vision 2.5e-5 / language 5e-6) and ODinW 10-shot text-only.
  - The SAM3 pixi env (torch 2.10 cu128, sam3 0.1.0, pycocotools) lacks `hydra` and `submitit`: `pip install -e ".[train]"` first.
  - Checkpoint in the HF cache (3.3 GB).
  - Licence: SAM License, which allows modification and derivative works. Fine-tuned weights are derivative works and stay under the SAM License (cannot be relicensed; see the open-source memory).
- **Data:**
  - 04/05 keyframes (~3,400 each, `prod/scratch_sam3`) and 10 fps native streams (~7,600 frames each).
  - SAM3 fruit clips (`prod/bateleur/sam3_fruit`: 04 48 clips, 05 43) and `trees_only` parent masks.
  - 3D-corroborated fruit: n_fruit3d 04 = 108 on 17 trees, 05 = 154 on 20 trees.
- **Plan (each phase has its decisive test):**
  0. **Eval set + baseline.** ~50 frames over 04 and 05: the known problem frames plus a spread of lighting and distance, every visible orange masked. Annotation is SAM3-assisted (stock detections pre-filled, human adds misses and deletes FPs). Score stock SAM3 per fruit (mask IoU ≥ 0.5): recall and precision overall and by fruit size and position (low/bright vs canopy). Decision point (Paul): is the miss rate worth fine-tuning?
  1. **Training labels:** ~100 frames (the repo's 100-image regime), same protocol, disjoint from eval. Split by tree or row so neighbouring views do not leak, and hold one survey out (train 04 / test 05 and the reverse) for generalisation. Option to cut labelling: project the 3D-corroborated fruit as box prompts to propose misses (still human-verified).
  2. **Fine-tune:** install the train extras. First a 1-iteration dry run to MEASURE A100 memory and step time (not estimated here). Then full fine-tune from the 100-image config with our COCO json; a frozen-backbone variant if memory is tight.
  3. **Test:** eval-set recall and precision vs stock at the same score cut-off; the cross-survey split. The fine-tuned model is used only for the fruit prompt; stock SAM3 keeps trees.
  4. **Downstream:** rerun 04/05 fruit masks → fruit3d corroborated counts and native fruit containment, compared with today's.
- **Dependencies:** annotation time for ~150 frames (the bottleneck; not measured); the A100 (the Citrus B stage service still holds 12 GB); disk is fine.
- **Scope revised** (Paul: "Sam3 in the loop, think few shot rather than full finetuning" / "Nothing hand labeled").
  - No annotation anywhere, including evaluation. Labels and scoring come from multi-view consistency at the native 10 fps (kf ⊂ native, exact stamps; 04 7,692 frames, 05 7,592).
  - **Automatic labels:**
    - (a) SAM3 video mode (detect + track; `examples/sam3_video_predictor_example.ipynb`) on 10 fps clips: a track's propagated masks in frames where the per-frame detector missed are candidate misses.
    - (b) A 3D check: triangulate each track (LIO poses at native stamps; splat-rendered depth or bearing-only rays, because ZED depth was empty on 76 % of fruit detections in the 08-28 pilot). Keep static, ≥ 2-viewpoint oranges; their projections are the per-frame pseudo-labels, and the 3D check drops tracker drift.
  - **Few-shot, cheapest first:**
    - (A) No training: SAM3's in-image exemplars. `Sam3Processor.add_geometric_prompt(box, True)` re-runs grounding for "fruit" conditioned on positive boxes. Give each frame K exemplars from its pseudo-labels and test K = 1 / 3 / 5.
    - (B) The repo's ODinW 10-shot recipe (`odinw_text_only_train.yaml`: shot10, nothing frozen, lr ×0.1, 40 data epochs × 1,500 samples) on K = 10 pseudo-labelled frames, then 20 / 40 as the loop grows. It needs `hydra` + `submitit` in the SAM3 env; a dry run measures time and memory.
  - **Loop:** better detector → more tracks and 3D oranges → better pseudo-labels → next round.
  - **Label-free decisive test**, on held-out trees and the other survey:
    - (1) Per-view recall against 3D oranges built from the OTHER views (leave-one-view-out, visibility by rendered depth).
    - (2) Precision proxy: the share of detections corroborated in 3D by other views.
    - (3) Downstream: fruit3d counts per tree, 04↔05 cross-epoch stability, native fruit containment.
    - Baseline: stock canonical recipe.
  - **Pilot first:** 05 tree 72, the 08-28 pilot (68 native frames; 18 corroborated fruit at 10 fps vs 7–10 at keyframes). Run tracking-propagated misses → 3D check → exemplars (A), scored with test (1)/(2).
  - Not measured: video-tracker speed and memory at this resolution; how much of the propagation is real misses vs drift.

## 2026-10-06 — SAM3 few-shot pilot, citrus 05 tree 72, no hand labels (Paul: "have a go and see where we get to")
- **Goal:** can multi-view consistency on the 10 fps clip stand in for labels, and does SAM3-in-the-loop (exemplar prompting) recover what the per-frame recipe misses?
- **Data:** `fruit3d_t72` (08-28 pilot): 68 native frames in two sighting windows (36 + 32; baselines 4.2 / 4.0 m), LIO pose per stamp, tree masks, the canonical stock detections (534 events, scores ≥ 0.5, median 0.63, area median 91 px).
- **Script:** `automation/sam3_fewshot/pilot_t72.py` (sam3 pixi env) stages track / tri / score / exemplar; `pilot_sheet.py` check sheet.
- **Tracking (SAM3 video, text 'fruit', per window on a fixed-size crop following the tree centroid, 2×):**
  - At the builder's hard-coded thresholds (new object 0.7, detection 0.5): 24 tracks, median 2 objects per frame vs stock 7; only 131 of the 534 stock detections score ≥ 0.7. 41 s, 6.4 GiB.
  - At stock-level thresholds (`pred.model.new_det_thresh = 0.5`, detection 0.5; set on the in-process model): **62 tracks**, 8 objects per frame, 457/534 stock detections carried by a track; 47 s, 7.5 GiB.
- **3D check (bearing-only triangulation of each track's centroids):**
  - LIO poses at native stamps: reprojection median 12 px (fruit are 10–17 px across), too loose.
  - Trained keyframe poses (h3dgs_expo aligned export) × LIO increment to the native frame: no better (14 px).
  - Keyframe views only with trained poses: bimodal, p25 3.8 / p50 10.4 / p75 18.9 px. So the native-frame pose interpolation is the weak link (timing or LIO jitter; not isolated), and about half the tracks drift.
  - Rule adopted: confirmed = track with ≥ 3 keyframe views, median reprojection ≤ 6 px, parallax ≥ 2°, depth 0.5–12 m, within 6 m of the tree. **13 confirmed oranges, 165 views** (78 keyframe, 87 native).
- **Stock recall on confirmed orange-views: 0.82** (keyframes 0.78, native 0.85); no confirmed orange is missed in every view; 26 of the 30 misses are mid-track, not at track ends. Precision proxy not meaningful (most detections are on oranges the 3D check never confirmed).
  - Check sheet `fewshot/misses_sheet.jpg`: the red rings sit on visible oranges. Frames nat_002446–48 have 0 stock detections: see the mask check below.
- **Exemplar prompting (image model, canonical crop, text 'fruit' + K positive boxes of confirmed oranges the text-only pass already found; recall on the frame's OTHER confirmed views):**

  | K | frames | test views | text-only recall | + K exemplars | detections/frame | not on a confirmed orange |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1 | 28 | 108 | 0.870 | **0.944** | 11.0 → 12.9 | 6.2 → 7.9 |
  | 3 | 20 | 53 | 0.811 | **0.887** | 13.2 → 11.8 | 7.5 → 6.3 |
  | 5 | 9 | 17 | 0.588 | **0.765** | 15.7 → 14.1 | 9.1 → 7.7 |

  K=1: 8 frames gained a missed orange, 0 lost one. The extra unmatched detections at K=1 are unscored (unconfirmed oranges or FPs).
- **Caveats:** the confirmed set only contains oranges SAM3 detected somewhere (tracker = same detector + memory); oranges never detected by any pass are invisible to this evaluation. One tree.

**Whole cabbage clip (Paul: "re-segment and reconstruct the whole cabbage video, is gpu free").** The GPU is not free: the 05 retrain's post-opt holds 32.6 GB until ~17:10. IMG_7993: 126 frames at 6 fps (21 s). The prep kept one segment, [54, 125], and dropped frames 0–53 as a "turn": consecutive-pair inliers fell below max(300, 0.35 × median 2120) = 742, and min segment is 60. The clip's SIFT database had been reclaimed (a 0-byte file my sqlite check created was removed). `automation/image_farm/cabbage_whole_clip_stageA.sh` re-runs SIFT + exhaustive matching on the CPU (16:30) to profile the opening. Next: a whole-clip segment dir `IMG_7993_sall` (not s0), GLOMAP + the track gate, then H3DGS at the 8 M recipe once the GPU is free, then SAM3 on the 54 new frames, undistorted maps, the v3g embedder recipe, and a re-census.

**Paul (02:2x SAST): "Which tree is in front of me? Show me." → "The context does not provide information about which tree is in front of you."** Cause: his page ran CACHED app code — the launch app's files under /app had no cache-control (server.py set no-store only for /viewer and /tassili), and Babel re-fetches the JSX from the HTTP cache, so every fix tonight needed a hard reload. Fixed: /app is no-store too (aru_sil_core/src eeeab51f, pushed; :8011 restarted). On a fresh page the position payload is populated once the registry and path have loaded (e.g. Citrus B at 9 m: tree 2 ahead-left 2.9 m, tree 5 ahead-right 3.7 m …). Also: position questions are only answerable on surveys with a registry — gwakungu-chilli has none, so "in front of me" there is honestly unanswerable. Paul is driving the stage himself now (chilli staged at 02:3x); no more re-stages from me.
**Paul (03:xx SAST): "the cuts for chillies and cabbages are vastly superior to the live view — why not the same cropping? it looks tiled; walks on phone videos should be much slower."** Three causes found, all fixed: (1) the live viewer's camera was ALWAYS 75° vertical (THREE default in main.js) — my per-survey FOV override set `pose.fov`, which never reached the camera, and the catalogue's `scene` block is not even sent to the app (launch_api PUBLIC_SURVEY_FIELDS). Tracking rerun with `TRACK_MASKS=1` for those 12 trees. Train extras installed in the sam3 env.

Patched in `code/sam3/sam3/perflib/fused.py`: with grad enabled, run the plain `act(linear(x))`.

  - **Second trap:** `build_sam3_image_model(checkpoint_path=<trainer ckpt>)` loads NOTHING (its loader keeps only `detector.*` keys, the video layout) and leaves random weights → 0 detections. The `ckpt` stage now loads the trainer's `model` dict directly onto the stock model.
  - **10-shot run launched 03:14 box** (`citrus_fruit_10shot.yaml`: 10 images, 40 epochs; an epoch = 10 steps, so 400 steps; loss 102 → 39 by epoch 3; ~17 GB).
- **Dry-run model (10 steps on 10 pseudo-labelled crops) on held-out tree 72, same `ckpt` path:** recall on confirmed views **0.818 → 0.964**; detections per frame 9.0 → 12.0; off confirmed oranges 6.0 → 8.4, of which on a tracker object 4.7 → 5.2 and **on nothing 1.3 → 3.1**. Sheet `fewshot/ckpt_dryrun60_sheet.jpg`: the extras are a mix of dim/fallen oranges and doubtful spots; not resolvable per frame without labels.

The trainer checkpoint's keys match the video detector's.
- **LOOP TEST, dry-run detector (10 steps) inside the tracker, held-out tree 72** (`fewshot_dryrun60/`): 77 tracks (stock detector 62); 3D-confirmed oranges **13 → 23** at ≤ 6 px (7 → 13 at ≤ 4 px), 304 confirmed views; 3 of the 23 were never found by the stock per-frame recipe; stock recall on this larger confirmed set 0.674. The extra detections include real, static, multi-view-consistent oranges, not only drift.
- **10-shot run done (03:30 box, 15 min):** 40 epochs × 10 steps; loss 86 → 26 (epoch 8) → 36 (epoch 32); val COCO bbox AP on the confirmed-only GT 0.33 / AP50 0.50 (an ambiguous number here: unlabelled real oranges score as false positives). Checkpoint `logs/sam3_fewshot_train/10shot/checkpoints/checkpoint.pt` (10 GB with optimiser state).
- **10-shot (400 steps) vs stock vs the 10-step dry-run model, held-out trees (03:40 box):**

  | | tree 72: per-frame recall / off-confirmed per frame (on nothing) | tree 72: 3D-confirmed oranges ≤ 6 px (≤ 4 px) | tree 5: recall / off-confirmed (on nothing) | tree 5: confirmed ≤ 6 px (≤ 4 px) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | stock | 0.818 / 6.0 (1.3) | 13 (7) | 0.929 / 7.7 (1.1) | 50 (36) |
  | 10 steps (dry run) | 0.964 / 8.4 (3.1) | **23 (13)** | not run | 50 (27) |
  | 400 steps (10-shot) | 0.824 / 6.2 (1.8) | 14 (9) | **0.770** / 5.4 (0.6) | 42 (31) |

  - The 400-step model went conservative: fewer detections per frame (tree 5: 17.6 → 13.4), recall on tree 5 down 16 points, fewer confirmed oranges there (50 → 42); tree 72 unchanged. Reading (not isolated): pseudo-labels cover ~5.5 objects per crop against 7–17 detections per frame, so unlabelled real fruit is trained as background; the loss also rose after epoch 8 (26 → 36).
  - The 10-step model is the one that found more real fruit (tree 72: 23 confirmed, 3 never seen by stock) at the price of more unsupported detections (3.1 per frame on nothing).
  - But on tree 5 the 10-step model adds nothing: 50 confirmed at ≤ 6 px either way, and FEWER at ≤ 4 px (36 → 27). The tree-72 gain does not repeat; tree 5 is the larger sample (792 views).
- **Next:** schedule sweep at 2 / 4 / 8 epochs (20 / 40 / 80 steps) with the same data, each scored both ways on trees 5 and 72, to find where the gain peaks before the suppression sets in. Then label completeness (tracks ∪ stock detections) if the sweep says the labels are the limit.
- **Schedule sweep (03:41–03:58 box; 10 images; same scoring), confirmed oranges at ≤ 6 px and per-frame recall on the stock-confirmed views:**

  | steps | tree 72 recall · confirmed | tree 5 recall · confirmed |
  |---|---|---|
  | 0 (stock) | 0.818 · 13 | 0.929 · 50 |
  | 10 | 0.964 · 23 | – · 50 |
  | 40 | 0.897 · 16 | 0.813 · 42 |
  | 80 | 0.873 · 15 | 0.760 · 39 |
  | 400 | 0.824 · 14 | 0.770 · 42 |

  - Monotone on tree 5: every tens of steps on these pseudo-labels lowers recall and the confirmed count; tree 72's early gain fades by 40 steps. The 2-epoch run saved no checkpoint (see below).
  - Verdict: as built, few-shot training on tracker pseudo-labels degrades the detector on the larger held-out tree from the first tens of steps. Consistent with incomplete labels (unlabelled fruit trained as background), not yet isolated.
  - **Isolating test (running):** labels = tracker masks ∪ stock per-frame detections (stock SAM3 rerun on the 376 training crops, tree-mask gated, added where no label sits within 12 px), 4 epochs, same scoring.
  - The 2-epoch run died with a ZeroDivisionError in the trainer (20 total steps against the recipe's 20-step warm-up / 20-step cool-down schedule); not pursued.
- **LABEL COMPLETENESS CONFIRMED as the main driver (04:09 box).** Union labels (2,059 tracker + 536 stock detections on the same crops, +26 %), 4 epochs, same scoring:

  | 40 steps | tree 72 recall · confirmed | tree 5 recall · confirmed |
  |---|---|---|
  | tracker labels only | 0.897 · 16 | 0.813 · 42 |
  | tracker ∪ stock labels | **0.921 · 18** | **0.899 · 43** |
  | stock (0 steps) | 0.818 · 13 | 0.929 · 50 |

  - Tree 5's loss shrank from −11.6 to −3.0 recall points at the same steps and detections per frame (17.2 vs stock 17.6); tree 72 kept its gain (2 confirmed oranges never found by stock). Still below stock on tree 5 (0.899 / 43 vs 0.929 / 50), i.e. the remaining label gaps still cost.
  - Next two tests (queued): (a) union labels + 40 training images in 1 epoch (40 steps, more label diversity); (b) union labels + frozen vision backbone (less plasticity for the gaps to act on).
- **Test (a), union labels + 40 training images, 1 epoch = 40 steps (`union40_ep1`, 04:18 box): first model above stock on BOTH held-out trees, both metrics.**

  | | tree 72 recall · confirmed (never seen by stock) · on nothing/frame | tree 5 recall · confirmed (never seen) · on nothing/frame |
  |---|---|---|
  | stock | 0.818 · 13 · 1.3 | 0.929 · 50 · 1.1 |
  | union40_ep1 | **0.945 · 22 (5)** · 6.0 | **0.944 · 58 (2)** · 3.6 |

  - Detections per frame 9.0 → 14.8 (tree 72) and 17.6 → 20.8 (tree 5). The 3D-confirmed count is the metric that cannot be inflated by drift or false positives: +69 % / +16 %. The cost is 2.5–4.7 more detections per frame that no multi-view evidence supports (false positives or fruit the tracker never caught; not separable without labels).
  - Queued: the loop over all 45 trees with this detector (survey-level confirmed count vs stock's 92), after the frozen-backbone test.
- **Test (b), union labels + 10 images, 40 steps, vision backbone frozen (`lr_vision_backbone: 0`; `union_frozen_ep4`, 04:27 box):** tree 72 recall 0.964, confirmed **21** (2 never seen by stock), on nothing 4.2/frame; tree 5 recall 0.932, confirmed **56** (1 never seen), on nothing 2.2/frame. Above stock on both trees with fewer unsupported detections than union40_ep1 (tree 5: 2.2 vs 3.6 per frame; tree 72: 4.2 vs 6.0) for one less confirmed orange each. Reading: the transformer/heads carry the gain; the backbone's plasticity mostly adds unsupported detections.
  - Queued after the fleet loop: union labels + 40 images + frozen backbone (both levers).
- **FLEET LOOP, union40_ep1 detector over all 45 trees (05:18 box; 50 min; `fewshot_union40_ep1_summary.json`):**
  - 3D-confirmed oranges **92 → 175**; 50 of the 175 were never found by the stock per-frame recipe in any view; stock recall on the new confirmed set 0.626 (2,020 views).
  - Per tree: 19 trees up, 0 down, 21 trees with at least one confirmed orange (stock 13). Held-out 5 + 72: 63 → 80. The 33 trees in neither the training nor the held-out set: 6 → 30. The 10 training trees: 23 → 65.
  - Precision caveat stands (only measured on 5/72): 3.6 and 6.0 detections per frame on nothing, against stock's 1.1 and 1.3.
- **Test (d), union labels + 40 images + frozen backbone (`union40_frozen_ep1`, 05:26 box):** tree 72 recall 0.970, confirmed **28** (8 never seen by stock), on nothing 7.3/frame; tree 5 recall 0.953, confirmed **62** (6 never seen), on nothing 4.7/frame. The most confirmed oranges of any variant and the most unsupported detections: with 40 images the frozen backbone no longer reduces the extras.
- **All variants, held-out trees (confirmed ≤ 6 px t5 / t72 · on nothing per frame t5 / t72):** stock 50 / 13 · 1.1 / 1.3; union 10 img 43 / 18 · 1.6 / 3.4; union 10 img frozen 56 / 21 · 2.2 / 4.2; union 40 img 58 / 22 · 3.6 / 6.0; union 40 img frozen 62 / 28 · 4.7 / 7.3. One curve: more confirmed oranges come with more unsupported detections.
- **Label-free precision signal: track confirmability** (confirmed oranges / tracks with ≥ 3 keyframe views). Stock 0.49 (t5) / 0.37 (t72); the fine-tuned variants 0.39–0.46 / 0.33–0.38. The extra objects the fine-tuned detectors track triangulate at nearly stock's rate, so at the track level they are about as real as stock's; the per-frame "on nothing" detections (never tracked) stay unresolved.

- **Frames for Paul** (`automation/sam3_fewshot/frames_sheet.py` → `demo_video_v2/sam3_fewshot/frames_union40_ep1.jpg`): per held-out tree, the frame with the most new confirmed-orange hits and the frame with the most "on nothing" detections. Both worst frames (tree 5 nat_007237, tree 72 nat_004910) are frames where **stock SAM3 returned zero detections** on trees with plainly visible fruit, so nothing was ever tracked there and every fine-tuned detection counts as "on nothing" by construction; most of those rings sit on visible oranges. The unsupported-detection caveat is therefore partly an artefact of the scoring (no track where stock saw nothing), not only false positives.

## 2026-10-07 — Relevancy on 04/05 with fruit from the fine-tuned SAM3 (Paul: "can we redo relevancy then on 04/05 including fruit")
- **Goal:** chunk-specific native identity (trees + fruit) on 04 and 05 with fruit supervision from the few-shot fine-tuned detector (`union40_ep1`, the survey-validated one) instead of stock SAM3.
- **Decisive test:** per cell, fruit containment IoU on held-out keyframes (containment_eval, the cell script's own scoring) against the new supervision, beside the old cell's numbers against the old supervision; lit-fruit pixel counts and the tree IoUs (must not move).
- **Chain:** (1) `ledgers_ft.sh` = fruit_chain.sh step 1b per prod block with `fruit_in_trees_ledger.py --ckpt` (added), written to `experimental/sam3_fruit_union40/clip_NNN` (prod ledgers untouched); ~1 min per block measured in August → 05 43 blocks + 04 48 blocks ≈ 1.6 h. (2) `native_sam3_cell.sh` with `FRUIT_GLOB` (added) per chunk → new cells `chunk_<CN>_sam3_ft`: 05 h3dgs_expo 1_0 (tree 5: 78 fruit3d) and 0_0; 04 h3dgs 1_1 (38), 0_0 (46), 1_0 (15), 0_1 (9); 04 has no native cell yet, so this is its first relevancy. (3) score; (4) stage if better.
- Ledgers launched 05:5x box (05 then 04).
- First block (05 b000): fine-tuned ledger 1,038 instances on 76/78 frames vs the stock ledger's 387 on 43/78 (2.7×); the compile step's strict_fruit_tree_v1 filter (per-frame relative score, fill, aspect) decides what enters the maps.
- Old 05 chunk-1_0 cell baseline (B_bg2share vs stock-fruit supervision): FRUIT eval 0.402 (n=2) / train 0.297 (n=3); TREE eval 0.700; ROW eval 0.811.
- Cells queued behind the ledgers: `logs/cells_ft_chain.sh` → `h3dgs_native/chunk_<CN>_sam3_ft` (05: 1_0, 0_0; 04: 1_1, 0_0, 1_0, 0_1).
- **Ledgers done** (05 32 min, 04 34 min). **Cells done 16:37 box** (5–8 min each). 05 chunk 1_0 (`chunk_1_0_sam3_ft`): supervision fruit px 92,125 → 238,948 (2.6×), frames with fruit 89 → 296, fruit ids 7 → 16 (compile: raw 3,554 → kept 2,192; 1,310 dropped as not in the fruit inventory). Trees unchanged: TREE eval 0.699 (old 0.700), ROW 0.810 (0.811).
- **Fruit containment, B seed, against the new supervision: eval 0.131 (n=8) / train 0.140 (n=3)**; old cell against the old supervision 0.402 (n=2) / 0.297 (n=3). On the three heads in both cells: kf_000412 tree 34 0.548 → 0.366 (prec 0.68 → 0.55, rec 0.74 → 0.52); kf_003286 tree 5 0.328 → 0.346; kf_000015 tree 3 0.255 → 0.195. 04 cells (first relevancy there): fruit eval 0.008–0.022, trees scored (tables in each cell).
- The eval sets differ (8 vs 2 fruit heads); cross-scoring (new/old field × new/old supervision on the same 14 frames) run to separate "harder supervision" from "worse field".
- **Cross-scoring, same 14 frames (fruit heads; IoU / precision / recall):** old field vs old supervision 0.377 / 0.55 / 0.55 (n=3); new field vs old supervision 0.262 / 0.40 / 0.45; old field vs NEW supervision 0.087 / 0.17 / 0.59 (n=11); new field vs new supervision 0.133 / 0.23 / 0.41. Trees identical in all four (0.737).
  - So the fine-tuned supervision is a much harder truth (2.6× the fruit pixels, many small fruit), and on it the new field is better than the old (0.133 vs 0.087); on the sparse old truth the new field is worse (0.262 vs 0.377). Precision 0.17–0.23 against the dense truth: the field lights 4–6× the labelled fruit area, the seed-B share rule's leaf lighting (memory: fruit-seed-share-rule-fp) scaled up by the denser supervision.
  - Verdict: fruit relevancy on H3DGS is limited by the seed/field (share rule, 32-d census seed at tau 3), not by the detector; a better detector exposes that. Trees unaffected. Not staged.
- **Paul: "This doesn't track though. Has supervision gotten worse?" — measured (`automation/sam3_fewshot/supervision_check.py`, 55 chunk-1_0 keyframes carrying 3D-confirmed oranges; references = oranges confirmed by the stock loop and by the fine-tuned loop):**

  | compiled fruit supervision | components/frame | median area | on a confirmed orange (stock ref / fine-tuned ref) | recall of confirmed oranges (stock set / fine-tuned set) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | old (stock SAM3) | 10.0 | 94 px | **0.45 / 0.46** | 0.59 / 0.51 |
  | new (fine-tuned) | 14.9 | 85 px | **0.31 / 0.36** | 0.60 / 0.59 |

  - Yes: the new supervision is noisier. It adds ~5 components per frame, about two thirds of which sit on no confirmed orange, for +0.08 recall on the fine-tuned-confirmed set and none on the stock set. (Both references are incomplete, so "on a confirmed orange" is a lower bound on precision; the relative drop is the finding.)
  - Correction: fruit3d's `is_kf` flags every native frame within ~300 ms of a keyframe (542 flagged over 45 trees, 316 exact); the check now maps only exact keyframes (kf_dt_ms 0). Numbers after the fix: old 0.45 / 0.46 on a confirmed orange, recall 0.61 / 0.55; new 0.31 / 0.36, recall 0.62 / 0.63. Same reading. (The pilot's "keyframe-only" triangulation also used the flagged frames, with the trained pose composed with the LIO increment; sub-keyframe spacing, not revisited.)
  - Both supervisions miss ~40 % of the confirmed oranges and only 36–46 % of their components sit on one; the compile step keeps nearly every ledger instance (kf_000022: 33 instances → 31 components), so the gap is in the ledger crops/detector, not the compiler.
  - **Amplification in the seed:** fruit gaussians by argmax 1,091 → 3,530 and by the 0.1 share rule 13,478 → 28,352 (old → new cell, same hierarchy). A 50 % noisier supervision became a 2.1× larger fruit seed, which is the leaf lighting that drops containment more than the supervision degraded.
  - **Answer to Paul:** yes, the supervision got noisier (precision proxy 0.45 → 0.31 for +0.08 recall of confirmed oranges), and the share-rule seed amplified it. Sheet `demo_video_v2/sam3_fewshot/supervision_old_vs_new.jpg`.
- **Paul: "where do the FPs/FNs come from — from a gaussian supervision perspective this should be almost binary." Measured on the census (`W.npz`) and on ONE training view (`automation/sam3_fewshot/view_gaussian_audit.py`, kf_000025, tree 5's fruit; field vs its own supervision there: IoU 0.46, prec 0.60, rec 0.66 in both cells):**
  - Census-wide: gaussians with any fruit weight 98k (old) / 225k (new); fruit share ≥ 0.9 on only 487 / 1,130 of them, carrying 1 % of all fruit weight; 41–46 % of the fruit weight sits on gaussians with share 0.1–0.5. Not binary.
  - On the view, FN side: the fruit pixels' alpha weight comes 67 % from fruit-seeded gaussians, 28 % from tree-seeded (can never light), 5 % unassigned. The 8.5–10k gaussians that touch fruit pixels are mostly leaf gaussians even within this single view (in-view fruit share median 0.18–0.19; 65 % below 0.5).
  - FP side: the fruit-seeded gaussians visible in the view land 24 % of their weight on fruit pixels, 50 % on tree pixels, 26 % unlabelled → lit as fruit, they cover twice as much leaf as fruit; 96 % of that leaf weight comes from gaussians the 0.1 share rule promoted (global share 0.1–0.5).
  - Old and new cells give the same picture on this view (same frame scored 0.464 / 0.458). So the FPs/FNs are the primitive: a tau-3 gaussian straddles orange and leaves (orange 10–17 px; same gaussians render both), so every seed decision is a threshold on a mixed share, and the share rule puts the mixed gaussians on the fruit side. Remedy is fruit-sized gaussians (HiGH fruit densification measured 0.027 → 0.30 on the side-car; `h3dgs_expo_fruitd` exists untrained) or a seed rule on in-view share, not the detector.
  - **Correction (Paul: "what do you mean the h3dgs was never trained, so what's the densification coming from"):** the "0.027 → 0.30" I cited is the SIDE-CAR's fruit v3 (10-01, nerfstudio HiGH stage 2 on the chunk's exported gaussians), and 0.30 was one verdict frame with a fitted threshold; over the 27 clip frames the densified side-car averaged 0.198 (held-out 0.175) against the native chunk's 0.197 / best-containment 0.259 (10-05 entry). So densification as built there did NOT lift held-out fruit. The native chunk's 17.7 M gaussians come from colour training only; the native fruit-densify fine-tune (`train_single_fruit.py`, project `h3dgs_expo_fruitd`) ran to it ~2,100 (12.3 → 13.9 M gaussians, ~40 k fruit splits per 300 steps) on 10-04 and was stopped by Paul ("retrain first before densification"); no checkpoint. Fruit-sized gaussians remain a hypothesis supported by today's audit (mixed gaussians), not a measured result.

## 2026-10-07 — Native fruit densification of 05 chunk 1_0 (Paul: "we deprecated the side car … densify the native")
- **Goal:** fruit-sized gaussians in the native chunk (the audit above: the same tau-3 gaussians render orange and leaf). Recipe = the 10-04 chain `automation/h3dgs/fruit_densify/fruit_densify_05.sh` unchanged: fine-tune the trained chunk's leaves 6,000 its with the fruit term (boost 5, split carriers with max scale 4 cm–0.5 m, budget 15 M) on the STOCK supervision of `chunk_1_0_sam3` (the same labels as the 10-04 baseline, so the test isolates gaussian size from the detector), then hierarchy, post-opt 15k, native cell `chunk_1_0_sam3_fruitd`, `fruit_bc_score.py`.
- **Decisive test:** tree 5's fruit by best containment on the fruit clip frames vs the baseline 0.251 (held-out 0.161 / trained 0.355), PSNR not lower than 20.5 on those frames.
- Launched 17:xx box; the 10-04 partial log kept as `fruit_ft_stopped_1004.log`.
- **Fine-tune done (19:38 box, 1,015 s):** 12.26 M → **15.16 M gaussians** (budget 15 M; fruit splits every step, 16 k even at it 6000 over budget), loss 0.037; hierarchy built in 285 s (3.3 GB). **Post-opt OOM** at the first backward (needs ~3.97 GB more than the 2.5 GB free: 15 M gaussians + the stage's render service holding 6.1 GB). The 12.3 M chunk's own post-opt fitted; +23 % gaussians tipped it.
- **Post-opt attempt 2 (19:46–19:51 box, after stopping the orphaned demo render service, 6.1 GB, API already "idle", no clients):** died at it 960 with `cudaErrorMemoryAllocation` inside `expand_to_size` (the hierarchy cut's own allocation) at a torch peak of 39.1 GB: the densified hierarchy has 22.2 M nodes (chunk: 17.7 M). Patch (opt-in, `H3DGS_POST_EMPTY_CACHE=1` in `train_post.py`): `torch.cuda.empty_cache()` before the cut, so the extension can take torch's freed blocks. Attempt 3 launched 21:4x box. (Two hours lost to my own watchers: a 10-04 line in the appended log, then a pattern that only matched hour 2x; watchers now read only lines added after launch.)
- **RESULT, native fruit densification (00:32 box):** post-opt 9,420 s (with the cache flush); cell `chunk_1_0_sam3_fruitd` seeded 1.70 M of 22.2 M nodes. `fruit_bc_score.py` on the 28 clip frames with SAM3 fruit (same scorer, same stock labels as the baseline):

  | | all (28) | held-out (15) | trained (13) | PSNR all / trained / held-out |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | baseline native (10-04) | 0.251 | 0.161 | 0.355 | 20.3 / 23.8 / 17.7 |
  | fruit-densified native | **0.232** | **0.160** | **0.315** | 20.4 / 24.0 / 17.6 |

  - No gain: held-out flat, trained views −0.04; PSNR unchanged. Splitting carriers down to 4 cm did not make the field's fruit cleaner at the serving cut (tau 3).
- **Why no gain — the cut:** at the serving cut (tau 3) the densified hierarchy's node mix is unchanged: fruit share ≥ 0.9 nodes carry 2 % of the fruit weight (baseline 1 %), 0.1–0.5 nodes 45 % (46 %), < 0.1 nodes 39 % (40 %); single-view audit kf_000025: fruit-seeded gaussians land 0.21 / 0.53 / 0.26 on fruit / tree / unlabelled (baseline 0.24 / 0.50 / 0.26). The fruit-sized leaves exist (15.2 M gaussians, 16 k fruit splits per step at the end) but the census and the renderer read the hierarchy at tau 3, where interior nodes merge them back with leaf gaussians. Tau-0 (leaf-level) census of the densified cell running to test this.
- **Tau-0 census of the densified chunk (00:40 box): the leaves are NOT more binary either.** Fruit weight by node fruit share, tau 3 → tau 0: ≥ 0.9: 0.02 → 0.02; 0.5–0.9: 0.14 → 0.13; 0.1–0.5: 0.45 → 0.46; < 0.1: 0.39 → 0.39 (75,671 leaves with fruit weight). So the split children straddle orange and leaf exactly like their parents; the fruit term as built (split carriers with max scale 4 cm–0.5 m, boost ×5) makes smaller mixed gaussians, not fruit-only ones. The tau-0 seed + score (densified vs baseline, both at the leaves) finishes the test; the mix predicts no gain.
- **Tau-0 scores (00:44 box), fruit by best containment, 28 clip frames (all / held-out 15 / trained 13):** densified at tau 0 **0.229 / 0.159 / 0.310**; baseline at tau 0 **0.238 / 0.155 / 0.333**; baseline at tau 3 (10-04) 0.251 / 0.161 / 0.355; densified at tau 3 0.232 / 0.160 / 0.315. PSNR 20.4–20.7 everywhere. All four within noise of each other.
- **Verdict:** native fruit densification as built (fruit term ×5, split carriers 4 cm–0.5 m, +2.9 M gaussians) does not improve fruit containment at the cut or at the leaves, because the split gaussians straddle orange and leaf like their parents (share mix unchanged at both levels). Fruit identity through the colour gaussians is limited by the primitive; a separate 3D fruit layer from the confirmed oranges (the loop's triangulated positions) is the route that does not fight it. Code: `train_post.py` opt-in cache flush (`H3DGS_POST_EMPTY_CACHE`), `fruit_bc_score.py` TAU / FEAT env, `supervision_check.py`, `view_gaussian_audit.py`.

## 2026-10-08 — Two tracks (Paul: "Try this approach [CountingFruit-style semantics] and give feedback tomorrow … make h3dgs faster for our use case, tackle that in parallel")
- **A. Fruit field, CountingFruit-style, on the native 05 chunk 1_0:** per-gaussian feature vectors TRAINED with a rendered per-pixel loss (L1 + cosine against class codes: fruit, tree/leaves, with unlabelled pixels ignored) instead of the census assignment; query = dual threshold (close to fruit, far from leaves). First on frozen geometry (the served hierarchy at tau 3), same stock labels, same 28-frame fruit scorer (baseline 0.251 / 0.161 / 0.355) so the result isolates "learned, blending-aware features" from "gaussians that move".
- **B. Speed:** test 1 = post-opt length at the serving cut: 05 chunk 1_0 held-out PSNR at tau 3 for no post-opt / 5k / 15k (`logs/postopt_len_test.sh`, sibling project `h3dgs_expo_post5k`). Launched 01:0x box.
- **A, implementation:** `automation/h3dgs/fruit_field/fruit_field_train.py` (h3dgs env). F[N=17.7 M, D=3] trainable, codes fruit (1,0,0) / tree (0,1,0); per step one of the 294 training views rendered at tau 3, 640×360, through the same gsplat path as native_identity.feature_pass (cut fixed, gather differentiable); loss L1 + (1 − cos) on labelled pixels only; Adam lr 0.02, 3,000 its. Query: dual threshold (cos to fruit > 0.7 ∧ cos to tree < 0.5 ∧ |f| > 0.3) and parameter-free argmax. Score on the 28 clip frames: class-level fruit vs all SAM3 fruit, and fruit-of-tree-5 = fruit mask inside the tree-5 supervision region vs SAM3 10001 (the baseline scorer's quantity). Training: 0.13 s/it, loss 0.34 (it 200) → 0.24 (it 500); run `fruit_field_v1` launched 01:3x box.
- **B, measured memory (from logs):** train_single peaks at 19.5–19.7 GB at the 8 M budget (both lane runs), i.e. two train_single runs fit the 40 GB card at once (a free 2× on survey throughput; 12 M-budget chunks and any post-opt do not pair). Klapmuts memory: the 60k schedule was a ≤ 0.2 dB no-op on chunk 1_1 (09-23), untested on citrus. Test 2 queued behind test 1: 05 chunk 1_0 at the half schedule (30k / 22.5k / 0.0075, 12 M cap) vs the served 60k chunk, held-out PSNR at tau 0 and 3 plus train time (`logs/halfsched_test.sh`, project `h3dgs_expo_half`).
- **A, v1 result (01:4x box; `fruit_field_v1`, loss 0.081 at it 3000):** fruit of tree 5 on the 28 clip frames, argmax rule **0.202 / held-out 0.116 / trained 0.301**, dual threshold 0.156 / 0.093 / 0.230; baseline census seed 0.251 / 0.161 / 0.355. Class-level fruit vs all SAM3 fruit (43 frames): argmax 0.213 (held-out 0.156, trained 0.285). The learned field is below the census seed. Suspected cause: the plain per-pixel mean is a tree loss (fruit is ~0.3 % of labelled pixels) and the 0.5-scale render blurs 10–17 px fruit; v2 = per-class means + full-resolution render + 6,000 its, launched.
- **B, test 1 result (post-opt length at the serving cut, 05 chunk 1_0):** the evaluator gave the same held-out PSNR at tau 3 for `hierarchy.hier` (no post-opt) and `hierarchy.hier_opt` (15k): full-frame 15.75 / 15.76 mean, 14.98 median (71 held-out views; low because held-out views render with the mean exposure). Checked that this is real, not a loader artefact: post-opt changes 30 % of nodes (leaves and interior alike; alpha mean |Δ| 0.32, xyz p99 4.8 cm), yet direct renders of held-out views from the two files at tau 3 agree at 35.6–39.5 dB (mean |diff| 0.003–0.005). **At the 1280-px tau-3 cut we serve and census at, the 15k post-opt is invisible on this chunk** — one hour of the 3.4 h per chunk. The 5k point died (OOM beside the fruit-field training) and is queued alone with a tau 0 + tau 3 three-way evaluation. Caveat: post-opt is what makes coarse cuts (far cells of a merged survey hierarchy) look right; not tested here.
- **A, v2 result (`fruit_field_v2`: per-class means, full-res render, 6,000 its, loss 0.089):** fruit of tree 5, dual threshold **0.269 / held-out 0.157 / trained 0.398** (census baseline 0.251 / 0.161 / 0.355); argmax 0.216 / 0.142 / 0.301. Class-level fruit (43 frames) dual 0.208 / 0.132 / 0.304. So a learned, blending-aware field reaches the census seed on held-out views and +0.04 on trained ones: parity, not a step. The held-out/trained gap (0.16 / 0.40) is the same as the seed's, i.e. the ceiling is the geometry and view generalisation, as the audit said. Next: the same v2 recipe on the fruit-densified chunk (a learned field can exploit smaller gaussians where the share rule could not).
- **A, v2 on the fruit-densified chunk (`chunk_1_0_sam3_fruitd/fruit_field_v2`):** fruit of tree 5, dual 0.231 / held-out 0.150 / trained 0.324; class-level 0.193 / 0.130 / 0.274. Slightly below the same recipe on the undensified chunk (0.269 / 0.157 / 0.398). A learned field does not exploit the split gaussians either.
- **A, feedback summary:** learned per-gaussian fruit features (CountingFruit's loss and query, frozen geometry) = parity with the census seed on held-out views (0.157 vs 0.161), +0.04 on trained views; densification adds nothing under either seed or learned field; every variant shows the same held-out/trained gap (~0.16 / ~0.40), so the fruit limit on this chunk is view generalisation of the colour geometry, not the assignment rule. CountingFruit avoids this by counting in 3D (clusters), which is what our tracking + triangulation loop already does (92 → 175 confirmed oranges with the fine-tuned detector). Untested from the paper: joint training (geometry moving under the semantic loss) — a much larger change to H3DGS's training.
- **B, test 2 result (half schedule, 03:32 box; `h3dgs_expo_half`):** train_single 30k / 22.5k / 0.0075 at the 12 M cap: **3,755 s** (the served 60k chunk: 8,191 s), 12.01 M gaussians; hierarchy 199 s; post-opt 15k 3,118 s. Held-out, same 71 views, same evaluator: tau 3 full-frame **15.92 mean / 15.57 median** (served 60k chunk: 15.76 / 14.98), sky-masked 16.44 / 16.35; tau 0 15.90 / 15.56. The half schedule is 2.2× faster and +0.6 dB median on held-out views (the 60k run overfits them). Training-view fit measured next (what the walkthrough renders).

  - Post-opt rerun: 15k vs none identical again at tau 0 as well (15.75 / 14.99 both); the lone 5k post-opt failed a second time (rc=1 in 209 s, GPU alone) — cause below.
  - The lone 5k post-opt died at it 850 from an OOM I caused (I launched the training-view evaluations beside it at 32 GB); with 0 and 15k identical at both taus the 5k point adds nothing, so test 1 is closed: **post-opt can be skipped for a chunk served and censused at the 1280-px tau-3 cut.**
- **B, test 3 launched (03:40 box, `logs/fast_recipe_chain.sh`): the fast recipe = half schedule + no post-opt** on 05 chunk 0_0 (vs the served expo 0_0: 60k + 15k post-opt) and then 04 chunk 1_1 (its first 01-formula chunk). Held-out PSNR at tau 0 and 3 with the same evaluator for the fast chunk and the reference chunk; GPU peak sampled from nvidia-smi every 20 s (train_single's progress bar does not print peak memory under the 01 flags). Expected ~1.1 h per chunk against ~3.4 h for the full recipe.

  | chunk | fast recipe (30k, no post-opt): time · held-out tau 3 mean / median | reference chunk · held-out | GPU peak |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 05 0_0 (reference = served expo 0_0: 60k + 15k post-opt, exposure model) | 3,084 + 205 s = **55 min** · **16.48 / 16.59** | 16.40 / 16.45 | 20.2 GB |
  | 04 1_1 (reference = old `h3dgs` 1_1: no exposure model) | 3,246 + 205 s · 14.50 / 14.52 | 16.27 / 16.49 | 18.5 GB |

  - 04: NOT like-for-like — the reference has no exposure model (all 897 matrices identity), the fast run has one (lr 0.001, |M − I| up to 0.23); this evaluator renders held-out views at the MEAN exposure, which penalises exposure-trained models. Rerun of 04 1_1 on the fast recipe with `exposure_lr_init 0` launched (`h3dgs_fast_noexp`, ~55 min) to separate the schedule from the exposure confound.
- **04 1_1 fast recipe WITHOUT the exposure model (06:40 box, `h3dgs_fast_noexp`):** 3,361 s, 11.6 M gaussians, 20.2 GB peak; held-out tau 3 **14.82 / 14.78** vs the old reference 16.27 / 16.49. The exposure model explained only +0.3 dB of the 2 dB; the rest is the old 04 recipe vs the 01 flags at half schedule, not isolated (the old chunk's training flags are not in cfg_args). So the fast recipe is proven like-for-like only on 05 (+0.14 dB at a third of the time); on 04 a 60k run with the same flags would be needed to separate schedule from recipe.
- **Why the old 04 chunk wins held-out:** it is the stock survey recipe (`h3dgs_survey.sh`, 09-27): DA-V2 depth prior, LiDAR seed, 30k train (3,149 s) + 15k post-opt (3,170 s), default densification → **4.55 M nodes (~3 M gaussians)**; the fast run has 16.3 M nodes (11.6 M gaussians) and is 1.7 dB worse on held-out views. Same direction as the Klapmuts findings (more gaussians and the 60k schedule are not held-out levers) and as 05's 60k overfit.
- **B, summary (feedback for Paul):**
  1. Post-opt (15k, ~55 min/chunk) is invisible at the 1280-px tau-3 cut we serve and census at: skip it for demo/identity chunks (keep it for merged survey hierarchies viewed at coarse cuts).
  2. 30k instead of 60k: 2.2× faster train and better held-out (05 1_0: +0.6 dB median; 05 0_0 fast vs served: +0.14).
  3. Two chunks fit on the 40 GB card at once (train peaks 18.5–20.2 GB at the 12 M cap, 19.6 GB at 8 M): 2× throughput with no code.
  4. Budget: the stock 3 M-gaussian recipe beats the 12 M 01 flags by 1.7 dB on held-out views (04 1_1); the 01 flags were adopted for TRAINING-view fit (walkthroughs on trained poses), which this evaluator's sample could not measure reliably (11 views). The right recipe depends on which views the product shows.
  → Fast recipe per chunk ≈ 55 min alone, ~30 min effective with two in flight, against ~3.4 h today; a 6-chunk survey ≈ 3 GPU-hours instead of ~20. Untested: the stock recipe without post-opt (would be ~52 min and the best held-out so far on 04).

The half schedule is a trade (−1.1 to −1.7 dB on training views for +0.1–0.6 on held-out) and is NOT recommended for the walkthrough chunks.

At 03:40 the solo 30k run peaked at 20.2 GB; whether two 12 M-cap runs fit the 39.7 GB card is exactly what this measures.
- **Paul: "no I wanted to measure the PSNRs." Measured — and it found a bug.** On the peer's 203 in-cell training views of the served 05 1_0 chunk (`eval_incell_1_0/scores.json`, tau 0: 26.28 median), my renderer gave 23.42 — until the exposure was applied the way H3DGS does (`gaussian_renderer` use_trained_exp: `image_row × E[:3,:3] + E[:,3]`); then it reproduces the peer's per-view numbers exactly (28.62 / 28.62 / 27.52) and the **26.28 median**. Every script that rendered with `torch.einsum('ij,jhw->ihw', E, img)` applied E TRANSPOSED: bar_walk, the citrus/cabbage/chilli cuts, row panels, fruit_bc_score, fruit_field — and `hier_render_service.py` (the live stage, lines 225/227). The exposure matrices are far from symmetric (05 1_0: off-diagonal |entry| median 0.10, max 0.40), so this is a real colour cast on served frames and PSNR captions 0.6–2.5 dB low, not a rounding matter. Fixed in all of them (service committed on exp/2026-07-14-week; demo scripts in automation); identity/IoU numbers are unaffected (exposure touches colour only).

The half schedule's training-view cost is 1.5–3.6 dB: firmly not for walkthrough chunks. Remaining 05 1_0 variants (no post-opt, half) re-measured next.
