## 2026-10-08 — A/B: stock splatfacto vs H3DGS on the same views (Paul: "3 dB is a lot, what's unique about 3dgs that stock splat cannot do? can we a/b with the splatfacto using the same views")
- **Set-up:** `splatfacto_1_0/` = 05 chunk 1_0's COLMAP (sparse/0 symlinked, rectified images symlinked) → transforms.json via `colmap_to_nerfstudio.py`, frames limited to H3DGS's **294 training views** (`train_filenames`) and the **71 in-cell held-out views** the H3DGS evaluator scores (`test_filenames`), `ply_file_path` = the chunk's `points3D.ply` (the same init H3DGS trained from), `nerfstudio-data --eval-mode filename`. splatfacto defaults otherwise, 30k then 60k (`logs/splatfacto_ab.sh`), no appearance/exposure model.
- **Scoring:** ns-eval on the 71 held-out (nerfstudio PSNR) vs H3DGS 15.76 (mean exposure) — H3DGS carries a ~0.3 dB exposure penalty there; training views = ns-render of the train split, PSNR on the same 60 views as `trainview_psnr.py`, against H3DGS 24.29 (own exposure) and the no-exposure H3DGS number measured below.
- **What H3DGS adds over stock 3DGS:** the hierarchy (interior nodes + per-view cut by tau = level of detail), chunked training with a shared scaffold so a survey merges into one model that renders at a budget, the post-opt of that hierarchy, a gaussian budget, the depth prior and the per-image exposure model. Per chunk at the fine cut it is 3DGS; this A/B measures whether the per-chunk quality differs at all.
- **H3DGS 05 1_0 training views with NO exposure applied (`NOEXP=1`): 17.05 median** vs 24.29 with each view's own exposure. The per-image exposure affine is worth **7 dB** on training views of this chunk: the raw model colours sit far from the photos (auto-exposure / white balance across the drive), and the 3×4 affine with off-diagonals up to 0.4 absorbs it. Correction to earlier wording: the evaluator's held-out numbers (15.76) were RAW renders (no `--exposure_json`), not "mean exposure", so they are the like-for-like partner of a splatfacto run without an appearance model; the walkthrough's 24–26 dB depends on the exposure model, which stock splatfacto does not have.
- splatfacto trains at ~22 ms/it on these 294 views (H3DGS train_single: 140 ms/it), i.e. 30k in ~11 min.
- **A/B RESULT (14:22 box), 05 chunk 1_0, identical 294 training views / poses / 55k init points / 71 in-cell held-out views:**

  | model | train time | held-out (raw renders, 71 views) | training views, same 60 (median) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | H3DGS served (60k + 15k post-opt, exposure model, 12 M cap) | 8,191 + 3,468 s | 15.76 (H3DGS evaluator, raw) | **24.29 with own exposure / 17.05 raw** |
  | splatfacto 30k (default, no appearance model) | **643 s** | **16.87** (ssim 0.555, lpips 0.538) | 22.54 raw |
  | splatfacto 60k | 1,183 s | 16.91 | 23.40 raw |

  - Raw colour fidelity: stock splatfacto beats H3DGS by 5.5 dB on training views and 1.1 dB on held-out views, in 11 min against 3.2 h. H3DGS's 24.29 exists only through the per-image exposure affine; its gaussians do not carry the photos' colours (17.05 raw), the affine does.
  - The held-out ceiling (~16–17 dB) is the same for both: view generalisation of this drive's geometry, not the method.
  - Third arm running: splatfacto 30k + bilateral grid (per-image ISP handling, nerfstudio's analogue of the exposure model).
  - Capacity: splatfacto reached this with **0.73 M gaussians** (30k: 732,377; 60k: 712,890) against H3DGS's 12 M (budget-driven densification at grad 0.0075).
  - **Like-for-like walkthrough number:** a per-view 3×4 affine fitted to each training photo (what H3DGS's trained exposure is for a training view) lifts splatfacto only 22.54 → 22.76 (30k) and 23.40 → 23.46 (60k): its raw renders already match the photos' colours, so its residual is structure. Against H3DGS's 24.29 (its own affine): H3DGS +0.8 dB on training views with 16× the gaussians and 10× the time; splatfacto +1.1 dB on held-out views.
- **Bilateral-grid arm (14:40 box):** splatfacto 30k + `use-bilateral-grid` (per-image ISP handling, training-time only in nerfstudio): 1,050 s; held-out 15.97 (raw), training views raw **19.08**, with a fitted per-view affine 21.63. The grid absorbs the appearance variation the way H3DGS's affine does, so its raw gaussians drift from the photos (like H3DGS's 17.05) without reaching H3DGS's 24.29 when corrected; ns-render does not apply the trained grid, so this arm is scored at a disadvantage on training views.
- **A/B verdict:** on identical data, stock splatfacto (default) is the better raw model (held-out +1.1 dB, training views +5.5 dB raw) at 1/10 the time and 1/16 the gaussians; H3DGS's remaining edge is +0.8 dB on training views via its trained per-image exposure, and the hierarchy for survey-scale rendering. The walkthrough-quality lever is the exposure model, not the hierarchy or the budget.
- **Post-geometry exposure on splatfacto (Paul: "can the per-frame exposure be a post-geometry optimisation?"), `automation/h3dgs/fruit_field/splat_exposure_refine.py`:** 30k splatfacto, geometry frozen (means/scales/quats/opacities), colours (SH) re-optimised jointly with a per-training-view 3×4 affine (identity init), L1 on training views. v1 (3,000 its, dc and rest both at lr 0.0025, affine 0.001): DRIFTED — training views raw 22.53 → 21.64, with affine 21.35; held-out 16.95 → 16.73; affines barely moved (diag 0.93–1.03). Cause: features_rest at 20× splatfacto's own rate. v2 = dc 0.0005 / rest 0.000025 / affine 0.005.
  - v2 (dc 0.0005 / rest ÷20 / affine 0.005): colours stable (raw 22.53 → 22.57, held-out 16.95 → 17.01) but the affines, each updated only ~10 times by Adam, ended as noise (diag 0.79–1.15) and HURT: with own affine 19.39. v3 = alternating: on every visit the view's affine is solved exactly by least squares on the current render (the closed form the post-hoc fit used), and only the colours take a gradient step through it.
  - v3 result (closed-form affine per visit, colours dc 0.0005 / rest ÷20, 3,000 its, 2.3 min; first run had the affine layout transposed, fixed): training views raw 22.18 → **with own affine 22.89 median / 23.42 mean**; held-out raw 16.85, with the mean affine 17.18. Against the plain post-hoc affine on the untouched 30k model (22.76) the joint refinement adds 0.1 dB; against H3DGS's 24.29 it stays 1.4 dB short on training views while leading on held-out (16.85–17.18 vs 15.76).
  - **Answer:** exposure CAN be a post-geometry stage (a 2-minute closed-form affine per view recovers 0.2–0.3 dB on splatfacto), but it does not reproduce H3DGS's training-view edge: that edge comes from co-training 12 M gaussians for 60k iterations WITH the per-view affines, i.e. the geometry fits around the exposure, not from the affine itself. A splatfacto with a per-image exposure co-trained from the start (the bilateral grid is the stock option; applied at render it was not evaluated) is the remaining test.
- **Bilateral grid applied at render (Paul: "try that"; `automation/h3dgs/fruit_field/splat_bilateral_score.py`, the grid applied exactly as training does, on the eval-mode render):** splatfacto 30k + bilateral grid, training views raw 19.08 → **with the trained grid 23.64 median / 24.13 mean**; held-out raw 16.47 median / 15.97 mean. Against H3DGS served (24.29 median / 24.14 mean with its exposure; held-out 15.76): co-trained per-image appearance in stock splatfacto matches H3DGS on training views (equal mean, −0.65 median) in 17 min with ~0.7 M gaussians, and leads on held-out. So Paul's premise holds: the recipe is replicable and the exposure is the lever; the affine must be CO-TRAINED (post-hoc on frozen geometry gives 22.9), as the bilateral grid does. 60k + bilateral arm launched for the last data point.
- **Paul: "Can this be the new prod recipe, v3?"** Proposal written: `plans/recipe_v3_splatfacto.md` (what v3 is, the measured basis, the four things to build before the prod bar — chunk builder, splatfacto render service with per-view grids, a flat-gaussian identity port (the long pole, 2–3 days), the blessing checks — and what v3 does not cover: survey-scale LoD).
- **60k + bilateral grid (17:41 box):** train 2,051 s; training views raw 19.73 → **with the grid 24.12 median / 24.64 mean**; held-out raw 16.50 median / 15.94 mean. Parity with H3DGS v2 on the walkthrough views (24.29 / 24.14) and +0.7 dB held-out median, at 34 min vs 3.2 h and ~0.7 M vs 12 M gaussians. v3 table in `plans/recipe_v3_splatfacto.md` updated.

- **Recipe CPU audit (Paul 16:0x: "we shouldn't be spending so much time on CPU operations"), measured over the 22 fleet cells:** per cell median 43.6 min = **training 38.4 (88 %)** + ns-eval/grid score 0.6 + chunk supervision 2.5 (paint 15 s, compile ~1 min, text bank, frame pick — pure CPU) + identity 2.1 (census 22 s, seed B ~40 s, 14 feature maps 15 s, verdicts ~1 min) + other ≈ 0. **CPU overhead per cell ≈ 5 min (11 %); the GPU training is the cost.** One-off per survey: 02 export 38 min (per-chunk COLMAP triangulation 19 min, sequential, single-thread per chunk), 03 89 min (chunks 56 min) — CPU, already done. Lanes: SfM 32–117 min each (exhaustive matching + GLOMAP). **The real CPU finding: GLOMAP's solve runs at 706 % CPU on the 12-core box (load 11.6) and starves the trainer's host thread — GPU SM utilisation 35–53 % and 88 ms/it while a lane SfM runs, vs ~37 ms/it alone (comparison SM % being measured once GLOMAP exits).** Actions: lane loop stops after Lindendhof lane 3; `v3_lane_fleet_v2.sh` pins the lane chain to cores 0-5 (taskset) and uses sequential matching (test on lane 4 pending); 02/03 supervision already pre-built on one core so those cells drop the 2.5 min.

First peaks came out at 0.5 m pitch; **independent check, the LiDAR trunk band (ground + 0.25–0.70 m) autocorrelates at 1.44–1.48 m along every well-seen row** → the trees branch into several upright leaders ~0.5 m apart that SAM3 also calls trunks. Fix: only mask points in the trunk-base band (ground + 0.2–0.7 m), trees ≥ 1.0 m apart, rows ≥ 2.4 m apart → **126 trees in 7 rows, row spacing 2.75–3.1 m, in-row pitch median 1.30–1.60 m (p10 1.0–1.3)** = the LiDAR period. Decisions: RA-L (6 + 2 pages), HiGH method paper, paper first in the GPU window after the phone fleet, real LangSplat time-boxed to a day, no NeurOsiris fork, full set → submit 20–27 Nov.
- **E1 protocol scorer (`automation/h3dgs/v3/argmax_eval.py` + `identity_inventory.py`, `e1_protocol_run.sh`, `protocol_table.py`):** one decode per frame of the existing `maps_v3/*.npz`, then oracle (containment_eval's 57-threshold sweep, kept as the upper bound), **argmax** (per pixel the best word of the level among the cell's bank; presence = the norm check only), IoU curves at all 57 thresholds with/without argmax (the aggregator fits ONE threshold per level on census heads → held-out, and across surveys), nesting consistency (row-level argmax = the row of the tree-level argmax). **Check: reproduces the recorded oracle lines — 05 1_0 77/77 heads, max |ΔIoU| 0.0009; 05 0_0 68/68, 0.0055; 01 0_2 73/73, 0.0011; cabbage 56/56, 0.0049; 40–70 s per cell.** First cells: 05 1_0 TREE held-out oracle median 0.849 → argmax 0.757 (n=26), ROW 0.865 → 0.872 (n=21), FRUIT 0.355 → 0.024 (n=2), nesting 0.887; 01 (3 cells) TREE 0.881 → 0.858, ROW 0.930 → 0.911, nesting 0.921; the fixed per-cell threshold adds ≤ 0.01 over argmax and a threshold fitted on another citrus survey costs ≤ 0.03. **Run over the 103-cell inventory started 21:21** (`logs/e1_protocol.log`, nice'd beside the phone fleet); 05 0_0 needed its text bank built first (`build_text_bank.py`); the cabbage cell's (IMG_7993_s0) supervision had moved to `h3dgs_native/chunk_lane_ud` → scored with `--sup`.
- **E2 geometry ablation, tooling + first cell (05 1_0, one cell — not a result yet):** `hyperbolic_ae.py` (training copy + package copy) gained `euclidean` (same MLPs, losses and level norms in flat space: exp/log maps = identity, Lorentz distance → L2, Oxy angle → its Euclidean counterpart; `train_hyperembedder_graph.py --euclidean`; `build_census_init.py` seeds without the log map and now dumps `<features>.labels.npy`); `build_flat_seed.py` = arm C (three PCA-32 blocks tree|row|fruit of the raw CLIP text, basis on the survey vocabulary: 154 words, 32 components explain 49.7 %, 96 floats per gaussian); `argmax_eval.py --space hyper|hyper_own|euclid|flat`; `e2_arms_cell.sh` chains seed → render → score per cell. **05 1_0 held-out (n=26 trees / 21 rows / 2 fruit), argmax medians: A hyperbolic containment TREE 0.757 / ROW 0.872 / FRUIT 0.024, nesting 0.887; D own point only (no ancestors) 0.757 / 0.174 / 0.025, nesting 0.292 → the ancestor steps ARE the row level; C flat 96-d 0.777 / 0.861 / 0.246, nesting 0.813 → the pre-registered risk: flat per-level fields match trees and rows at 3× the storage, the single hyperbolic field keeps the levels consistent.** The Euclidean embedder (05, `experimental/identity/embedder/05_13D_euclid_v1`, production flags + `--euclidean`) trains beside the fleet; row retrieval 97 % at epoch ~900.
- **E3 LangSplat baseline, env + datasets (21:43–21:50):** conda env `langsplat` = clone of `h3dgs` (py 3.12, torch 2.4.1+cu124) + LangSplat's three CUDA/C++ submodules built against `_cuda12` in their own shell (rasteriser 1 min, all imports ok) + SAM ViT-H (2.56 GB) — 3 min in all, no patch needed (`logs/langsplat_env.sh`). LangSplat's COLMAP reader trains on every registered image and its only split is every-8th, so `langsplat_prep.py` builds a dataset per cell from our TRAINING split only (symlinked images + filtered `images.bin`; the chunk workspaces register train + held-out: 01 3_1 728 + 82, 04 0_0 449 + 54): 01 3_1 every 2nd → 368 images, 04 0_0 → 228, 05 1_0 (`splatfacto_1_0`, 294 training views) → 151, census frames always kept. Chain `langsplat_cell.sh`: preprocess (SAM+CLIP, same ViT-B-16/laion2b as ours) → autoencoder → RGB 3DGS by LangSplat's own trainer (`langsplat_train_rgb.py`: include_feature=False, which its CLI cannot set) → 3 feature levels → `langsplat_render_named.py` (our verdict cameras from the COLMAP poses, 640×360) → `argmax_eval.py --space langsplat` (AE decode 3→512, LERF relevancy with the canonical negatives, min over them). Preprocess timing run on 05 1_0 started 21:46 beside the fleet, E1 and two embedders (GPU at 36 of 40 GB).
- **LangSplat preprocess timing (21:46–23:03, killed):** `preprocess.py` on the 05 1_0 dataset (151 images, 1280×720, SAM ViT-H automatic masks at 32 points/side + 1 crop layer, CLIP per mask at three granularities) took **4,539 s for the first image** beside the fleet trainer, the E1 scorer and two embedders (4 GPU processes, 36 of 40 GB) — 151 images would be ~190 h; stopped. Not a verdict on LangSplat's cost: re-measure alone on the card (after E1 finishes) before deciding `--resolution` / `--every`; the one-day box for the baseline is now on the preprocessing step, not the env.
- **E2 four arms on 05 1_0 (held-out argmax medians, 26 tree / 21 row / 2 fruit heads; one cell):** hyperbolic containment (A) TREE 0.757 / ROW 0.872 / nesting 0.887; own point, no ancestors (D) 0.757 / 0.174 / 0.292; flat 3×32-d per-level fields (C) 0.777 / 0.861 / 0.813; **Euclidean 32-d embedder, same recipe (B) 0.780 / 0.209 / 0.365** (its twin trained by `e2_embedders.sh`, row retrieval 97 %, 22:36). Reading, if it replicates: a single 32-d field reaches the row level only with the hierarchy in its geometry (B and D fail rows the same way), or by paying 3× storage for separate fields (C), and the hyperbolic field keeps the levels more consistent (nesting 0.887 vs 0.813). Euclidean twins trained for 01/02/03/04/05/April/Lindendhof (`e2_embedders.sh`, 21:42–23:03); `e2_arms_run.sh` (B, C, D on all 103 cells) starts automatically when E1 finishes.
- **Row-union rule (05 1_0, held-out, 21 row heads):** scoring a row as the union of its member trees' tree-level argmax pixels (scene-graph lookup at query time) gives A 0.872 / D 0.872 / C 0.865 / B 0.875 — every arm reaches the row level once the hierarchy is consulted. So the hyperbolic field's row advantage (0.872 vs 0.174 / 0.209 by the row WORD alone) is that the hierarchy lives in the field — one word per level, no graph lookup, no extra fields — not accuracy; the paper's claim must be stated that way (representation, storage, consistency), with the union numbers in the table. The 05 1_0 hyperbolic arm was re-scored with the current scorer (77/77 oracle heads reproduced, |ΔIoU| ≤ 0.0009) so all four arms carry the union metric.
- **E1 DONE (21:21–23:24, 101 cells by the runner + 3 by hand = 104 cells, 17,231 heads, 1,444 frames; table `logs/e1_protocol_table_v3.md`, numbers `aru_sil_core/src/docs/paper/numbers/e1_v3.json`).** Held-out argmax IoU medians (oracle in brackets), trees / rows: **01 0.848 (0.881) / 0.900 (0.925), 02 0.808 (0.846) / 0.791 (0.845), 03 0.832 (0.851) / 0.850 (0.877), 04 0.674 (0.739) / 0.800 (0.779), 05 0.773 (0.829) / 0.859 (0.840)** — the oracle-to-argmax gap is 0.02–0.07 on trees and ≤ 0.05 on rows (rows even gain on 04/05); ONE fixed threshold per level fitted on a cell's census frames gives the argmax number ± 0.01, and a threshold fitted on any other citrus survey costs ≤ 0.03 (transfer matrix in the table) — the draft's "a fixed threshold transfers across blocks" holds within citrus. Near trees (≥ 3 % of the frame): 01 0.908, 02 0.852, 03 0.890, 04 0.712, 05 0.796; nesting 0.81–0.89; sibling confusion medians 0.07–0.23. Off-site: April trees 0.588 (near 0.689), rows 0.638; pepper (IMG_7990_s1) 0.969 / 0.969; cabbage 0.534 / 0.655; Lindendhof all-head 0.115 but near trees 0.741 and near rows 0.887, nesting 0.920; Dec all-head 0.000 (4,400 of 5,395 held-out bag heads are < 0.2 % of the frame) with near bags 0.717 and near rows 0.612, nesting 0.555. **Fruit collapses under argmax competition with tree words (04/05/pepper held-out 0.000; oracle 0.001–0.384)** — the verdict frames were not fruit-picked and the fruit word loses to the tree word on mixed primitives; E4 scores fruit on fruit-picked frames with instance recall. E2 arms (B, C, D) started on all cells at 23:24 (`logs/e2_arms.log`).

## 2026-10-13 — paper experiments first (fleet paused), E2 fleet-wide, LangSplat and FruitNeRF
- **Phone v3 fleet PAUSED 03:3x (Paul: "I might prioritise paper experiments ahead of the image farm work" → pause chosen):** runner stopped after 5 of 45 cells; the running cell `gendia/osuga_full` (step 52k of 60k) was left to finish (the fleet marks a cell done by `eval_heldout.json`). Resume: `bash automation/h3dgs/v3/v3_fleet.sh /home/paperspace/logs/v3_fleet_queue_phone_20261012.txt` (skips finished entries); 40 cells ≈ 48 h alone (estimate from 37–76 min per cell).
- **FruitNeRF real data (Zenodo 10869455, 19.06 GB):** single-stream 8 KiB/s (ETA ≈ 650 h) → `automation/datasets/zenodo_parallel_get.py` (byte ranges on the API endpoint, 64 MiB resumable chunks): 32 connections 0.44 MB/s, **128 connections 1.33 MB/s, ETA ≈ 5 h**, no throttling errors.
- **E2b seeding ablation, tooling + first cell (05 1_0, held-out argmax medians, 26 tree / 21 row heads; 03:47–03:57):** `train_feature_field.py` learns the per-gaussian features by gradient descent with stage 2's recipe (geodesic loss on the hyperboloid, labelled pixels only, frozen geometry; 294 census frames at 640×360, Adam lr 0.01, 3,000 steps = 2.5 min), `projection_vote_seed.py` is the naive lift (gaussian centres projected into the census frames, majority label, no alpha weights), `e2b_seed_cell.sh` / `e2b_seed_run.sh` chain them with the E1 scorer. **Census seed (A) TREE 0.757 / ROW 0.872 → learned from zero (Z, 3k) 0.646 / 0.713 (geodesic still falling, 2.09 at 3k) → seed + refine (R, 3k) 0.652 / 0.736 (the 10-02 regression replicated on v3: training moves the seed 0.69 on average and lowers it) → projection vote (P) 0.292 / 0.525 (assigns 77 % of the gaussians vs the census's 36.7 %: background and occluded gaussians get labels).** One cell, one lr/step setting (a 10k-step zero run follows); the 15-cell run (`logs/e2b_cells.txt`, fixed before results) starts now.
- **Zero-init trained longer (05 1_0, 10,000 steps, 4 min):** geodesic 1.906 (3k: 2.088), TREE held-out 0.672 (3k 0.646), ROW 0.696 (3k 0.713) — still 0.09 / 0.18 below the census seed (0.757 / 0.872); the learned field keeps improving slowly on trees and not on rows.
- **E2 arm B artefact found (04:3x):** the Euclidean twins seed at very different norms — citrus 01 5.85 / 05 4.58 (like the hyperbolic tangents, 5.67 / 4.89) but April 1.03, Dec 1.10 — so containment_eval's absolute presence check (||f|| < 0.5 = void) voided most of the blended Euclidean pixels off-site (April TREE 0.000 under arm B while the same seed's citrus numbers are 0.84–0.89). `argmax_eval.py --void-ref <arm bin> <hyper bin>` scales the check to the arm's seed norms (0.5 × median ratio); arm B is re-scored that way for the non-citrus surveys (`e2_rescore_rv.sh`, old files kept as `protocol_euclid_abs05.jsonl`). Flat arms seed at 6.7–7.9 (raw CLIP PCA) and are unaffected.
- **E2 DONE on all cells (23:24–04:38, 102 cells by the runner + 05 1_0 / 0_0 by hand; `logs/e2_arms_table.md`, `aru_sil_core/src/docs/paper/numbers/e2_arms.json`).** Held-out argmax IoU medians over the heads every arm scored — trees (hyperbolic containment / own point / flat 3×32-d / Euclidean 32-d): 01 0.848 / 0.848 / 0.878 / 0.894 (n=864), 02 0.808 / 0.810 / 0.808 / 0.844, 03 0.832 / 0.834 / 0.852 / 0.866, 04 0.674 / 0.675 / 0.708 / 0.688, 05 0.773 / 0.774 / 0.787 / 0.788; **rows by the row word: 01 0.900 / 0.057 / 0.894 / 0.058, 02 0.791 / 0.094 / 0.810 / 0.108, 03 0.850 / 0.086 / 0.803 / 0.080, 04 0.800 / 0.181 / 0.789 / 0.245, 05 0.859 / 0.162 / 0.834 / 0.182; rows as the union of member trees (scene-graph lookup): every arm 0.77–0.90; nesting consistency: hyperbolic 0.81–0.89, flat 0.66–0.87, own point / Euclidean 0.09–0.35.** Reading, fleet-wide: the Euclidean and flat fields are slightly BETTER on trees (+0.015 to +0.046 over hyperbolic, near trees 0.93 vs 0.91 on 01); a single 32-d field answers a parent query from the parent's word alone only with the hierarchy in its geometry (own point and Euclidean fail rows identically); the flat stack matches rows at 3× the storage and lower consistency. Off-site (April near trees 0.70 / 0.70 / 0.70, Lindendhof 0.74 / 0.74 / 0.73, Dec near bags 0.72 / 0.72 / 0.69) the arms agree on trees; the Euclidean off-site numbers await the relative-void re-score. The paper's geometry claim is therefore: one compact field, parent levels from words, consistency — not accuracy.
- **LangSplat preprocess, second timing (04:39–05:21, killed):** first image 2,482 s on a card shared with three light jobs (E2b trainer, Euclidean re-score, flat-30 arm; util 17–50 %) — vs 4,539 s under four heavier tenants. Either way > 100 h for the 151-image 05 1_0 dataset: SAM ViT-H automatic masks at 32 points/side + 1 crop layer (5 × 1,024 prompts) and CLIP at four granularities per mask is latency-bound and collapses under GPU time-slicing. Decision: the real-LangSplat box is spent unless an exclusive-card test (5 images, no co-tenants, after today's arms) shows ≤ 3 min per image; the fallback `LangSplat-lite` (per-gaussian CLIP image features lifted from SAM3 mask crops with the alpha-blend weights, no labels, no training) is being built as the external-style baseline.
- **E2b seeding ablation DONE on the 15 fixed cells (03:58–05:2x; `aru_sil_core/src/docs/paper/numbers/e2b_seed.{json,csv}`, `logs/e2b_seed_table.md`).** Held-out argmax tree IoU medians, census seed → learned from zero (3k steps, stage-2 geodesic loss) → seed + 3k refine → projection vote: **01 0.855 → 0.327 → 0.663 → 0.301; 02 0.775 → 0.513 → 0.614 → 0.556; 03 0.795 → 0.173 → 0.518 → 0.326; 04 0.672 → 0.455 → 0.562 → 0.304; 05 0.747 → 0.643 → 0.684 → 0.325; April 0.680 → 0.517 → 0.574 → 0.604**; rows by the word follow the same order (01 0.892 → 0.640 → 0.771 → 0.323). On every citrus survey the census seed beats a field learned from zero by 0.10–0.62 and refining the seed lowers it by 0.06–0.28; the naive centre-projection lift is 0.30–0.56 (0.60 on April's large plants). One optimiser setting (Adam 0.01, 3k steps; 10k on 05 1_0 gave 0.672) — the budget caveat goes in the paper.
- **LangSplat preprocess is CPU-bound here:** the killed process ran at 891 % CPU with 0 % GPU on a 12-core box at load 13–14 (SAM's mask post-processing and CLIP tiling on CPU); an exclusive card would not have fixed 2,482 s per image. LangSplat-lite (alpha-lifted SAM3 mask-crop CLIP features, no labels, no training) replaces it as the external-style baseline.
- **Euclidean arm off-site is a training failure, not a scoring artefact (05:3x):** with the relative presence check April's Euclidean arm still scores TREE 0.000 (sibling confusion 1.000: one word wins everywhere) while its oracle is 0.33. The twins' own training logs explain it — object retrieval **April 15.18 % (73/481), Dec 1.99 % (15/754)** vs 100 % on every citrus survey (100–309 objects), Lindendhof (126) and the image-farm cells, with row retrieval 93–100 % everywhere; the hyperbolic production embedders for April/Dec keep object identity (near trees 0.69 / bags 0.72 under E1). So the same recipe in flat space loses object identity on the two largest hierarchies — one run each, lr/epochs as production; a retrain with another seed/lr would tell whether it is capacity or optimisation. Reported as measured in the arms table (euclid 0.000 on April/Dec) with this explanation.
- **LangSplat-lite built and run on 05 1_0 (05:3x, 208 s):** `sam3_clip_targets.py` (3,007 SAM3 'tree' masks over 289 of 294 census frames → two-level mask-crop CLIP features as the HiGH encoder makes them → PCA-32, 77.5 % of the variance) → `lift_pixel_features.py` (alpha-weighted lift, closed form: 412,907 of 787,196 gaussians, 23 s) → render → scorer. On the cell's instance words it scores 0.000 on every head, oracle included — image-CLIP features have no relation to census-assigned vocabulary tokens, which is the fairness point to state, not a result. **Category protocol added (`argmax_eval.py --category tree=ALL,orange=FRUIT`: LERF relevancy against the canonical negatives, identical for every space):** "tree" vs all labelled pixels, held-out 10 frames — hyperbolic 0.883 (oracle; rel ≥ 0.5 0.882; argmax 0.882), flat 3×32 0.879 / 0.555 / 0.872, **LangSplat-lite 0.701 / 0.668 / 0.668**; "orange" ≈ 0 for all three (fruit is 0.1 % of these frames; E4 frames needed). Running the lite chain + category scoring over the 15 E2b cells.
- **E4 fruit on fruit-picked frames DONE (06:02–06:12, 12 cells of 04/05; `aru_sil_core/src/docs/paper/numbers/e4_fruit.{json,csv}`):** frames re-picked by fruit pixels (`pick_frames.py --prefer-fruit`, 4 census + up to 10 held-out per cell), existing v3 seed rendered and scored. Held-out fruit heads (04: 17, 05: 29, all < 0.2 % of the frame): pixel IoU oracle 0.003 / 0.158, argmax 0.000 / 0.005; **instance recall (any lit pixel on the orange): 0.94 / 0.90 at the oracle threshold, 0.41 / 0.55 under argmax**; census frames 0.32 / 0.37 oracle IoU, recall 0.95 / 0.96. Trees on the same frames 0.61 / 0.66. Reading for the paper: at 10–17 px the fruit level is a detection ("this tree carries fruit, here") — recall 0.9 with a threshold, ≈ 0.5 under competition with the tree word — not a segmentation; pixel IoU is written as the limitation and the 3D-confirmed-fruit layer as the counting route.
- **E2 equal-storage arm (C30: three flat PCA-10 blocks = 30 floats) DONE on all 104 cells (04:40–06:19; `numbers/e2_arms_flat30.{json,csv}`).** Held-out argmax tree IoU medians hyperbolic 32-d / flat 3×32 / flat 3×10: 01 0.848 / 0.878 / 0.752, 02 0.808 / 0.808 / 0.782, 03 0.832 / 0.852 / 0.520, 04 0.674 / 0.708 / 0.506, 05 0.773 / 0.787 / 0.655, April 0.588 / 0.574 / 0.000; **rows by the word: flat 3×10 0.000–0.090 on every survey** (hyperbolic 0.64–0.90, flat 3×32 0.51–0.89). At the hyperbolic field's storage the flat stack loses the row level entirely and 0.07–0.31 of the tree level — the compactness claim, measured (PCA compression; a learned autoencoder per block is the untested stronger flat variant).
- **Flat 3×10 correction (01 3_1 check):** the row collapse is a discrimination failure, not detection — per-head oracle thresholds still reach ROW 0.923 / TREE 0.913 with the 10-d blocks, but the argmax among row words is 0.000 (one row word wins everywhere) and among tree words drops to 0.791 (sibling confusion 0.107 vs 0.057 hyperbolic); the PCA-10 basis explains only 19.5 % of the vocabulary's variance. So "at equal storage the flat stack cannot tell the row words apart under competition" is the precise claim.
- **E3 LangSplat-lite + category protocol DONE on the 15 cells (05:38–06:31; `numbers/e3_category.{json,csv}`).** Held-out "tree" (every labelled pixel; 20 heads per survey) — oracle-threshold IoU hyperbolic / flat 3×32 / LangSplat-lite: **01 0.880 / 0.894 / 0.491, 02 0.788 / 0.803 / 0.496, 03 0.855 / 0.876 / 0.380, 04 0.821 / 0.829 / 0.491, 05 0.874 / 0.879 / 0.673**; at LangSplat's usual relevancy ≥ 0.5: 0.79–0.87 / 0.77–0.87 / 0.37–0.66. "orange" ≈ 0 for every field (3–4 fruit heads, < 0.2 % of the frame). The label-free image-feature field finds "tree" at 0.38–0.67 IoU; the supervised fields at 0.79–0.89 — and only they can name an instance or a row. Lite's lift costs 1–7 min per cell (SAM3 masks exist already); no training anywhere.
- **E5 serving cost (06:5x, `v3_ws_check.py` against `v3_render_service.py` on 05 1_0, 787,196 gaussians + identity features, A100 shared only with one embedder training):** 20 sequential free poses at 1280×720 — render 18.1 ms, total 28.9 ms, round trip 37.4 ms median → **26.7 fps** (vs 9.2 fps measured under contention on 10-08); named trained views PSNR 23.3–25.5 dB, held-out 10.3–21.4 (the free-pose grid blend equals the named grid within 0.07 dB); identity queries 97 ms (row, 7 competitors) and 219 ms (first tree query, includes the text encode). Seed-to-seed spread ≤ 0.02 IoU — the "three seeds" sentence the draft could not support is now measured.
- **Phone v3 fleet RESUMED 07:2x** (the paper's GPU list is complete: E1, E2 + equal-storage arm, E2b, E3-lite + category, E4, E5, E7; only the optional 02/03 row fix — a production scene-graph change — waits for Paul): `v3_fleet.sh` on the phone queue, 40 cells left (5 done + osuga_full), ≈ 48 h alone.
- **Fruit IoU, resolution test (07:4x, 05 1_0 fruit frames re-rendered and scored at 1280×720 instead of 640×360, tag `v3_fruit_full`):** per-head oracle IoU 0.213 / 0.333 / 0.243 / 0.357 / 0.370 / 0.277 / 0.226 vs 0.211 / 0.346 / 0.257 / 0.359 / 0.371 / 0.263 / 0.234 at half resolution — identical within 0.02; resolution is not the limiter. What limits it is the halo: at each head's best threshold precision 0.27–0.50 and recall 0.33–0.59 (full res) — the fruit-lit region is a blob two to three times the orange that covers about half of it, because the gaussians that carry the fruit word also carry leaves (primitive mixing, 10-07). Under the argmax rule the tree word takes most of that blob back (argmax IoU 0.04–0.27). Fruit "ids" in the supervision are confirmed-fruit entities (fruit 10001 spans 160–840 px per frame), not single oranges.
- **Diagonal rows on 02/03 (Paul: "yeah fix the diagonal rows", 07:5x):** strict hierarchies built with the 01 recipe (`build_marker_hierarchy.py --row-solver coral --dominant-direction-xz 1,0 --dominant-direction-threshold 0.985`, today's prod markers + filtered semantic monos; 74 s / 209 s) → `experimental/coral_rows_20261013/strict_0985/`. `rows_vs_lanes_survey.py`: **02 prod (census 0.7) 28 rows, 12 row-less, 6 straight diagonals at 36–45° (24 objects) → strict 36 rows, 0 row-less, 0 diagonals, 27 along the lanes; 03 prod 40 rows, 20 row-less, 19 diagonals at 45° (148 objects) → strict 37 rows, 0 row-less, 0 diagonals, 36 along the lanes**; object ids identical in both (supervision stays valid). `coral_strict_cascade.sh` started for 02 then 03: prod hierarchy swapped (old copy + v1g embedder copied to `experimental/era_census07_20261013/`, v1g itself untouched so the lio_row100 block fleets keep their era), new survey embedder `<SV>_v1h`, every v3 cell re-banked / re-seeded / re-rendered / re-scored (old seed, maps, arms and protocols moved to `<identity>/era_census07_20261013/`), Euclidean twin retrained, arms B/C/C30/D, then E2b + E3 on the listed cells. The PSNR-Log artifact's identity numbers for 02/03 and `e1_v3.json` are census-0.7-era until the cascade finishes and the numbers are regenerated.
- **Fruit scored like every other level (Paul: "fruit and tree are the exact same thing, semantic masks. Only difference is size"; `argmax_eval.py --fruit-rule level`, 05 1_0 fruit frames):** argmax among fruit words only, presence where the pixel's tangent norm is at fruit depth (≥ 6.08 = midpoint of the tree targets' median 4.76 and the fruit targets' 7.39 — depth is the radius). Per head argmax IoU 0.188 / 0.318 / 0.196 / 0.259 / 0.214 / 0.210 / 0.175 vs 0.182 / 0.281 / 0.074 / 0.053 / 0.076 / 0.169 / 0.174 under the mixed fruit+tree competition, with precision 0.48–0.90 and recall 0.20–0.43: the pixels that render at fruit depth are the right ones, but only a fifth to two fifths of an orange's pixels do — the rest carry leaf-dominated blends at tree depth. Same mechanism as trees; the trees of that size (0.2–1 % of the frame) score 0.11–0.42 in E1, < 0.2 % score 0.00–0.05 (Dec/Lindendhof far objects), i.e. one object-size curve across levels. "Detector" was the wrong word; the paper will plot IoU against object size in the frame with fruit at the small end. Running the level rule over the 12 E4 cells.
- **Level rule over the 12 fruit cells (`numbers/e4_fruit_level.json`):** census frames improve and tighten — 04 argmax IoU 0.116 → 0.159 (precision 0.20 → 0.69), 05 0.156 → 0.214 (0.38 → 0.66) — but **held-out fruit stays at median 0.000 under either rule** (04: 17 heads, oracle 0.003; 05: 29 heads, oracle 0.158), with 90–94 % of the oranges still getting some lit pixel at the oracle threshold. Trees on the same frames are 0.61–0.66 held-out vs 0.73–0.74 census. Reading: a fruit's identity lives on gaussians that blended into the census-frame oranges; from a held-out viewpoint those mixed primitives project a few pixels off the orange, which a tree (thousands of gaussians, volumetric) never notices — the small-object end of the size curve generalises worst across views. Both rules go in `e4_fruit.csv` at the next regeneration.
- **Fruit LoD on v3 (Paul: "we need fruit nerf style densification and splitting which I thought we had … opportunistic level of detail"; 17:0x):** why it was absent — recipe v3 is stock splatfacto (no HiGH model, no fruit protection/densification); stock densification splits only above `densify_size_thresh` = 0.01 model units = 0.28 m world at 05 1_0's scale (0.0356), so 8 cm oranges stay on ≥ 28 cm carriers that also render leaves; the lever that worked on 1 Oct (H3DGS chunk side-car: tree-5 fruit 0.027 → 0.301) lives in the HiGH trainer. `fruit_lod_cell.sh` = the v3 recipe trained by `ns-train high` with fruit-protect (tau 3.0) + fruit-densify (gate 0.04 m, max 0.5 m, split threshold 0.04 m through the dataparser scale, tail 0, stop-split 15k), bilateral grid, feature loss warmed-up off, supervision from the native cell; then census → seed B → E1 verdicts (identity_fd) → fruit-picked frames with the level and mixed rules. Decisive test: held-out fruit IoU/recall vs the stock v3 cell on the same frames, trees/rows unchanged. Started on 05 1_0, then 04 0_0 (shares the card with the phone fleet and the row-fix cascade; disk 56 G free, Paul's `deprecate_20261013.sh` not yet run).
- **Mask-size LoD (Paul 18:0x: "increase LoD for small masks … like mAP small/large on COCO … HiGH keeps relevancy and rendering for agricultural produce zero shot"):** (1) `masked_psnr_by_size.py` — PSNR inside the supervision masks by object size (E1 strata + COCO pixel buckets), grid-applied census views and raw held-out. **Stock 05 1_0: census views 24.29 dB over the frame but 16.87 inside the fruit masks (< 0.2 % stratum); held-out 14.44 frame, 13.30 fruit, 12.51 for objects < 0.2 % of the frame, 12.90 for COCO-small (< 32² px)** — the small-object rendering gap the LoD must close. (2) The HiGH trainer now takes `HIGH_LOD_MAX_PX`: `HighDataloader.get_small_mask` flags the pixels of any instance covering ≤ that many pixels of the frame (from the transport id maps), the datamanager passes it as `batch["lod_small"]`, and the model's protection tally treats those pixels like fruit pixels — class-agnostic LoD; the fruit-keyed run is the special case. `fruit_lod_cell.sh LOD_PX=1024` (= 32², COCO small at 1280×720) names its run `high_lod1024_fd_60000_bilateral` / `identity_lod1024_fd`. The 05 fruit-keyed run spent 9 min building its per-mask CLIP cache (2.9 G, `clip_cache_*`, added to the reclaim script as tier4) and is training.
- **Fleet stopped + disk reclaimed (Paul 17:4x: "stop the fleet on image farm then and reclaim the 62 gs"):** phone v3 fleet runner and its current cell (IMG_7960_s0, unfinished) stopped at 17:42 (resume command in `logs/v3_fleet.log`); `deprecate_20261013.sh` tier1 (ablation feature bins, 02/03 excluded while the cascade rebuilds them) + tier1m (ablation maps + LangSplat-lite targets) + tier2 (the census-0.7-era arms the cascade moved aside) → **free 46 G → 170 G (124 G reclaimed; all regenerable, the protocol files stay)**. Fruit-LoD 05 1_0 at step 1,000: 5,520 protected gaussians, 3,516 boosted this round (the split is bounded by the 4 cm–0.5 m gates; gaussian count watched).
- **Fruit-LoD, first run aborted (tau 3.0, 17:32–17:45):** `fruit_protect_tau 3.0` is the 1,500-step side-car refine's value; in a 60k run from scratch protection accrues over every epoch (max mass 127) and the protected set went 304 → 5,520 → 38,888 → 117,903 → 312,361 → **558,323 of 834 k gaussians by step 3,000**, boosts 216 → 40,175 per round, gaussians 952 k at step 2,300 — blanket densification of everything a fruit sightline grazes, not level of detail on small objects. Relaunched (17:49) with **tau 30, min-frames 3**: step 500 protected 7, boosted 1 (selective); `fruit_lod_cell.sh` takes FP_TAU/FP_MINFR, LOD_PX for the general small-mask rule, and `env` for the variable prefix (two launches failed on a trailing comment that swallowed the line and on `${LOD_PX:+VAR=…}` not being an assignment). Chain: 05 1_0 fruit-keyed → 04 0_0 → 05 1_0 LOD_PX=1024 → pepper LOD_PX=1024 (`logs/lod_chain.sh`).
- **Fruit-LoD tau 30 also saturates (18:0x, stopped at step ~3,000):** protected 7 → 256 → 2,834 → 5,653 → 27,692 → **171,707 by step 3,000, boosts 45,972** — the tally is cumulative, so a higher tau only delays the blanket. Added `fruit_protect_share` to the HiGH model: a gaussian is protected only when its fruit alpha mass is at least that share of its TOTAL alpha mass over the same frames (one more autograd pass per step; the "mostly fruit" carrier criterion from the primitive-mixing diagnosis, which the cumulative tau cannot express). Chain relaunched with share 0.3, tau 30, min-frames 3; aborted runs kept as `*_tau3_aborted` / `*_tau30_aborted`.
- **Measured fruit share per gaussian (share-0.1 run, step 500, 72 gaussians with fruit mass ≥ 10): p50 0.006, p90 0.020, p99 0.068, max 0.072** — at splatfacto's primitive size no gaussian renders more than 7 % orange by alpha mass; share 0.3 and 0.1 protect nothing (the mixing diagnosis, stated by the trainer). LoD must bootstrap from the most-fruity mixed carriers: relaunched with share 0.03 (≈ p95), tau 10, min-frames 3 — children inherit protection and, being smaller, should rise in share; the quantiles are printed every 500 steps to confirm.
- **Where the rendered fruit lives (05 1_0 stock splat, census W = 294 frames @ 640×360, 7 fruit words, 18:2x):** total fruit mass 10,413 px·alpha = **35 fruit px per census frame**; 25,713 gaussians touch fruit, 159 have ≥ 10 px·alpha in the epoch. The top-2,000 carriers render 82 % of the fruit mass with median share **0.037** (p10 0.010, p90 0.176) and median size **4.2 cm** (p10 1.4, p90 10.5); fruit-mass-weighted share median 0.054 → half of every rendered orange comes from gaussians that are ≥ 95 % leaf. Mass by carrier size: < 2 cm 25 %, 2–4 cm 27 %, 4–8 cm 31 %, 8–15 cm 14 %, > 15 cm 4 %. Trainer-side (share-0.03 run): protected 507 → 1,208 between steps 3k and 4k, boosted 9–54 (only the > 4 cm third of a set that already excludes the low-share half) → the run is effectively a no-LoD control (kept as V0: `high_fd_60000_bilateral`, 4 cm global split threshold). Chain wrapper killed before 04 0_0 (`lod_chain.sh`); the cell continues.
  - Why `tau` saturates: 60k steps / 294 frames = 204 epochs; an absolute px·alpha tally crosses any tau for every grazer eventually (tau 3 → 558 k protected by step 3k). Share is epoch-invariant; the carriers' share is 0.01–0.2, so **share 0.01** is the LoD membership (≈ 85 % of fruit mass), not 0.03/0.1/0.3.
  - Next: V1 = share 0.01, tau 10, split boost + global split threshold at **2 cm** (self-limiting: 7 cm → 4.4 → 2.7 → 1.7 cm, ≤ 8 children per carrier; expected +20–40 k gaussians); V2 = V1 + small-mask L1 weight (the existing `tree_weight` path, `HIGH_LOD_LOSS_W`). Scored by masked PSNR by size (stock vs V0 vs V1/V2) and the E4 level/mixed rules on the fruit frames.

- **CORRECTION (01:2x) — the "hidden behind leaves" explanation was wrong.** (a) A depth-based visibility census (`--visible-only`, centre within 2·scale + 3 cm of the rendered depth) dropped the carriers in the very views that show them (median contributor visibility 0.07) → rejected, outputs archived `*_depthtest_aborted`. (b) A transmittance-weighted census (`splat_census.py --vis-weight`: per-view weight × blend weight / unoccluded footprint) left the carriers' ratios unchanged (g677366 12.5 / 4.0 / 35.5 → 1.5 / 0.5 / 4.9 fruit / tree 5 / unlabelled). (c) Per-view votes (`fruit_votes_fig.py` → `identity/figs/lod/fruit_votes_g677366.png`): g677366 gets weight in 46 views; its fruit votes come from kf_000023/25 (2.2 + 10.3), its unlabelled votes from views of OTHER passes (kf_003273/75/77: 2.4 / 4.6 / 16.1, kf_000402/404, kf_002891) where it is VISIBLE (0.05–0.23) and projects onto the hedge / aisle beside tree 5's trunk, not onto an orange. In kf_000025 it sits at 2.45 m while the rendered surface at its pixel is 1.79 m (alpha 0.998) → **a blob 0.6 m behind the leaves that hold the orange: it paints the orange from one viewpoint and background from the others.** The majority rule withholding it is correct; the 10 % rule giving it the fruit word is the cross-view leak. Fruit identity needs the orange's blobs at the right 3D place (depth on fruit, or seed fruit from the triangulated 3D oranges) — not a census reweight. Hypothesis (untested): the LoD colour weight also rewards such single-view blobs, consistent with its gain being training-view only.

Stock @30k baseline added to `logs/lod_mix_table.md` (purity 0.11 / 0.39, E4 10 % 0.30 / 0.19, majority 0.00 / 0.01). **Conclusion of the night:** the identity LoD levers that act on the existing geometry (colour weight, mixing split, splat-depth seeding) do not make fruit-native blobs; placement must come from the views that label the fruit (triangulated mask centres), judged only on those views, seeded after the densification window — Paul's framing at 04:2x ("surely we'd just use 24 and 25"). Not started.
- **Arm D design (Paul 04:4x: "the depth is given by the neighbours — we have a tree, depth cannot come from behind"; 04:2x "surely we'd just use 24 and 25"):** (1) `fruit_seed_depth_from_neighbours` — seed depth = median rendered depth of the TREE-labelled ring (1–2.5 radii) around the mask, never deeper than the mask centre's own; (2) seeds tracked in `strategy_state["fp_seed"]`, opacity restored to 0.5 right after every gsplat reset (`reset_every` = 3000 steps, while refining) — the 3.1k batch died at the resets; (3) the mask-views fruit rule: `splat_census.py --fruit-views-npz` writes W2 (a view's votes for a gaussian count only if it touches a fruit mask there), `fruit_relabel_views.py` promotes gaussians with ≥ 50 % fruit among those votes to their fruit word (and demotes base fruit labels that fail), `reseed_from_labels.py` re-seeds; `lod_eval_ckpt.sh` now scores three seeds (10 %, labelled-majority, mask-views) + purity; `lod_mix_table.py` gained the mask-views column. Smoke (seed at 3100, reset at 6000) running; `logs/seed_chain3.sh` = arm D (ring depth + L1 10, 30k, 15k/30k) with every earlier arm re-scored under the mask-views rule alongside, then D's fate traces, the table and the kf 25 rule figure.

Fate at 30k: 164 of 620 within their radius (3.1k batch 38/324, 15.1k batch 126/296), moved 2.1 cm, size 1.8 cm, opacity 0.64, **22 of 164 fruit-majority by votes**. Purity 0.13 / 0.38 (stock @30k 0.11 / 0.39). E4 (census / held-out IoU): 10 % rule 0.36 / 0.22 (stock @30k 0.30 / 0.19, V2 0.46 / 0.21); labelled-majority 0.00 / 0.03; **mask-views majority 0.13 / 0.15**. Fruit PSNR 17.00 / 19.29 (stock @30k 16.44 / 18.57). **Mask-views rule across arms (census / held-out):** stock @30k 0.08 / 0.06, stock @60k 0.09 / 0.09, V2 0.29 / 0.15, mix A 0.26 / 0.08, mix B 0.28 / 0.08, C1 0.22 / 0.09, C2d 0.07 / 0.14, D 0.13 / 0.15 — recovers fruit relative to the strict majority (0.00–0.08) but stays below the 10 % rule (V2 0.46 / 0.21) at similar precision (0.65 vs 0.62). **Night's conclusion:** no training-side mechanism (colour weight, mixing split, splat-depth seeding, ring-depth seeding) made fruit-native blobs at this fruit size (15–25 px): blobs on a 20-px orange also cover its rim pixels and get densified / moved by the colour loss like any other; the colour weight is the one lever with a measurable gain (rendering +3.9 dB, identity 0.21 → 0.46 census under the 10 % rule). Figures `identity/figs/lod/lod_seedD_rules_kf25.png`, `seed_fate_D_{15000,29999}.png`; table `logs/lod_mix_table.md`.
- **Arm E design (Paul 06:3x: "spring multiple gaussians on the mask pixels", exclusive — "blot out the other blobs" on the fruit's own pixels; "go ahead"):** `fruit_seed_tile_px` 6 — a grid of blobs over each mask's interior (one per 6×6 px cell of the seeding view, ≈ 7–12 per orange), each coloured by the pixels under it, sized 3 px × 0.5 in world units, all at the instance's tree-ring depth; `fruit_seed_fix_opacity` (0.95 re-imposed every step, not trained); `fruit_seed_no_densify` (the seeds' means2d grad / absgrad zeroed before the strategy accumulates them → never duplicated or split); `fruit_seed_knockdown` 0.3 (blobs with centre inside the mask within 2 radii of the seed depth have their opacity × 0.3 at seeding); position / size / colour trainable; instance-level dedupe across views. `fruit_lod_cell_v7.sh`; `logs/seed_chain4.sh` = arm E (+ L1 10, 30k, 15k/30k) → evaluator (3 rules + purity) → per-tile fate → table → kf 25 rule figure. - **Arm F — guided split (Paul 17:xx: "can the split ensure we have gaussians split into the fruit itself"; "go ahead"):** `fruit_guided_split` — the fast tally gains 5 channels (normalised ray coords of each blob's fruit px, mass and ray coords of its tree px) → per blob, the 3-D point of its fruit paint and of its tree paint (on the pixel rays at the blob's own camera depth, summed over views); every 1000 steps until 25k a mixed blob (labelled diet 5–90 % fruit, ≥ 2 labelled mass, ≥ 1 cm) is replaced via gsplat `duplicate` by a fruit child at its fruit point and a tree child at its tree point (moves capped at 2 sizes, scales / 1.6, tallies reset, sizes held). Two fixes from the smokes: (1) tallies reset after every round (`fruit_guided_window`); (2) **anchor reset** — gsplat's split hands the parent's sums to children sampled ~1 parent-σ away, so during 1k–3k densification 95 % of the moves hit the cap (both points > 2 sizes from the blob, 1.4 sizes apart = stale ancestor positions); a blob that drifts > 0.5 size from where its sums started now restarts them. Offline check on stock 30k: fruit / tree points 0.59 / 0.22 sizes from the blob centre. Smoke after the fix: 10–15 splits at 1k–3k, 148 at 4k (fruit child moved 2.5 cm, tree child 1.2 cm, separation 0.74 size, 1 % capped). `fruit_lod_cell_v8.sh`; `logs/guided_chain.sh` launched 18:xx (F = guided + L1 10, 30k; evaluator at 15k/30k; table; kf 25 rule figure; orange coverage figure vs stock @30k).
