- **FruitNeRF through our pipeline (Paul 22:1x: "run HiGH on this and see how our registry goes"):** `automation/fruitnerf/` — `prep_images.py` / `fruitnerf_structure.sh` (ALL frames, 1620×1080 working set — the prod recipe's 300-frame cap would cut the orbit), our SfM: GLOMAP registered tree_01 359/359 but only **194/366 (tree_02) and 220/422 (tree_03)** → switched all three trees to FruitNeRF's own COLMAP poses (`fruitnerf_poses.py`: 345 / 357 / 413 posed; their OPENCV distortion removed — working set undistorted with a new pinhole K, alpha 0; per-tree intrinsics differ: f 2433 / 2372 / 2384 px at 1080). SAM3 image mode 'apple' (min mask 100 px; `build_tree_instances.py` lists only `kf_<K>.png`, so `prod/scratch_sam3` gets symlinks), DA3 windowed depth (`--dmax 30`), then `fruit_registry_count.py`: lift each detection (mask centroid at the median depth of its eroded mask), single-linkage at 1.0 × the median apple radius (apparent radius × depth / f — scale-free), ≥ 3 detections per apple, count apples within 0.5 × the median camera distance of the vertical axis through the cameras' look-at point (pre-registered; a sensitivity table over eps 0.75–1.5, min views 2–5, crop 0.4–0.6 is printed, not used to choose). Hand counts: 179 / 113 / 291.
- **FruitNeRF registry, pre-registered settings (done 23:25, reported 00:4x):** detections lifted 18,900 / 17,822 / 24,948 (≈ 55 per frame); apples counted **590 / 596 / 1,174 vs hand counts 179 / 113 / 291 (+230 / +427 / +303 %)**. Top-down (`high/tree_0X/fruit_registry/registry_topdown.png`): the target tree separates cleanly from the background rows (the crop is not the issue); inside it every apple fragments into several clusters. Cause: single-view DA3 depth is uncertain along the ray by several apple radii at 4–7 scene units (apple radius 0.03–0.06 units, cameras 121–128 radii away), so lifted points of one apple scatter beyond eps = 1 radius. No setting in the sensitivity grid is right on all three (e.g. ≥ 5 views: 264 / 224 / 441 — off in different directions); not tuned against the hand counts. Next: associate detections by multi-view reprojection (an apple's centre must project inside its mask in every view that detected it, depth-consistent), i.e. the triangulated "confirmed fruit" logic of our citrus registry.
- **Triangulated registry v1 (01:2x, `automation/fruitnerf/fruit_registry_tri.py`, tree_01):** depth sweep along each detection's ray (DA3 × 0.7–1.3, 161 steps) maximising the number of frames whose masks contain the point's projection with depth agreement ≤ 10 %; support median 29 frames (p90 75); the best depth moved the DA3 depth by −7 … +10 % (p10 / p90). Greedy claiming then founded 1,739 apples, **646 confirmed (≥ 3 views, reprojection ≤ 6 px, parallax ≥ 2°) vs 179 (+261 %)**, median 4 views per confirmed apple → still fragments: a candidate whose hits are partly claimed founded a new apple from the leftovers. v2 (running on all three trees): merge rule — a candidate joins the apple that already claims ≥ 30 % of its hits (pre-registered, structural, not fitted to the counts); sweep outputs cached (`fruit_registry/tri_cache.npz`).
- **Registry v2 / v3 on FruitNeRF (01:4x–02:1x), pre-registered each time, none right:** v2 (greedy + merge into the apple holding ≥ 30 % of the hits): founded 568 / 642 / 707 (250 / 211 / 407 inside the tree with ≥ 3 views), confirmed (reprojection ≤ 6 px, parallax ≥ 2°) 53 / 56 / 107 vs 179 / 113 / 291 — and 65–72 % of its apples claim two masks of the same frame (over-merging touching apples; median reprojection 12–17 px). v3 (`fruit_registry_cl.py`: single linkage on the swept points at 2 radii with a same-frame cannot-link): 694 / 589 / 1,038 (+288 / +421 / +257 %); the cannot-link blocks ~45 % of the candidate merges; median 10–14 views per apple vs 29 frames of support per detection → still fragmenting. **Detections are not the problem:** per sampled frame SAM3 gives ~1.5× the connected components of FruitNeRF's own fruit masks (657 / 470, 574 / 380, 731 / 448 over 12 frames per tree; their components merge touching apples), only ~5 % nested duplicates. **The problem is association** of 30–100 sightings per apple when apples touch: DA3 depth can't separate neighbours along the ray, and the projection test cross-links touching apples. Next (planned, not another rule): associate along the orbit — consecutive frames are ~1° apart, so frame-to-frame tracking (mask overlap after projection) is nearly unambiguous — then merge tracks across occlusions with the cannot-link; this is how the citrus confirmed-fruit registry worked (SAM3 tracks + triangulation).

## 2026-10-15
- **Housekeeping (01:4x, Paul: "kill leftover process, code commit"):** stopped the 8-Oct `fast_recipe_chain.sh` GPU-memory sampler (its `while pgrep … || sleep 20` never ends; 7 days of appends). Commits (not pushed): ujamaa-ops main 5cbeb25 / 9565075 / bd9a8c1 / 208682b / a038d70 (paper E1–E7 tooling, fruit LoD experiments, dataset downloader fixes, FruitNeRF registry, notebook); aru_sil_core/src exp/2026-07-14-week dfdd392f (Euclidean twin, census label dump, paper numbers); high exp/2026-07-14-week ed37fec (mask-size LoD + guided split in the trainer).
- **Correction — FruitNeRF frames are NOT a dense sequential orbit:** consecutive posed frames differ by a median 12–14° in view direction (p95 46–48°, max 62–142°) and jump up to 5–12 scene units (several heights / passes) — my "consecutive frames ~1° apart" was a guess, now measured false. Frame-to-frame tracking in capture order is therefore out. New route (FruitNeRF's own logic, with our tools): train a splat per tree, census the SAM3 apple masks through it (fruit vs everything else, labelled votes), take the fruit gaussians and cluster them in 3-D — the splat does the multi-view association.
- **BUG FOUND (02:3x) — no "+L1" arm since 10-14 01:xx had the colour weight.** When the mixing arm added an `elif` (frames without fruit still tally), the `HIGH_LOD_LOSS_W` block ended up inside that branch (frames WITHOUT fruit), so it never weighted anything; caught because the new FruitNeRF smoke printed `lod-loss frames=0`. Affected: mix B, seed C2 / C2d / D / E and **guided F at 30k and 60k (05 + 04) — all ran without the weight**. Unaffected: the colour LoD V1/V2/V3/px1024 (10-13, older code; V2's log shows the weight lines). Re-reading: F's identity gains are the guided split ALONE; its rendering deficit vs V2 (−1.9 dB 05, −4.5 dB 04) is the missing weight, not size-holding; "both levers in one run" has never been tested. Fixed (`high_model.py`, weight block back under the fruit branch; smoke: `lod-loss frames=501` by step 500). Same session: `HIGH_FRUIT_MASK_DIR` (uint8 masks 2 fruit / 1 labelled non-fruit, read directly — no embedder; `automation/fruitnerf/fruit_masks.py`) for off-pipeline data; FruitNeRF smoke: 345/345 masks, guided split at 1k on 24,781 mixed blobs.
- **FruitNeRF splat registry chain (`logs/fruitnerf_high_chain.sh`, launched 02:4x, waits for the plain splats):** `automation/fruitnerf/fruit_registry_splat.py` (pre-registered: fruit gaussians = fruit share of labelled mass ≥ 0.5 and fruit mass ≥ 1; single linkage at 1 apple radius; apple = cluster painting fruit in ≥ 3 views; tree crop 0.5) on (1) the plain v3 splats (baseline) and (3) HiGH with guided split + colour weight 10 (both levers, weight bug fixed) read from `fruit_masks/` (30k, checkpoints 15k/30k, guided floor 0.3 apple radius in model units).
- **FruitNeRF splat registry results (chain done 04:35; reported 09:5x):** plain v3 splats (30k, DA3 init, all frames): **366 / 358 / 874 vs 179 / 113 / 291 (+105 / +217 / +200 %)** — 21–25 k fruit gaussians, 2–3 per apple cluster, 24–25 views per apple. HiGH guided split + colour weight (weight verified: `lod-loss frames=29501`): **tree_01 323 (+80 %)** — 75 k fruit gaussians, 11 per apple, 20 views per apple. **trees 02 and 03 HiGH: CUDA OOM at 8.0k / 7.5k steps** — three runs in parallel (13 GB each) while the guided split grew N to 1.9–2.3 M (60–70 k splits per round at 6–8k; these apples are far larger in frame than citrus oranges, so far more mixed blobs). Not complete: 02/03 to rerun two at a time; and the count is still well above the hand count even on HiGH → the registry over-splits apples (clusters at 1 apple radius).
- **FruitNeRF counting — goal and target (10:0x, Paul: "Match fruitnerf counts", "Exclude lod for these apples"):** FruitNeRF's own real-tree numbers, from the LaTeX source of arXiv 2408.06190v2 (Fig. 8 table, `SAM / SAM-B / U-Net / U-Net-B / GT`): tree_01 147 / 173 / 146 / 172 / **179**, tree_02 86 / 112 / 88 / 114 / **113**, tree_03 190 / 264 / 255 / 243 / **291**; "detection rate" = count / GT averaged over the four variants (≈ 89 / 88 / 82 %); images 1000 × 1500 px; **the point cloud is cropped by hand to the tree of interest**; DBSCAN + template-Hausdorff clustering, ≤ 6 fruits per cluster. Their default model sits at −18 / −24 / −35 %, FruitNeRF-Big at −3 / −1 / −9 %.
- **LoD exclusion for large fruit (`HIGH_FRUIT_MAX_PX`, high_model.py + high_datamanager.py + high_dataloader.py, uncommitted):** fruit instances above the cutoff become unlabelled, so the tally, the guided split and the colour weight are blind to them; mask-dir path = connected components at load (scipy.ndimage.label, prints `fruit instances excluded`), id-map path = `get_small_mask(…, big=True)` → `batch["lod_big"]` masked out of `fruit_px` and `lab_px` (and the feature-path `fruit_px`). Measured FruitNeRF apple masks (1620 × 1080, every 12th frame): instance area p10 / median / p90 = 233 / 750 / 2,605 px (equivalent diameter 31 px), 44–51 instances per frame, 2.9–3.6 % of the pixels; 62–67 % of the instances are ≤ the COCO-small 1,024 px (frame 25 of tree_01: 14 of 20 above). Citrus oranges are 15–25 px → ~180–490 px, so a 1,024-px cutoff keeps the citrus LoD and leaves these apples alone. FruitNeRF counting uses the **plain v3 splats** (no LoD); the OOM'd HiGH runs on trees 02/03 are not rerun.
- **Registry v5 = mask-linked (`automation/fruitnerf/fruit_registry_masklink.py`, chain `logs/fruitnerf_masklink_chain.sh`, 10:07–10:20):** the SAM3 masks define the instances, the splat ties the views: fruit gaussians (fruit share of labelled census mass ≥ 0.5, mass ≥ 1) are projected into every training view and, where in frame and VISIBLE (centre depth within 5 % of the rendered depth), vote for the mask they land in (votes cached: 0.55–0.67 M per tree); two gaussians are linked when they share a mask in ≥ 2 views and in more views than they fall in different masks of one view (cannot-link); apples = components seen in ≥ 3 views inside the crop. Census 76–95 s per tree (3.5 / 2.6 / 2.4 M gaussians, 21–24 k fruit gaussians). A first star-linking version gave 127 on tree_01 at the pre-registered crop; the clique + cannot-link version 128 → the linking was never the problem, the **crop** was: 0.5 × median camera distance is the citrus-walk constant. Counts (link ≥ 2 + cannot-link, ≥ 3 views) per crop: **tree_01 128 (0.5 ring) / 179 (camera ring = 1.0) / 179 (row cut); tree_02 116 / 279 / 81; tree_03 205 / 244 / 244** vs 179 / 113 / 291. Sensitivity at the ring (tree_01): link ≥ 1 → 139, link ≥ 3 → 203, no cannot-link → 158, ≥ 2 views → 195, ≥ 5 views → 169. Component sanity: apple extent median 0.0086–0.0098 cam_r (RMS; ≈ a 3–4 cm radius if the cameras stood 3 m out), nearest-neighbour median 0.063 cam_r, 8 touching pairs on tree_01, 0 closer than one radius. **GT-free mask check (`masklink_mask_check.py`):** of all SAM3 apple masks over all views, 63 / 61 / 67 % are explained by exactly one counted apple, 1–2 % by two or more (splits or touching pairs), 2 % only by uncounted fragments, and 31–36 % hold no visible fruit gaussian — those are the small ones (median 418–505 px vs 974–1,103 for the explained; 49–61 % ≤ 500 px: background and neighbouring-tree apples, small detections). Figures: `fruit_registry/registry_masklink_topdown_plain30k.png` (top view: apples, cameras, components outside the crop) and `masklink_masks_<k>_plain30k.png` (one view, masks outlined by status). **Maps: trees 01 and 03 have their row neighbours outside the camera ring (next trees ≈ 2 cam_r away) → the ring is the tree; tree_02's neighbours stand at ±0.65–0.9 cam_r, inside the ring, canopies interlocking** (along-row apple density: peaks 53 / 54 at ∓0.6 / +0.7, the target's 26–33 around 0, minima 22 / 15 at −0.3 / +0.3 → the row cut gives 81). The crop is the one free rule and was changed after looking at the maps (0.5 → ring → row cut → trunks), never at the counts; tree_03 was the untouched test of the ring rule (244, −16 %, in line with FruitNeRF-Big's 243–264).
- **Trunk-based per-tree crop (the automatic stand-in for FruitNeRF's manual crop; `fruit_registry_trunks.py`, SAM3 'tree trunk' per tree in `prod/bateleur/sam3_trunk`, 1.1 min per tree):** trunk masks censused through the splat (share ≥ 0.5), trunk gaussians below the median apple height, 2-D KDE (0.05 cam_r) peaks ≥ 20 % of the strongest, 0.3 cam_r apart = trunks; an apple belongs to the nearest trunk; peaks within 0.4 cam_r of the target's trunk (support posts, forks) count as the target. tree_01: 98 k trunk gaussians, one peak at 0.38 cam_r from the look-point (the trunk map shows the trunk at the look-point plus three post-like clusters — the strongest is a post), all 179 ring candidates assigned to it → **179**. Trees 02/03 running (`logs/fruitnerf_trunks_post.sh`).
- **Trunk route, result (10:25):** mass-weighted peaks found only the target's trunk on every tree (the near trunk's big gaussians drown the rest); one vote per gaussian + a 10 % floor: tree_02 finds the left neighbour's trunk at −0.84 cam_r (the right one at ≈ +0.85 is visible in the map but under the floor) → 180; tree_03's second peak (+0.64, +0.46; a dense vertical structure with no canopy of its own — a post, like tree_01's strongest "trunk" at 0.40 cam_r next to the real trunk) takes 41 of the target's apples → 203. SAM3 'tree trunk' returns posts as readily as trunks, so **trunk-Voronoi is not adopted**; the trunk census stays as corroboration of tree_02's neighbours (`registry_trunks_topdown_plain30k.png`).
- **Protocol of record (GT-blind, `fruit_registry_masklink.py --crop 1.0` + `masklink_row_cut.py`):** apples = mask-linked components seen in ≥ 3 views, inside the camera ring and — along the row axis — inside the canopy-density minima toward a substantial neighbouring canopy (≥ 1.5 × the minimum and ≥ 25 % of the ring's densest point), else the ring. **Counts 179 / 81 / 244 vs hand counts 179 / 113 / 291 (0 / −28 / −16 %)**; FruitNeRF default 147 / 86 / 190–255 (−18 / −24 / −35…−12 %), FruitNeRF-Big 172–173 / 112–114 / 243–264 (−3…−4 / −1…+1 / −9…−16 %). Tree_01 exact, tree_03 at FruitNeRF-Big level (their U-Net-B: 243), tree_02 at FruitNeRF-default level — the tree whose row neighbours interlock inside the camera ring (their manual crop vs our density minima at ±0.3 cam_r) and whose unexplained SAM3 masks are the most (36 %). The unexplained masks are the SMALL ones everywhere (median 418–505 px) → the one principled lever left is the mask-size LoD on small fruit instances only (`HIGH_FRUIT_MAX_PX=1024`: 33–38 % of the instances excluded, no OOM), scored by the mask check's explained share, not by the hand count. Not launched (Paul's call: GPU ≈ 1 h per tree).
- **Small-fruit LoD on FruitNeRF (10:4x, Paul: "let's see if that closes the gap"; `logs/fruitnerf_lod_small_chain.sh`):** HiGH guided split + colour weight 10 on fruit instances ≤ 1,024 px only (`HIGH_FRUIT_MAX_PX=1024`), one tree at a time (02, 03, then 01 as the control), one checkpoint per run; then the registry of record (`high_small30k`) + row cut + mask check by size class. Pre-registered decisive test: the small-mask explained share (plain splat: see the `by size` lines below) rises and the count moves toward the hand count while tree_01 stays ≈ 179; nothing is tuned on the counts.
- **Small-fruit LoD, tree_02 (15:00; `high_small30k`, 35 min, no OOM, weight on 29,336 of 30k frames, 5,414 of 15,700 instances excluded):** detection moved, the count did not — small masks explained by one counted apple 48 → 54 %, unexplained 48 → 35 %, large 86 → 89 %, 3.4× the fruit gaussians (72 k); but ring 279 → 246 and row cut 81 → 57: the denser fruit gaussians bridge touching apples under single linkage (component extent 0.0096 → 0.0136 cam_r, 17 counted apples sharing a mask in > 50 % of their views vs 5).
- **Merge-check variants (15:1x–15:5x, Paul: "go ahead"; all GT-blind, all scored by the mask check; rejected):** the components of record hide clusters — tree_01's three largest (1,064–1,537 gaussians) have members in ≥ 3 distinct masks in 76–79 % of views and 85 % of their member pairs are seen apart more often than together — so the 179 / 81 / 244 of record carry compensating errors (clusters counted as one vs fragments). (1) **Signed-graph split** (Fiedler cut accepted when cross-part different-mask views exceed same-mask views, recursive): tree_01 179 → 402, "≥ 2 apples per large mask" 2 → 42 %, apples sharing masks in a median 80–97 % of their views — a leaf pieces ONE apple into several masks consistently across views, so "different masks" ≠ "different apples". (2) Same with a tight visibility tolerance (0.015 cam_r ≈ 1.2 apple radii instead of 5 % of depth = 15 cm): votes −40 %, small-mask "none" 47 → 63 %: the splat's fruit gaussians are smeared along the rays by more than an apple radius. (3) **FruitNeRF-style size template** (e0 = median RMS extent of single apples 0.0085–0.0098 cam_r; components wider than 1.5 e0 k-means-split, 0.03 cam_r tolerance): 286 / 148 / 492, ≥ 2 apples per large mask 20–33 % — the depth smear makes single apples elongated. (4) **Targeted giant split** (only components whose members hit ≥ 3 masks in the median view; k-means with k = that median; 7–17 per tree): **205 / 88 / 289** vs 179 / 113 / 291 and the LoD tree_02 76 — the counts move toward the hand counts on 02/03 and away on 01, but one-apple-per-large-mask drops 86 → 69 / 81 / 72 % and ≥ 2 apples rises 2 → 19 / 7 / 17 %, so by the GT-free metrics it over-splits inside the clusters and is not adopted. `--merge-check {none,giants,size}` kept in the script, default **none** = protocol of record. Conclusion: the splat's depth precision on fruit (≈ 5–15 cm, worse than an apple) is what limits the registry — mask co-membership and 3-D extent both fail as a split cue; the LoD adds fruit gaussians but not depth precision.
- **Small-fruit LoD, tree_03 (15:38, 36 min, 8,573 of 21,478 instances excluded) and the verdict:** under the protocol of record (all dumps restored to it, `logs/fruitnerf_masklink_record.log`): small masks explained by one apple 53 → 60 % (tree_02 48 → 54 %), unexplained 44 → 31 % (48 → 35 %), large masks 86 → 89 % on both — the LoD detects more small apples on every tree it ran on; counts: tree_03 ring 244 → 211 (the LoD splat's candidate profile even grew a canopy minimum at −0.47 cam_r → row cut 175), tree_02 row cut 81 → 57. Fruit gaussians per apple 29 → 99 / 54 → 77 (median), extents larger: the extra detections merge into existing components under single linkage rather than becoming apples. **The small-fruit LoD does not close the gap; it moves detection, and the registry cannot turn that into count without depth precision better than an apple.** tree_01 control running (15:40 →).
- **Small-fruit LoD, tree_01 control (16:17, 37 min, 6,106 of 16,141 instances excluded; chain done 16:19):** 172 vs plain 179 (hand count 179) — the count holds within 4 % where no clusters merge; the same detection gain as on 02/03: small masks explained by one apple 49 → 55 %, unexplained 47 → 35 %, large 86 → 88 %. Final LoD table (record protocol): 172 / 57 / 211 (ring on 03; row cut 175) vs plain 179 / 81 / 244 vs hand 179 / 113 / 291. GPU idle; nothing queued. Outputs `outputs/high_small1024_30000_bilateral` per tree (one checkpoint each) + the OOM'd `high_guidedW_30000_bilateral` dirs from 02:4x are candidates for cleanup (Paul's rm).
- **How the under-count happens (16:3x–17:0x, Paul: "we seem to be undercounting on two and three", "show me how this is happening", "we are going to bridge the gap, no shortcuts"):** two mechanisms, both measured. **(1) Clusters counted once:** `masklink_cluster_fig.py` — tree_03 component 49 (2,110 gaussians, members in 6 distinct masks per view) spans a dozen cleanly-masked apples and counts as ONE; tree_03 has 14 such components (3–6 masks per view, sizes 545–2,110), tree_01 nine (one with 8 masks per view), tree_02 seven. Single linkage bridges touching apples because the 5 %-of-depth visibility test (15 cm at 3 m, twice an apple) lets a gaussian behind a neighbour vote for the neighbour's mask. A new dominant-share metric (top counted apple ≥ 80 % of a mask's counted votes) is 99–100 % for the record (trivially: clusters are one apple) and 94–98 % with the targeted giant split (contested 2–5 %), so that split was not clearly over-splitting either — the "exactly one apple per mask" metric that rejected it is itself biased toward merging. **(2) Masks with no fruit gaussian = mixed primitives:** `masklink_miss_audit.py` on tree_03 (52 views) — the painters of the un-voted masks have census fruit share median 0.25 (88 % between 0.1 and 0.5, 5 % below 0.1 = false positives, 7 % ≥ 0.5 = depth-rejected), full alpha coverage; the voted masks' painters 0.55. Lifted by the rendered depth, 63–76 % of the un-voted masks lie inside the camera ring: 11–14 missed masks per view against 31–40 explained → **26–29 % of the apple masks inside each tree have no fruit gaussian**, the citrus mixed-primitive problem (share threshold 0.5). Trunk peaks down to 3 % (row coordinates): tree_02 target + one neighbour at +0.84 along the row; tree_03 target + a peak at −0.79 along the row (on the row axis → possibly a real neighbour trunk inside the ring, not a post); tree_01 a post at +0.40.
- **Route, no shortcuts — the MASK-GRAPH registry (`fruit_registry_maskgraph.py` + `_count.py`, chain `logs/fruitnerf_maskgraph_chain.sh`, 17:0x):** masks are the units. Stage 1 (GPU): for every SAM3 mask in every view, the exact alpha mass of every gaussian on that mask (one backward pass per mask at half resolution; a gaussian behind another apple contributes ~nothing — transmittance replaces the depth test; no share threshold — mixed painters count). Stage 2 (CPU): masks of different views are the same apple when their mass vectors agree (cosine ≥ θ), masks of the same view never are (exact cannot-link); greedy agglomeration by descending similarity; apple = cluster of masks from ≥ 3 views at the mass-weighted mean of the lifted centroids, inside the ring + row cut. Decisive test, stated before the run: the count-vs-θ curve shows a plateau; at that θ the three trees move toward the hand counts together (tree_01 may rise above 179 if its nine clusters hide apples that fragments compensated); the cluster figure redrawn on the mask-graph apples shows the dozen apples as a dozen.
- **Mask-graph registry, results (17:1x–17:5x; `fruit_registry_maskgraph.py` stage 1 = 2.5–3 min per tree, 1.5–1.9 M (mask, gaussian) mass entries, ≈ 78 contributors per mask at ≥ 0.2 % of the mask mass):** two association rules on top of it, both rejected by GT-free checks. (a) Greedy mask agglomeration under the one-mask-per-view constraint (`_count.py`): 806 → 1,441 on tree_03 and 652 → 938 on tree_01 across θ 0.05–0.6, no plateau, 8–9 views per "apple" — one early wrong merge between touching apples blocks every later correct merge, so each apple's masks fragment into chains. (b) Louvain communities of gaussians by mask-mass profile (`fruit_registry_community.py`, igraph in `/home/paperspace/venvs/leiden`): the count is a dial (tree_01 157 / 226 / 265 / 303 / 359 / 419 at resolution 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 / 6 / 8; tree_03 114 / 193 / 247 / 291 / 354 / 421; tree_02 175 / 242 / 295 / 326 / 387 / 364 (row cut)) with no plateau; owned-masks-per-view is 1.0 from resolution 2 up (merges gone) but the communities' mass-weighted RMS extent is 6–8 apple radii at every resolution (85–94 % wider than 1.2 radii) — they are not apples: the gaussians that render a mask (surface + boundary + context) are not exclusive to one apple, so gaussian-level association cannot resolve touching apples whatever the clustering. Stage 1 stays (exact per-mask mass is useful for ownership and the crop); association by gaussians is closed.
- **Route (17:5x): multi-view tracking-by-detection over the orbit.** Masks are detections, apples are 3-D hypotheses: views ordered by orbit angle; per view the live hypotheses are projected, masks assigned by Hungarian matching gated by the mask radius, assigned masks refine the hypothesis by triangulation (12–14° baselines → cm precision, unlike the splat's 5–15 cm smear), unassigned masks start hypotheses from the splat-lifted centroid; apple = hypothesis with ≥ 3 assigned views inside the ring + row cut; duplicates (same apple started twice) merged when closer than one radius with disjoint views. Decisive test: count-vs-gate plateau, nearest-neighbour distances ≥ 2 radii, tree_01 ≈ 179 without calibration.
- **How twelve apples become one registry apple (19:4x, Paul: "focus on those 12 clear apples … how does that happen?"), tree_03 component 49 (2,110 gaussians, 13 apples with ≥ 10 gaussians in image_287, centre-to-centre ≥ 1.1 radii, median 7.9):** of the 842 (view, mask) pairs its gaussians vote in, 299 hold gaussians of two or more of those apples — **bridge masks of median 1,884 px (62 % above two apples' worth; one apple ≈ 800 px here): SAM3 masks that cover two touching apples in that view.** Inside such a mask the dominant apple has 91 % of the gaussians; the other apple's 2–10 gaussians sit 2.5 radii from the dominant centre (86 % beyond 1.5 radii) — on the other apple, legitimately inside the merged mask, not depth leaks. 363 of the 1,024 gaussians (35 %) bridge in a median of 2 views; two co-views satisfy the link rule, and single linkage walks the contacts. Diagnostic `bridge_diag_49.npz`. Mask area cannot flag merged masks (area / expected single-apple area at the lifted depth is unimodal, mode 0.3–0.4, truncation by leaves spreads it over a factor 20; a per-view median rule discarded 70–75 % of the votes). **Fix on the mechanism: a physical distance cap on links** (`--link-max-r`, apple radius 0.0125 cam_r): two gaussians of one apple are ≤ 2 radii apart, bridging pairs 2–3; cap 3 radii = 2 + one radius of smear slack, fixed a priori. Counts (cap 2.5 / **3** / 3.5): tree_01 210 / **200** / 195, tree_02 (row cut) 105 / **96** / 92, tree_03 306 / **289** / 272 vs 179 / 113 / 291 → **+12 / −15 / −1 %**; GT-free metrics unchanged (dominant-share ≥ 80 % in 99–100 % of masks, contested 1 %, large masks explained by one apple 84–85 % vs 86 % of record, ≥ 2 apples 3–4 % vs 2 %). Residual: clusters of touching apples whose contacts are < 3 radii apart still chain (tree_03: 16 components with 3–5 masks per view, largest 1,291 gaussians); tree_01's +12 % is now fragments/duplicates uncovered when its clusters split. Mask-graph / community / tracking variants (17:1x–19:3x) all recorded above; none adopted.
- **Within-mask outlier rule (20:0x; votes > 2 radii from their mask's median gaussian position do not link, on top of the cap): rejected** — removes only 2–4 % of the votes but fragments apples (320 / 89 (ring 581) / 486; fruit gaussians per apple median 3–18; large masks with ≥ 2 counted apples 12–20 % vs 3–4 % with the cap alone): the far votes are the depth-smeared members that connect an apple's near and far groups, not just bridges. The cap-3 registry stands: 200 / 96 / 289 (`--link-max-r 3`), image_287 redrawn (`masklink_cluster_287_plain30k_cap3.png`): the tight cluster (contacts < 3 radii) still chains.
- **CORRECTION (20:3x, Paul: "these are separate masks no"):** the "SAM3 masks covering two touching apples" reading of the bridges was wrong — bridge masks are normal single-apple masks (pair m53–m23: bridge masks median 3,462 px vs the apple's own 5,783 px in image_287; I had compared against an 800-px far-apple figure). Correct mechanism, per pair (`bridge_evidence_49_43_35.png`, 9 contacts among the 13 apples, centres 1.1–3.5 radii apart): two touching apples; in views along their contact the back one is behind the front one, SAM3 gives one mask (the front apple's), and the back apple's contact gaussians vote inside it because the visibility test tolerates 5 % of depth = 15 cm, twice an apple; ≥ 2 such views = a link. Test: tolerance 0.03 cam_r (≈ 9 cm, `--depth-tol 0 --depth-tol-r 0.03`, tag `plain30k_d03`): **195 / 102 / 302 vs 179 / 113 / 291 (+9 / −10 / +4 %)** with the GT-free checks intact (dominant ≥ 80 % in 99–100 %, large masks one apple 82–83 % vs 86 %, ≥ 2 apples 1–2 %) but 15 % fewer votes and more unexplained masks (small "none" 51–57 % vs 44–48 %); the tight cluster in image_287 persists (1,851 gaussians, 5 masks per view): the contact gaussians of apples 7 cm apart stay inside any tolerance the gaussians' own 1.4-radii depth scatter allows. Paul's "does the data not have sam3 masks?": the dataset's Grounded-SAM / U-Net masks are binary fruit masks (touching apples one blob, 1.5× fewer components than our SAM3 instances).
- **Disambiguating touching apples (20:4x–21:0x, Paul: "so can we disambiguate them"):** (a) votes by RENDERED MASS instead of the depth test (`votes_from_mass.py`, ≥ 0.5 % of the mask mass, occlusion included; tag `plain30k_mass`): explains far more masks (large masks one apple 86 → 91–92 %, small 48–53 → 57–64 %, unexplained 44–48 → 27–32 %) but bridges MORE at contacts (image_287's cluster 14 masks per view; 187 / 74 / 239) — the boundary gaussians of the back apple genuinely render a sliver inside the front apple's mask. (b) Cluster split by the masks occupied per view: k = median gives 205 / 88 / 289 with contested masks 2–5 % (the best cluster-level disambiguation by the GT-free checks); k = p75 → 233 / 105 / 339 (contested 4–9 %), k = p90 → 259 / 135 / 385 (8–11 %, ≥ 2 apples per large mask 22–30 %): k-means on positions smeared by 1.4 radii carves apples arbitrarily once k exceeds the true count. Conclusion: at a contact the gaussian-level evidence (depth test, rendered mass, link length) is genuinely mixed because the fruit gaussians' own depth scatter (1.4 radii) is comparable to the contact distance (2 radii); the clusters can only be split by a count of simultaneously visible masks (median rule) or by geometry sharper than the gaussians (apple spheres fitted to the mask silhouettes across views — not built).
- **Per image (21:1x, Paul: "in a certain image we will have a registry number smaller than the number of masks right?" / "show the worst one"):** tree_02 per image: SAM3 masks median 50, registry apples visible 19, masks explained 30 → 11 masks per image belong to merged clusters, 17 have no registry apple (35 %); tree_03: 63 / 19 / 41, 17 unexplained. Worst by raw gap: image_8 (71 masks, 8 registry apples) — mostly windfalls on the ground and far trees (`worst_image_8.png`). Worst by in-ring masks: image_172 (86 masks lifted inside the ring, 27 registry apples: 17 own-apple, 25 under merged clusters, 44 none; `worst_intree_image_172.png`) — the white masks include windfalls on the ground inside the ring and plainly visible apples with no registry apple (the mixed-primitive misses). Median over images: in-ring masks minus registry apples = 23.
- **WINDFALLS (21:3x): a distinct layer of counted "apples" lies on the ground.** Height histogram of the counted apples (cam_r above the look-point, 0.025 bins): tree_01 has 49 apples at −0.60…−0.53 then three empty bins, tree_02 103 at −0.65…−0.55, tree_03 84 at −0.58…−0.53 — the soil (the worst in-tree image shows the windfalls, `worst_intree_image_172.png`). The hand count is apples on the tree. `--no-ground` (floor = first empty bin above the lowest height mode, GT-blind) removes 51 / 108 / 84 (record) and 51 / 112 / 91 (median split). **Tree-only counts: record 128 / 65 / 160, median split 154 / 74 / 198 vs 179 / 113 / 291 (split: −14 / −35 / −32 %)** — the "179 = 179" of record was windfalls compensating misses. The row cut now also fires on tree_01 (a left lobe at −0.6 along the row, 81 % of the central density; the trunk census had a weak peak there) and on tree_03 at 25 % (not at 50 %): the crop rule is fragile once the ground layer is gone; ring counts without the cut: record 128 / 171 / 160, split 154 / 182 / 198. The under-count on every tree is the detection gap — the masks with no registry apple (17 per image), plainly visible apples whose painters are mixed primitives.
- **Registry ≠ relevancy (22:0x, Paul: "gaussians fruit/leaf only matters for relevancy not registry"; "this merging is too permissive"):** (a) all rendering gaussians voting (no census share, `votes_from_mass.py --all`): every mask explained (none 0–1 %) but leaf fringes under the masks become ≥ 3-view "apples" (1,152 / 1,403 on 02/03; the ground rule misfires) — the component must dominate masks, not merely touch them. (b) The link rule measured on tree_03 #49 (same-apple pairs = same image_287 mask): same apple co-views median 25, conflicts 0; different apples co-views 0, conflicts 12; the record rule (co ≥ 2, co > conf) passes 0.6 % of different-apple pairs (2,725 bridges); **co ≥ 5 and co ≥ 5 × conflicts** keeps 92 % of same-apple pairs and passes 0.0 % (170). (c) **Dominance count:** a component's views are only those where it holds ≥ 50 % of some mask's votes (`--dominant`); with strict links alone, lone gaussians seen in 3 views had become apples (916 on tree_02). **Strict links + dominance + windfalls out (`--link-views 5 --co-ratio 5 --dominant --no-ground`): ring 172 / 167 / 237, row cut (peak 0.5) 172 / 117 / 237 vs 179 / 113 / 291 (−4 / +4 / −19 %)**; checks: dominant-share ≥ 80 % in 97–98 %, contested 2–3 %, large masks one apple 72–77 % (record 86 %), ≥ 2 apples 6–8 % (record 2 %) — some apples now split into pieces; 10 cluster components remain on tree_03 (3–5 masks per view). Record links + dominance: 119 / 60 / 155 (clusters intact, fragments gone). Figures `registry_colours_{287,172}_strict.png`.
- **Cluster residual, two more rules (23:xx; Paul: "when will the cluster-chaining residual land"):** (1) co/(co+conf) ≥ 0.9 with co ≥ 3 (42 measured bridges): 160 / 88 / 231 — loses apples, the 10 tree_03 clusters stay (a few surviving pairs are enough for single linkage). (2) Over-segment (co ≥ 3 & 0.9, or co ≥ 8 & 0.95) then merge sub-components only when aggregate same-mask views > different-mask views over all cross pairs (`--merge-check evidence`): 208 / 84 / 143 and 188 / 85 / 149 — the clusters re-form (13,410 of 17,917 piece pairs merged on tree_03): the contact gaussians vote for both apples from different directions, so any aggregate of their evidence says "same apple". **Registry of record tonight: strict links (co ≥ 5, co ≥ 5 × conf) + dominance count + windfalls out = 172 / 117 / 237 vs 179 / 113 / 291 (−4 / +4 / −19 %)**, residual = chained clusters on tree_03 (10 components of 3–5 masks per view) and some pieces (large masks explained by one apple 72–77 %). Uncommitted: all of today's registry scripts and the trainer's HIGH_FRUIT_MAX_PX.
- **Transitive-safe evidence merge (23:3x; Paul: "are the masks disappearing between views?" — no: in image_259 the pink pairs 59/64 and 77/80 are two apples 2.2–3.2 radii apart with ZERO direct links (3,551 co-visible pairs with 15 conflicts each) joined through third groups by single linkage; apple 47 is one apple pieced by leaves, 0.6–1.4 radii, correctly joined):** over-segment (co ≥ 8, co/(co+conf) ≥ 0.95) then agglomerate sub-components by descending aggregate evidence S − C, refusing any merge that would put two sub-components with C > S (a "different" pair) into one apple even via a third (`--merge-check evidence`, transitive-safe). **292 / 109 / 197 vs 291 / 113 / 179 (+0.3 / −4 / +10 %)**, tree_03's clusters 10 → 3 (3 masks per view); cost: contested masks 4–7 %, large masks with two counted apples 12–23 % (record 2 %) — pieces of one apple blocked apart by spurious "different" pairs (2,095 blocked merges on tree_03). Figures `registry_colours_{259,287}_evt.png`.
- **Fuji-SfM added to the analysis (Paul: "add this to the analysis then"):** the one public apple set with per-apple 3-D ground truth — 11 Fuji trees, 582 SfM images (Canon 60D, 5184 × 3456, 3 m distance), 1,455 apples as 3-D bounding boxes in the SfM point cloud (Gené-Mola et al. 2020, Zenodo 3712808, CC BY 4.0, 6.9 GB rar); FruitLangGS reports 1,443 found, FruitNeRF++ F1 0.765. No camera poses or intrinsics ship with it, so: our own SfM on the 582 images (the same frame as the splat), plain v3 splat, SAM3 'apple', registry of record (strict + transitive-safe evidence + dominance + no ground), then a similarity alignment of our SfM points to their point cloud (ICP) and per-apple matching to the 1,455 boxes → **precision and recall per apple**, which the three FruitNeRF trees cannot give. Decisive test: per-apple recall/precision of the registry of record, and of the record-of-this-morning rules, against the 3-D boxes; merges and pieces become measurable, not inferred. Download started (`logs/fuji_sfm_download.log`, 32 connections).

## 2026-10-16
- **Fuji-SfM transfer, east side (02:0x–02:4x; rules frozen as committed 98fc648, `--crop 0`):** structure 291/291 registered (SfM 10 min), SAM3 2 clips, DA3 291 maps, fruit 6.26 % of the pixels; splat 30k (25 GB on the card); registry of record: 336 k fruit gaussians, 5.5 M votes, 14,553 components, **1,069 apples** (677 inside the GT region); this morning's rules 782. Alignment of our SfM to their cloud: ICP similarity, scale 1.030, inlier RMS 5.5 cm (47 % inliers = one side); the model→COLMAP transform verified on the camera centres (0.0000). **Per-apple result at 0.15 m: TP 174, precision 0.26, recall 0.12, F1 0.16** (0.30 m: precision 0.61, recall 0.28); per-tree recall 10–15 % everywhere. Not alignment: residuals unbiased per row segment, a GT-aware ICP (diagnostic only) lifts the within-0.15 m share only 29 → 34 %; not depth: position error is isotropic (sd 0.15–0.18 m per axis); triangulated or lifted-centroid positions change nothing (26–30 % within 0.15 m). The GT apples are packed at a median 0.10 m nearest-neighbour distance (p10 0.07) — 130 apples per tree — and our registry apples sit ~0.2 m from the nearest GT apple: components straddle several apples. Comparators on this set: FruitLangGS 1,443 / 1,455 found, FruitNeRF++ F1 0.765.
- **Fuji east, where the per-apple score stands (03:3x):** the registry looks right in the images (`registry_colours_225_record.png`: 56 masks, 36 registry apples own a mask, 11 merged, 4 unexplained) and the GT-free checks are FruitNeRF-like (dominant-share ≥ 80 % in 97 %, large masks one apple 81 %, ≥ 2 apples 16 %, none 2 %), yet per-apple matching is poor and every registry variant and every 3-D clustering variant scores the same F1 ≈ 0.26–0.27 at 0.15 m. Decisive test: **their GT apple centres projected into our images through our camera + the alignment land a median 81 px (3 apple radii) from the nearest SAM3 mask** (`gt_projection_225.png`: red circles in the sky and beside the apples); the ICP's 5.5 cm RMS was a dense-cloud artefact. Neither a dense multi-scale ICP (inliers 8 % at 2 cm) nor a 7-DoF similarity fitted to put the GT on the masks across 94 images (15 → 16 % within 1 radius; it drifts to a 17° rotation) fixes it; GT-aware local fits per 2 m reach only 45 % within 0.15 m. The GT is consistent with their own cloud (reddish points within 6 cm: median 7 % vs 0 % at a 30 cm shift). So the two reconstructions differ non-rigidly (ours: single side, 291 images, no loop closure, 20 m row); the per-apple score cannot be read until the frames agree. Count-level (both sides merged) remains.
- **Fuji west + both sides (03:3x):** west registry of record 1,089 apples (400 k fruit gaussians, 5.3 M votes, 96,803 → 24,619 components after the transitive-safe merge), this morning's rules 766. Inside the GT box after the coarse alignment: east 677, west 649, merged across sides at 0.15 m → **796 vs 1,455**; per apple at 0.15 m precision 0.26 / recall 0.14 (F1 0.19), at 0.30 m 0.60 / 0.33 (F1 0.43) — all of it conditioned on the frame alignment that puts their apples 3 radii off our masks, so neither the region filter nor the cross-side merge is trustworthy. How the literature evaluates on Fuji: FruitLangGS reports a count ratio only (1,443 / 1,455, no spatial matching, no alignment described); FruitNeRF++ matches in 3-D after COLMAP per side + manual registration of the sides and blames "noisy poses and manual registration" for its F1 0.765. Launched a JOINT reconstruction of both sides (582 images, `fuji_both_chain.sh`, survey `high/both`) so the sides share one frame for the count-level merge; ETA ≈ 06:00.
- **Joint reconstruction failed (03:58):** GLOMAP on all 582 images registers 291/582 — the two row sides share no features (a hedgerow under a hail net), so one frame for both sides does not come from SfM; FruitNeRF++ registered the sides by hand for the same reason. Consequence: on Fuji the cross-side merge needs an alignment better than the apple spacing (10 cm nearest-neighbour), and ours through their cloud is ~15 cm — so neither the per-apple score nor a merged count can be stated yet. Per-side counts stand (east 1,069, west 1,089, record rules). Next (not tonight): register each side to their cloud using colour or 2-D features instead of geometry alone, or evaluate per side against the GT apples visible from that side once the frame is good enough to decide visibility.
- **East→west through the global cloud (07:3x, Paul: "what is the current transform from east to west, can we register both to the global cloud and estimate it there"):** composing the two per-side registrations gives a 178.5° rotation (the sides face each other), scale 1.027, but east apples land a median 0.26 m from any west apple (1 % within 5 cm) — the two cloud registrations disagree. A direct east↔west ICP on the fruit gaussians, started from that composition, converges with a 9.4° rotation and 0.27 m shift away from it (the per-side cloud registrations were tilted: a canopy slab constrains rotation about the row poorly) and brings 61 % of east fruit gaussians within 5 cm of a west one (from 9 %); east apples within 5 / 10 / 15 cm of a west apple 27 / 47 / 57 %. **Merged count = east 1,069 + west 1,089 − shared: 1,873 at 5 cm, 1,653 at 10 cm (one apple spacing), 1,545 at 15 cm vs GT 1,455** (apple-cloud registration variant: 1,853 / 1,636 / 1,515). Next: register the now-consistent PAIR to the global cloud (both sides constrain the tilt) and retest the GT projection.
- **Joint pair → cloud (07:5x):** ICP of the joined east+west SfM points to their cloud moves 5° / 8 cm from the per-side solutions but leaves our apples a median 0.35–0.37 m from the GT (8 % within 10 cm) while east↔west consistency holds (48 % of east apples within 10 cm of a west apple). An unbounded multi-start fit of our 2,158 merged apples onto the cloud's apple-coloured points collapsed to scale → 0 (every apple on one red point: a degenerate optimum of a nearest-point objective); rerun with bounds (scale ± 6 %, rotation ≤ 12°, shift ≤ 0.6 m).
- **Fuji, closing state (08:0x):** bounded multi-start fit of the 2,158 merged apples onto the cloud's apple-coloured points (scale ± 6 %, rotation ≤ 12°, shift ≤ 0.6 m): best 17 / 44 % within 5 / 10 cm of a red point, yet 2 / 10 / 25 % within 5 / 10 / 15 cm of a GT centre; evaluator (record rules, 963 merged apples in the GT box): **15 cm precision 0.28 / recall 0.19 (F1 0.23); 30 cm 0.64 / 0.42 (F1 0.51)**. Five registration routes agree on one thing: our frame and theirs differ by more than a similarity (our east and west agree with each other at 5–10 cm). Usable Fuji outputs: per-side counts 1,069 / 1,089 and the merged count through the direct east↔west registration — **1,653 at a 10 cm merge radius (+13 % vs 1,455), 1,545 at 15 cm (+6 %)**; the per-apple score needs camera-level registration into their frame (not built). Published comparators are count-level (FruitLangGS 1,443) or manually registered (FruitNeRF++ F1 0.765, protocol unspecified).
- **Fuji, frame forensics (08:0x–08:3x; Paul: "do not close", "single image onto the point cloud", "reproject apple centres back"):** their truth is consistent with their own cloud (GT centre → nearest apple-coloured point median 3.1 cm; naive red clusters match the GT one-to-one at 10 cm with P 0.75 / R 0.74), and our side is consistent too: SfM points reproject at 0.32 px, our registry apples land on the masks of image_225 within one radius in 85 % of cases. Yet their GT reprojected into image_225 lands on a mask only 5 % (global alignment), 17 % (pose refined by image-space ICP on apples), 11–12 % (RANSAC P3P over 306 putative mask↔cluster pairs, ≤ 28 inliers) and 46 % at best in a global pose search (±2.5 m, ±20°) that leaves only 12 GT apples in frame; a control with our own apples through the same machinery gives 85 %. Low-order polynomial warps fitted to their red clusters (GT unused) change nothing (12 % within 10 cm), nor does a reflected alignment (mirrored fit: cloud inliers 48 %, apples 8 % within 10 cm — same as proper). Intrinsics are sound (fx/fy 0.9998, focal 3 % from EXIF). Open: whether the east/west image folders correspond to the faces I assumed (a face swap would fit every symptom: the slab fits either way, apples never match, our sides agree with each other) — testing every initialisation of the alignment with the camera side reported.
- **Face swap excluded (08:2x):** every proper initialisation (both row directions, cameras on either face at ±3.3–3.5 m across the row) converges to the same cloud fit (46–49 % inliers, 5.5 cm) and the same apple inconsistency (7–9 % of our apples within 10 cm of their apple clusters). Summary of the forensics: our images ↔ our apples consistent (85 % on masks), their cloud ↔ their GT consistent (3 cm; red clusters P/R 0.75 at 10 cm), their cloud ↔ our images inconsistent under any pose, handedness, face, similarity or low-order warp. The apple layout of their photogrammetric cloud is locally scrambled relative to the photographs by ~15–20 cm, and the GT boxes inherit it. Consequence: on Fuji, per-apple matching is only meaningful at a radius covering that scatter plus the registration (~0.3 m): registry of record merged P 0.64 / R 0.42 / F1 0.51 at 0.30 m (morning rules below); count-level 1,653 (+13 %) at a 10 cm merge.
- **Who has used Fuji-SfM and how (08:5x):** (1) Gené-Mola et al. 2020, CEA 169:105165 (the dataset's authors): Mask-RCNN on the 582 images, detections projected onto THEIR Agisoft Photoscan 1.4 cloud (same cloud the GT boxes were annotated in — no cross-frame registration), DBSCAN at 3 cm, nearest-to-camera group kept; 3-D TP = detection box with > 50 % overlap of a GT box; test set (trees 1–8): DR 0.991, R 0.906, P 0.857, FDR 0.037, multi-detection rate 0.106, **F1 0.881** (2-D F1 0.816). (2) Gené-Mola et al. 2020 Data in Brief (dataset paper); 2021 CEA apple-size estimation (PFuji-Size successor). (3) FruitNeRF 2024: F1 0.79 (SAM) / 0.78 (U-Net), "near the 0.88 of the original paper"; matching rule and registration not described. (4) FruitNeRF++ 2025: F1 0.765, COLMAP per side + manual registration of the sides, blamed for the shortfall. (5) CountingFruit / FruitLangGS 2025: count ratio 1,443 / 1,455. Same group's sibling sets: LFuji-air (LiDAR), PFuji-Size. The dataset's own protocol evaluates inside the provided cloud; every later method had to register into it, which is where we are stuck (15–20 cm).
- **Protocol decision (09:1x, Paul: "should we do the same, bundle adjustment at the downscale?"):** no — the GT's scatter comes from Agisoft's dense stereo at Medium quality (images downscaled 16×) scaled by 85-cm markers, a specific noise realisation no re-run of our camera solve reproduces. What reproduces the AUTHORS' protocol is lifting our 2-D detections onto THEIR cloud (`fuji_lift_eval.py`): per image, their cloud points projected through our camera (pose in their frame by the per-side bulk-structure similarity), the points inside each SAM3 mask DBSCAN-grouped at 3 cm, the group nearest the camera = the detection; boxes merged across images and sides at > 50 % overlap; TP = > 50 % overlap with a GT box (their metric; 2020: R 0.906 / P 0.857 / F1 0.881 with Mask-RCNN). Our detections then carry the cloud's errors exactly as the GT does — no apple-level registration.
- **Evaluating inside their cloud (09:2x–09:5x):** `fuji_lift_eval.py` (our SAM3 masks lifted onto their dense cloud through our cameras, DBSCAN 3 cm, nearest group, boxes merged at > 50 % overlap, TP = > 50 % overlap with a GT box — the authors' metric): 47,009 per-image detections merge into 9,759 boxes, recall 0.23 / precision 0.03 — the per-image lifts do not land on the same cloud apple across images because our poses in their frame carry the 15–20 cm registration error, so a mask's frustum catches different cloud points per image; the authors had exact poses from their own Agisoft solve. Localising images directly in their cloud (z-buffer render of their coloured cloud through our camera, SIFT photo↔render, PnP-RANSAC): full-res render covers 16 % of pixels, 115 ratio matches, 38 with 3-D, 0 inliers; quarter/sixth resolution: 31–33 % coverage, 5–6 matches, 0 inliers (`cloud_render_225*.png`: the rendered canopy does not line up with the photo and the net, post and wires are absent from their cloud). The dataset ships no poses; the authors' protocol is only reproducible with them.
- **Mutual-information registration to their coloured cloud (afternoon/evening; Paul: "this cloud has colour, project it to an image, there is mutual information"):** (1) point render + MI(intensity) + MI(redness), 6-DoF Powell, bounded ± 15° / ± 1.5 m (`fuji_mi_pose.py`): MI 0.050 → 0.103, camera moved 0.87 m / 12°, GT-on-mask probe 5 → 11 % within 1 radius — signal but thin (the point render covers 31 % of the pixels at quarter resolution). (2) Dense render: their cloud splatted as 2–5 cm isotropic gaussians through gsplat (`fuji_cloud_splat_render.py`, 48 % coverage at 2 cm; `fuji_mi_pose_splat.py` coarse-to-fine levels 8/4/2 with 24 wide starts): the unconstrained search finds a FALSE optimum — the camera turns to the sky where the cloud's net/wire points match the bright sky (overlay `mi_splat_225.png`), MI 0.77 with 0 GT apples in frame; constrained to ± 0.6 m per level it still drifts 1.7 m over the three levels to the same kind of view. Fix launched: MI computed only inside the canopy region of the start view (dilated) with a 0.85 coverage floor. The dataset's row is near-periodic (trees ~1 m apart) and the render/photo similarity is weak, so MI is multi-modal; whether it can reach apple-level accuracy is undecided.
- **Region-restricted splat-MI result (18:3x):** no escape any more (60–72 GT apples stay in frame) but no apple-level convergence either: level 8 → GT-on-mask probe 5 → 11 % within 1 radius (32 % within 2), level 4 → 7 / 29 %, level 2 → 5 / 37 %, camera 0.45–0.83 m from the start pose. Across every registration tried today (geometry ICP, image-based similarity, apple-cloud ICP, joint pair, bounded global search, reflections, both faces, PnP-RANSAC on apples, global pose search, point-render MI, dense splat-render MI with and without region restriction) the probe never exceeds ~14 % within one radius / ~45 % within two, while our own apples sit on our masks at 85 %. The cloud's apple layout is not the photographs' at the apple scale; the GT inherits that; only the authors' own poses (in that cloud's frame) reproduce their protocol.
- **Fuji registration solved — it was a face swap (19:xx–20:3x).** Paul: "I don't see the pole in this pointcloud is this the right region … keep the technical path". Chain of GT-free checks under `align_ground` (ground-profile pitch + z fix on top of `align_coarse`, then full-cloud ICP; apples near GT 18 → 25 % at 15 cm): (1) ray-cast of their cloud from our camera 225 puts the first surface at the same depth as our SfM points (ratio median 1.00, 84 % within 10 %) and the canopy/ground/net heights agree (`fuji_depth_ratio.py`, `fuji_vertical.py`), so distance and height were right; (2) the first-hit map (`firsthit_east_225.png`) showed the canopy silhouette MIRRORED left-right against the photo; (3) in the dataset's UTM frame the east cameras sat 3.2 m WEST of the row and the west cameras 3.0 m EAST (`fuji_flip_test.py`): every similarity ICP of a one-sided SfM cloud onto the two-faced row slab had converged to the mirrored face, and the slab + ground cannot tell the faces apart. Fix = 180° about the vertical axis through the row + the same full-cloud ICP → `align_flip.npz`: cloud inliers at 5 / 10 cm east 35 / 59 → **87 / 93 %**, west 26 / 50 → **94 / 96 %**; scales 0.915 / 0.859 (were 0.97 / 0.94); our apples within 5 / 10 / 15 cm of a GT apple east 1 / 11 / 25 → **33 / 70 / 76 %**, west 2 / 10 / 23 → **63 / 78 / 81 %** (the GT is a readout, nothing was fitted to it). Blur-free colour render of their cloud from camera 225 (`fuji_firsthit_rgb.py`, `firsthit_rgb_east_225_flip.png`) now shows the pole, the net clips, the tree tops and the apples where the photo has them — the pole was always in their cloud (column at along −3.97 m up to 4.7 m; the earlier gsplat render had 2 cm blobs and the wrong face). GT-on-mask probe (`fuji_gt_on_masks.py`, occlusion-tested against their cloud): east 225 11 / 33 → 15 / 66 % within 1 / 2 mask radii, east 150 23 / 76 %, west 225 5/5; median GT-to-mask-centre 27–36 px vs 18 px radius → the residual is ≈ 1.5 radii ≈ 6 cm (registration + annotation + our centroid). Yesterday's verdict ("their cloud is not the photographs' geometry") is withdrawn; the MI work, the warps, the reflection and the "every initialisation" tests all explored the mirrored basin.
- **Fuji per-apple scores (registry of record, `--crop 0`, `fuji_eval.py --align-file align_flip`, greedy one-to-one matching, GT region = GT bbox + 0.3 m):** east alone 890 apples in region — P / R / F1 at 10 cm **0.810 / 0.496 / 0.615**, 15 cm 0.884 / 0.541 / 0.671; west alone 940 — 10 cm **0.870 / 0.562 / 0.683**, 15 cm 0.914 / 0.590 / 0.717. Cross-side merge: the old rule (transitive union-find over every pair within 15 cm) collapsed 1,830 → 632 "apples" — the same chain disease as single linkage — replaced by greedy one-to-one east↔west pairs within the radius (`--merge one2one`, default): 491 pairs + 399 + 449 singles = **1,339 apples; 10 cm P 0.827 / R 0.761 / F1 0.792; 15 cm 0.864 / 0.795 / 0.828; 20 cm 0.891 / 0.820 / 0.854** (log `logs/fuji_eval_flip_one2one.log`). Per-tree recall at 15 cm 0.70–0.84 (Tree05 71/92 lowest, Tree02 88/105 highest). Literature on the same trees: Gené-Mola 2020 R 0.906 / P 0.857 / F1 0.881 (detections lifted onto their own cloud, > 50 % box overlap — no cross-reconstruction registration), FruitNeRF++ F1 0.765, FruitLangGS 1,443 / 1,455 (count only). Our matching radius is a centre distance, not their box overlap; at 5 cm the registration residual alone eats most matches, so the comparable statement is the 10–15 cm one with that caveat. Figures: `scratchpad/firsthit_rgb_east_225_flip.png`, `gt_on_masks_east_225_flip.png`, `fuji_region_map.png`. Scripts promoted to `automation/fruitnerf/`: `fuji_flip_test.py` (alignment of record), `fuji_region_check.py` (readers + map), `fuji_depth_ratio.py`, `fuji_firsthit_rgb.py`, `fuji_gt_on_masks.py`; `fuji_eval.py` gained `--merge`. Superseded on disk: `align_coarse/proper/refl/dense/image_based/apples/joint/robust/posts/ground.npz`, `mi_*`, `cloud_splat_225*.png`.
- **Fuji merged count at a 10 cm merge radius (20:4x, Paul: "what's the current count"):** `--radius 0.10` → 385 pairs + 505 + 555 singles = **1,445 apples** in the GT region (vs 1,455 GT); P 0.796 / R 0.790 / F1 0.793 at 10 cm — same F1 as the 15 cm merge (1,339; 0.827 / 0.761 / 0.792): the merge radius trades cross-side pairs for singles. Count of record = 1,445 (merge radius = one apple diameter, stated before the run).
- **Per-tree counts on Fuji through the hierarchy's tree anchors (Paul 21:0x: "let's do the per tree counts") — rules fixed before the run:** trunk gaussians = SAM3 'tree trunk' census through each side's splat (share ≥ 0.5, mass ≥ 1, every 2nd view; `fuji_trunk_census.py`), to the dataset frame through `align_flip`; our ground = p2 of our splat means' height within the row; trunk band 0.3–1.5 m above it; 1-D KDE along the row (one vote per gaussian, bandwidth 0.10 m), peaks ≥ 10 % of the maximum with 0.5 m non-max suppression; posts (hail-net poles) = peaks whose trunk gaussians reach > 3.0 m, dropped; both sides' peaks merged within 0.3 m; every merged apple (10 cm one-to-one east↔west, GT region, count of record 1,445) → nearest trunk along the row. Scoring: GT trees ↔ trunks by nearest median position (table only); our count per trunk vs the GT count per tree; on the apples matched to a GT apple at 10 cm, the share whose trunk is that apple's GT tree; per-tree P/R/F1 under our labels. GT tree medians (along the row, their frame): −4.63 −3.72 −2.76 −1.76 −0.94 −0.13 +0.72 +1.70 +2.84 +3.74 +4.72 (≈ 0.95 m spacing, p2–p98 extents barely overlapping), so the GT partition is an along-row interval partition — the trunk-Voronoi rule is a fair stand-in, not a fit. Chain `logs/fuji_trunk_sam3.sh` → `logs/fuji_trees.log` (SAM3 ≈ 5 min per side, census, scoring `fuji_tree_counts.py`).
- **Post rule amended before the real census ran (21:1x):** a code-path test of `fuji_tree_counts.py` on a throwaway census (2 % random splat means, numbers meaningless, file removed) showed the "95th-percentile height > 3 m" post rule flags every column once non-trunk gaussians are present; replaced by a physical one: a column is a hail-net pole if ≥ 30 trunk-labelled gaussians lie above 4.5 m (above the canopy top), else a trunk. Side note from the same test: the splat's own density in the 0.3–1.5 m band already peaks within 0.1–0.3 m of 9 of the 11 GT tree medians.
- **Per-tree counts on Fuji — result (21:0x–21:2x, chain `logs/fuji_trunk_sam3.sh`, log `logs/fuji_trees.log`):** SAM3 'tree trunk' 1,742 / 1,509 masks (≈ 6 per frame, 1 min per side), trunk census 111,568 / 115,816 trunk gaussians (41 / 29 s), 46,942 / 45,464 in the 0.3–1.5 m band. Peaks: east 9, west 11; merged 12 (+ one column flagged as a pole at +4.54 on both sides). **Record rule (pre-registered, poles dropped): 10 of the 11 GT trees get a trunk within 0.25 m of the GT median** (−4.72/−4.63, −3.80/−3.72, −2.83/−2.76, −2.01/−1.76, −1.13/−0.94, −0.24/−0.13, +0.62/+0.72, +1.56/+1.70, +2.76/+2.84, +3.76/+3.74); Tree11 (+4.72) has none because its trunk column is also the hail-net pole's (≥ 30 trunk-labelled gaussians above 4.5 m) and the pole rule removed it, so Tree10 absorbs Tree11 (308 vs 128, +141 %) and Tree11 counts 0; the other nine trees are within −19…+13 % (median |error| 12 %); apples matched to a GT apple whose trunk is the GT tree 907 / 1,150 (78.9 %); mean per-tree F1 0.619. **Post-hoc variant `--keep-posts` (a pole column in the tree line still marks a tree position; chosen after seeing the Tree11 failure, so GT-informed in timing, physical in form): all 11 trunks within 0.25 m of the GT medians, per-tree counts 119 / 114 / 117 / 120 / 104 / 120 / 122 / 154 / 149 / 126 / 182 vs GT 147 / 105 / 135 / 137 / 92 / 133 / 122 / 151 / 143 / 128 / 162 (−19…+13 %, median |error| 10 %, max 19 %), right-tree assignment 1,018 / 1,150 (88.5 %), per-tree P 0.61–0.85 / R 0.59–0.79 / F1 0.63–0.76 (mean 0.707).** Totals 1,427 of the 1,445 (18 apples lie beyond the end trunks' Voronoi cells). The 11.5 % wrong-tree assignments are boundary apples: GT trees interleave by up to 0.3 m (Tree04/05 medians 0.25 and 0.19 m from our trunks), and a branch's apples can hang over the neighbour's trunk cell — the along-row Voronoi is the stand-in for branch attachment. Outputs `tree_counts_flip_{record,keepposts}.{json,png}` under `/home/paperspace/data/public/fuji_sfm/`. For the paper: the hierarchy's tree level (trunk anchors from SAM3 'tree trunk' through the splat, the same mask→splat census as the fruit) places apples in trees at 88.5 % and reproduces the 11 per-tree counts within ±19 %, no per-tree ground truth used in any rule; report the pole exception.
- **What the "pole" at +4.54 m actually is (21:4x, `fuji_pole_fig.py`, figure `pole_column_4.54.png`):** not a hail-net pole. The photos that see the column best (east image_260/254, west image_269/259, SAM3 trunk masks overlaid) show Tree 11's thick trunk running from the ground up through the canopy, with a thin graduated stake beside it on the east side; the column's trunk-labelled gaussians reach 4.74 / 4.76 m above our ground (95 / 117 gaussians above 4.5 m) while the neighbouring trunks stop at 2–2.7 m. The real poles (e.g. the one in image_225, 7.1 m in our splat) never formed a trunk peak inside the GT region, so the pre-registered pole rule removed nothing but this tall trunk; the `--keep-posts` variant is therefore just "no pole rule". Correction to the 21:2x entry: Tree 11 was lost to the rule's 4.5 m threshold on a tall trunk, not to a pole at the trunk.

- **Fuji per-apple rule ladder (22:19, `logs/fuji_rule_ablation.sh` → `fuji_rule_ablation.log`; each variant both sides, `fuji_eval --align-file align_flip --radius 0.10`, merged one-to-one at 10 cm, GT region; P / R / F1 at 10 cm):** V0 single linkage (link ≥ 2 views, co > conflicts, no merge check = the 10-15 morning rules) 1,118 apples, **0.760 / 0.584 / 0.661**; V1 strict links alone (≥ 8 co-views, co/(co+conf) ≥ 0.95, no merge) 48,955 fragments, 0.029 / 0.987 / 0.057; V2 + transitive-safe evidence merge 2,134, **0.600 / 0.880 / 0.713**; V3 + dominance 1,445, **0.796 / 0.790 / 0.793**; V4 + windfall layer out (= record) identical to V3 — no ground-layer apple lies inside the GT region (GT starts 0.36 m above ground, the region crop already excludes the ground). Reading: strict links are only the over-segmentation step; the evidence merge is what makes apples (recall 0.58 → 0.88 against single linkage, with 854 extras = pieces and leaf-fringe components); dominance removes 689 of those extras for 130 lost matches (precision 0.60 → 0.80). Each step is GT-blind in form and was fixed before Fuji (commit 98fc648); this ladder is their per-apple effect, measured once. Stage-2 cost on 12 M-gaussian splats: 2 min (strict), 5.6–9 min (evidence merge) per side.

## 2026-10-17

We have everything?"):** `aru_sil_core/src/docs/paper/main.tex` rewritten as the RA-L letter (IEEEtran journal class; the 08-14 conference draft's method story — naive embedder failures, five fixes, walk — is replaced by the production method: supervision compiler → graph-only embedder (contrastive 2.0 / cosine-recon 2.0 / recon 1.0 / alignment 1.0, τ 0.2, frozen curvature, no level norms, 1,500 epochs) → census-init (Eq. 2 = the rasteriser-gradient W) → containment + void check + argmax (Eq. 3) → fruit registry + trunk anchors). So argmax is our evaluation protocol only; the paper now states the mechanism contrast explicitly (query subsection + related work: containment = max over own point and non-extrapolated ancestors, void check, vs softmax blend along the geodesic + fixed threshold), and the hero panels are titled by the mechanism ("containment decode of the row / tree queries") with the argmax named in the caption as the readout. The August draft's OpenHype bib entry (Kim/Park/Han, ECCV 2024) was wrong and is replaced by the real one.
